# BoxForged — Backlog

Technical debt and deferred work discovered during pre-production technical review (2026-08-19).

**Nothing here is authorized.** Items are recorded rather than silently resolved, per the studio change-discipline rule. Priority is impact-ordered, not schedule-ordered.

Severity: **P1** blocks or endangers the two team-built reference worlds · **P2** costs real time or quality soon · **P3** worth doing, not urgent.

## P1 — Endangers the reference worlds

### B1. Texture import policy pass
**Impact:** thermal throttling mid-run on target hardware; likely the project's dominant performance risk.

2.6 GB of source textures across 353 files; individual BaseColor maps run 27–31 MB. **Essentially every texture imports at `maxTextureSize: 2048` with only a `DefaultTexturePlatform` entry — no Android or iPhone override, `textureFormat: -1` (Auto).** Only 34 are capped at 1024, 15 at 512.

Estimated 100–150 MB of texture memory for a single room. The failure mode is not a crash but gradual slowdown ~10 minutes into a 10–15 minute run — the exact scenario a livestream demo hits.

**Proposed:** `AssetPostprocessor` enforcing per-category caps (characters/bosses 1024, weapons 512, env props 512, UI 256) with explicit ASTC overrides per platform. Should precede any further asset import.
**Note:** derived from file inspection, **not measured on device**. Profile before and after.

**First on-device measurement, 2026-08-27 (see `docs/PERFORMANCE_PROFILING.md` session 2026-08-27):** one mid-run frame during a full `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` playthrough showed **41.2 MB across 52 used textures** — well under the 150 MB budget, and far below this item's 100–150 MB file-inspection estimate. Not yet the full §4.5 detailed-sample procedure (per-zone `Take Sample` + `Texture2D` tree total) and not yet checked under thermal load at minute 12+, so this is encouraging but not a close-out of this item. Device model not yet recorded (Rule 2 — pass may be a lower bound if the test device is newer than the 3–4-year-old target class).

### B2. Telegraph channel — blocking dependency of the camera change
**Implemented 2026-08-19 — but see B32, a critical bug found in review that means it currently renders nothing on a real device.**

See [ADR-0003](adr/0003-attack-telegraph-channel.md). Approving the low camera *without* this is the one combination that should not ship; the fallback is raising the camera back toward current pitch. Design itself (pooling, shape-coded parryability, hooks into the `WindUp` seam) was verified clean by performance review — the only defect is the build-stripping issue in B32.

Includes fixing the standing accessibility defect: parryable vs un-parryable is encoded **entirely in hue** (red DrumSlam vs yellow Haymaker) — resolved via shape-coding, pending B32's fix to actually be visible.

### B3. `RoomDataSO` extraction before the scene rebuild — **CLOSED 2026-08-19, not needed**
**Superseded by the D4 decision to discard existing room data.** The existing rooms were built while figuring out process, not as content worth preserving — so there is nothing to extract. `RoomDataSO` as an *architecture pattern* (data-driven room authoring, so a future contributor can build a room as a data asset rather than wiring scene-local references) may still be worth building when World 1/2 rooms are actually designed — but that's a fresh design task at that point, not a transcription of the current scenes.

### B4. Consolidate the two ability systems — **scope corrected 2026-08-19, much smaller than believed, but blocked on real design work**

**The original framing of this item was wrong in a load-bearing way.** V3 and V4 are not two competing systems needing a large migration — they're stacked layers, and V4 *depends on* V3. `WeaponObjectSO` (V4) has a field, `baseEquippedData`, pointing at a `WeaponData` (V3) asset — this is the live per-character grip/visual-variant resolution path (`WeaponInventory.ResolveEquipData` → `WeaponCycler.ResolveWeapon` → `WeaponHolder.EquipWeapon`). **The 81 `WeaponData` assets are not migration debt — they are V4's own equipped-data layer and are not going anywhere.**

**Real scope, verified directly against the assets:** of 81 `WeaponData` assets, 69 have no ability at all and need nothing; the other 12 are just character-variant copies of 3 weapon families. **Only 3 `WeaponAbilityData` assets actually need retiring: Shuriken, SixShooter, DynamiteBundle** (plus their 2 already-dead sibling classes, `LassoAbilityData`/`QuickdrawBladeAbilityData` — **deleted 2026-08-19**, confirmed dead via GUID sweep, superseded by shipping V4 behaviours).

**Why it's still not a quick fix:** V4's `AbilitySO`/`AbilityExecutor` architecturally cannot express what those 3 weapons need — an attack-button trigger (V4 only has OnHit/OnSpecial/OnDodge/OnBlock/Passive, no equivalent of V3's `FiresOnAttackButton`), per-equip ammo/reload state (Six Shooter's 6-shot/1.2s-reload/counter-window "Fan the Hammer" behavior — `AbilitySO` has one flat `cooldown` float), counter-window context (`AbilityExecutionContext` doesn't carry it, `AbilityActivationContext` does), and a HUD readiness surface (`CombatController.SpecialAbilityProgress` reads a per-ability `ProgressFraction` that V4 doesn't supply). This is genuine design/architecture work, not asset conversion — extending `AbilitySO`, `AbilityExecutor`, and `AbilityExecutionContext`, and doing it without reproducing V3's existing SO-instance-state smell (ammo count living on the shared asset instead of a per-equip component).

**Recommendation: own dedicated task after Sprint 0 commits, ~2-3 days** (half a day design/ADR for the attack-trigger + ammo-state contract, ~1 day porting the 3 behaviours, ~1 day rewiring + regression testing ranged weapons). Not bundled into Sprint 0.

Gated on D3 (decided: keep V4, migrate what's left — now correctly scoped to 3 assets, not 81).

### B27. Camera fails its own lateral-width requirement — worse than first thought, affects common phones too
**Surfaced 2026-08-19, expanded 2026-08-19 by performance review — real acceptance-criteria gap, needs an owner decision, not just an engineering fix.** ADR-0001's acceptance table requires lateral width ≥16m at Kid's depth on every supported aspect ratio (4:3 to 21:9). Initial finding: fails at 4:3 (tablet, ≈10.3m). **Performance review recalculated per-aspect and found the arena itself (`_arenaBoundaryRadius = 8.5`, 17m diameter) overflows the visible frame on far more than just tablets:**

| Aspect | Visible width at player depth | 17m arena |
|---|---|---|
| 20:9 (modern phone) | ~17.2m | just fits |
| **16:9 (common 3–4-year-old Android — the actual target hardware)** | **~13.8m** | **~3m overflows off-screen** |
| 4:3 (iPad) | ~10.3m | ~7m overflows |

The 16.8m figure in the ADR only holds at roughly 20:9. **This means the arena edges are off-screen on the specific hardware class the mobile performance budget already targets** (3–4-year-old devices, which skew 16:9 rather than the newer 20:9 aspect), not just on tablets. This is arithmetic from the prefab values, not yet measured on an actual device — verify in a build on a 16:9 device before treating it as certain, but the math is straightforward enough to trust as a strong signal.

**FIXED 2026-08-19.** New component `Core/AspectAdaptiveCameraFraming.cs` on `pfb_CM_FollowCam.prefab`: scales `FollowOffset` along its existing direction (pitch and vertical FOV never touched) to guarantee ≥16m lateral width at every aspect ratio, only pulling back further than the authored base distance when narrower aspects would otherwise fall short — 20:9/21:9 are unchanged. Verified by invoking the actual compiled method against all four target aspects:

| Aspect | F (≥12) | R (≥4, floor 3.3) | W (≥16) | Top ray (≥10°) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4:3 | 23.74m | 6.50m | 16.00m | 13.5° |
| 16:9 | 17.81m | 4.88m | 16.00m | 13.5° |
| 20:9 | 15.34m | 4.20m | 17.23m | 13.5° |
| 21:9 | 15.34m | 4.20m | 18.09m | 13.5° |

All four floors clear at every aspect. Top-ray angle is exactly 13.5° regardless of distance (it only depends on pitch and FOV, both fixed), so this fix structurally cannot bring the horizon into frame. **One manual check still recommended:** confirm visually in a normal Editor session (not the headless MCP session used to build this) that switching the Game View aspect preset during Play Mode visibly changes `FollowOffset` in the Inspector — the automated check exercised the real method directly but couldn't do a literal visual resize due to the session's non-standard Game View size.

### B32. CRITICAL — the telegraph shader will be stripped from the real build; the whole system is currently invisible on-device — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: created `Assets/_Project/Materials/mat_TelegraphOverlay.mat` (using `BoxForged/TelegraphOverlayUnlit`), added a `[SerializeField] private Material _overlaySourceMaterial` to `AttackTelegraphService` referencing it, and removed the `Shader.Find` runtime lookup entirely from `AttackTelegraphIndicator`. Also collapsed the 8 per-instance runtime materials down to 2 shared ones (parryable/unparryable), owned and destroyed by the service. **Critically, the serialized-field fix alone does nothing unless something in the build actually assigns it** — the service was purely self-bootstrapping (never placed in any scene), so a new `pfb_AttackTelegraphService.prefab` (with the material wired) was created and placed in all 6 scenes that already carry `pfb_AudioManager` (`ForgeLoop_Test`, `CulDeSac_Room1`, `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley`, `CulDeSac_SaloonFront`, `CulDeSac_MailboxRow`, `CulDeSac_BossArena`), mirroring that existing precedent. Verified via a Play Mode smoke test in `ForgeLoop_Test` — zero console errors, service resolves the material without the "not assigned" fallback error path firing.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review.** `Assets/_Project/Shaders/TelegraphOverlayUnlit.shader` is referenced by zero materials, prefabs, or scenes, and is not in `ProjectSettings/GraphicsSettings.asset`'s Always Included Shaders. Unity only includes a shader in a build if something references it that way — a runtime `Shader.Find("BoxForged/TelegraphOverlayUnlit")` string lookup (what `AttackTelegraphService` currently does) does not count. On a real Android/iOS build, `Shader.Find` returns null, an error logs, and every telegraph indicator silently renders nothing. **This works perfectly in the Editor and fails silently in a build — the worst possible failure shape for a livestream demo.** Compounded by B29 (no audio clips authored yet either) — on a real build today, there is currently no attack telegraph at all, visual or audio.

**Fix:** create a material asset (`mat_TelegraphOverlay.mat`) using this shader, and have `AttackTelegraphService` reference it via a serialized field rather than a runtime string lookup. This also lets the 8 per-instance runtime materials collapse to 2 shared ones. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass.

### B33. `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance` will hang Play Mode entry with a flood of false-positive errors — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: `_cameraClearanceMask` now defaults to the Building layer only (matching `CameraOcclusion._wallMask`) instead of `~0`; the field was moved outside the `#if UNITY_EDITOR` guard (only `ValidateCameraClearance`'s logic stays editor-only) to avoid a prefab/scene serialization mismatch across build configurations; and per-vertex `Debug.LogError` calls (up to ~40, each with a stack trace) were replaced with a single aggregated `Debug.LogWarning` per validation run (e.g. "4 of 16 NavMesh vertices have < 6.0m clear above"). Verified via a Play Mode smoke test in `ForgeLoop_Test`: bake completed instantly (16 verts) and produced exactly one warning line, correctly scoped to real geometry rather than flagging props/pickups/enemies. The deeper "is 8m clearance achievable in an 8.5m arena" question is unchanged and remains tracked in B27 — this fix only makes the validator report sanely.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review.** Confirmed genuinely editor-only (correctly wrapped in `#if UNITY_EDITOR`, cannot run in a player build) — but as written it: (1) uses an unrestricted `~0` layer mask, so it flags props, pickups, enemies, and workbenches as "camera clearance violations," not just building geometry; (2) runs two raycasts per NavMesh triangulation vertex, synchronously, in one frame — thousands to tens of thousands of raycasts on a room-sized mesh; (3) can emit up to ~40 `Debug.LogError` calls, each capturing a stack trace. Expected result: a multi-second hang entering Play Mode and a console flooded with mostly-false-positive errors, which trains everyone to ignore the check — defeating its purpose.

**Fix:** restrict the mask to the actual building/geometry layer, move the check to an on-demand menu item rather than running on every Play Mode entry, and stride/dedupe the vertex list. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass.

### B42. Telegraph indicators are never explicitly hidden — ghost warnings linger over dead/stunned enemies
**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by code review.** `AttackTelegraphService.Show()` returns a handle specifically so callers can later call `Hide()` — but no call site across any of the 8 modified AI scripts actually captures that handle or calls `Hide()`. An indicator only clears itself on natural duration expiry or if its target GameObject is destroyed. **Failure scenario:** the player interrupts an enemy's wind-up by killing it (many enemies play a death animation before actually destroying the GameObject) or staggering it — the "attack incoming" icon keeps floating over the corpse/stunned enemy for the rest of the original wind-up duration. Not fixed in the Sprint 0 fix pass (deferred per reviewer's own recommendation, since it's a visual polish issue, not a safety/correctness one) — tracked here so it isn't forgotten. Fix suggestion: a small shared per-enemy telegraph helper (`Raise`/`Clear`) called from each AI's existing stagger/death state-entry methods, which would also deduplicate the identical one-line `Show()` call currently repeated across all 8 scripts.

### B43. Camera occlusion fade loses partial-occlusion detection and hysteresis with the single-raycast retune
**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by code review.** The single torso-height raycast (B5/B35's fix) is a large net performance win, but trades away two things the old 8-corner AABB test had: (1) no dead-band, so strafing along a wall edge can cause the wall to flicker between faded and solid frame-to-frame; (2) no partial-occlusion detection — a pillar or low wall covering everything except the exact torso sample point won't trigger a fade at all, where the old test would have caught it. Not fixed in the Sprint 0 fix pass (deferred per reviewer's recommendation) — worth a playtest pass at the new camera angle specifically. Candidate fix if it proves to be a real problem: add a short fade-hold after the ray clears, or sample a small fan of 3 rays instead of 1.

### B34. `ForgePresenter` can permanently zero the equipped weapon's visible scale — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: added a `CancelActiveSequence()` cleanup path (called from both `Dispatch()`, when a second forge/upgrade interrupts an in-flight one, and `OnDisable()`) that restores the tracked weapon transform's `localScale`/`localRotation` to its correct end-state and explicitly stops + zeroes the colour-bloom coroutine's volume weight, rather than relying on the coroutine reaching its own happy-path end. The bloom coroutine is now tracked in a field (`_colorBloomSequence`) instead of fire-and-forget.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review — correctness bug, not a performance one.** The transformation sequence sets the weapon's scale to zero at the start and only restores it at the natural end of the sequence. If the sequence is interrupted (forging twice in quick succession, or the player object being disabled mid-sequence), nothing restores the scale — the equipped weapon becomes permanently invisible until the player re-equips something. A related fire-and-forget color-bloom coroutine has the same problem: two overlapping forges can leave the imagination-restore visual effect stuck on.

**Fix:** restore scale/rotation in a guaranteed cleanup path (not just the happy-path end of the sequence), and track the bloom coroutine so it can be stopped alongside the rest of the sequence. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass.

## P2 — Costs real time or quality soon

### B5. Resolve the two occlusion systems — **DONE 2026-08-19**
Kept `CameraOcclusion.cs` (deleted `BuildingOcclusionFader.cs` — see B28, it had a real bug beyond being redundant). Retuned to a single torso-height raycast. **New finding from review:** the retuned method now calls `GetComponentInChildren<Renderer>()` on every ray hit, every frame, unfiltered — see B35.

### B6. `EnemyHealthBar` billboard and scale — **DONE 2026-08-19, verified no regression**
Billboard refresh now also triggers on enemy movement, not just camera movement. Performance review confirmed the added cost is negligible (~0.01ms at 20 enemies) and that the guard it modifies was already firing almost every frame anyway, since the camera follows the player continuously in combat — net cost delta ≈ zero. Bar dimensions scaled down by the camera-distance ratio as a documented estimate, not yet visually re-checked in-engine. Two `new Material` instances per enemy at runtime confirmed unchanged, cleanup confirmed still correct — this remains tracked debt (draw-call budget, not a regression from this change).

### B47. `AspectAdaptiveCameraFraming.Apply()` deserves a permanent EditMode test once test infrastructure exists
Validated 2026-08-19 via a one-off reflection-based check against the four target aspect ratios (see B27) rather than a real test, since no EditMode/PlayMode test assembly exists in the project yet (see B7). Once B7's minimum test boundary exists, this is a clean, pure-math candidate to cover permanently — asserting F/R/W/top-ray against the same four aspect ratios.

### B7. Minimum test infrastructure
Zero tests, zero test assemblies; exactly one `.asmdef` in the project (`{"name": "StatSystem"}`). `com.unity.test-framework` installed and unused. **The Definition of Done clause "affected tests pass" is currently unsatisfiable.**

Proposed minimum: one assembly boundary around pure rules (forge, durability, inventory, stat math) + EditMode tests, starting with `ForgeController.TryForge` (its check-then-spend ordering is exactly the invariant a regression would silently break). Not a whole-project asmdef refactor.

**Addition from B69's code review (2026-08-24):** nothing asserts that every `RandomRoomPool`/`ZoneStartScene` name in `GameManager.cs` is present and enabled in `EditorBuildSettings`, or that a `RoomDataSO` spawn-point's `enemyPrefab` actually carries `EnemyStats`. A ~20-line EditMode validator here would have caught the pre-existing `CulDeSac_Room1` Build Settings GUID bug B69 stumbled into for free, and would catch the next room-registration mistake (Saloon Front, Mailbox Row) before it ships. Worth building once this item's assembly boundary exists.

### B8. Arena radius vs camera framing — **CORRECTION 2026-08-19: NOT actually done, despite being reported as done — FIXED 2026-08-19 (for real this time)**
C# default changed 18f → 8.5f, but **code review found the value on `pfb_player.prefab` (line 140) is still the old serialized `18`** — Unity does not retroactively apply a new script default to a component instance that already has its own saved value on a prefab. The prefab itself needs the value changed directly, not just the C# initializer. Also superseded in target by B27 (8.5 isn't even enough at real target-hardware aspect ratios) — but right now, on disk, the arena is still 18. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass.

Fixed via Unity MCP `manage_prefabs.modify_contents` directly on `pfb_player.prefab`'s `Boxhead.Player.PlayerController` component, and verified by reading the serialized value back from disk afterward (`_arenaBoundaryRadius: 8.5`). B27's separate concern (whether 8.5 is even enough at target aspect ratios) is untouched — that's an owner decision, not this fix's scope.

### B9. Hardcoded FOV duplicate — **CORRECTION 2026-08-19: NOT actually done, despite being reported as done — FIXED 2026-08-19 (for real this time)**
Same class of bug as B8. `SpinCycleAI._normalCameraFoV` C# default changed to `45f` with a comment claiming it now matches the rig and fixes the boss-intro→gameplay handoff pop — but **`pfb_enemy_spincycle.prefab` (line 1053) still has the old serialized value `40`.** The comment asserting the fix is now actively misleading. The pop this was meant to fix still happens. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass.

Fixed via Unity MCP `manage_prefabs.modify_contents` directly on `pfb_enemy_spincycle.prefab`'s `Boxhead.Enemy.SpinCycleAI` component, and verified by reading the serialized value back from disk afterward (`_normalCameraFoV: 45`).

### B39. Same stale-prefab-serialization bug affects the EnemyHealthBar retune — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
`EnemyHealthBar._barWidth`/`_barHeight` C# defaults were shrunk (0.8/0.17) to compensate for the closer camera, but all 6 enemy prefabs (`pfb_enemy_milepost_marshal`, `pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller`, `pfb_enemy_skeptic_grunt`, `pfb_enemy_gnome_grunt`, `pfb_enemy_spincycle`, `pfb_enemy_sprinkler_sentinel`) still carry the old serialized `1.4`/`0.3`. Health bars remain oversized at the new camera distance until the prefabs themselves are edited. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass, alongside B8/B9 — same root cause, same fix pattern (edit the prefab's serialized value directly, not just the C# default).

Fixed via Unity MCP `manage_prefabs.modify_contents` on all 6 prefabs' `Boxhead.Enemy.EnemyHealthBar` components, verified by reading each serialized value back from disk afterward (`_barWidth: 0.8`, `_barHeight: 0.17` on every one).

### B40. `ForgePanel.Close()` unpauses the game underneath the first-forge cutscene — the most-demoed moment in the game — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: `ForgePanel.Forge()`/`Upgrade()` now route success through a new `HandleForgeOrUpgradeSuccess()`, which checks `CutscenePlayer.Instance.IsPlaying` immediately after `TryForge()`/`TryUpgrade()` return (reliable because `CutscenePlayer.Play()` sets `IsPlaying = true` synchronously before its coroutine even starts). If a cutscene just started, the panel hides its own UI but deliberately leaves `Time.timeScale`/`AudioListener.pause` untouched, and starts `WaitForCutsceneThenClose()` — a coroutine that polls with a bare `yield return null` (so it keeps advancing even at `timeScale = 0`) until the cutscene ends, then performs the normal `Close()`. Traced through both the first-forge case (cutscene fires → world stays frozen behind it → resumes correctly on cutscene end) and the normal subsequent-forge case (no cutscene → `Close()` fires immediately, unchanged behaviour). `Close()` itself now also cancels any pending wait coroutine, so whichever path reaches it, state stays consistent.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by code review — severe, podcast-relevant.** The very first successful forge in a save fires a cutscene (`CutscenePlayer`) that disables player input but does NOT touch `Time.timeScale`. Previously the forge panel stayed open (keeping `timeScale = 0`) until the player closed it, so the frozen panel kept the world frozen behind the cutscene too. Now `ForgePanel.Close()` runs immediately after a successful forge and sets `timeScale = 1`. **Failure scenario: player forges their first weapon ever — a full-screen video covers the screen, their controls are disabled, and the game world runs at full speed behind it. Any enemy in the room can freely attack them while they can't see or move.** This is specifically the first-forge moment — one of the most likely moments to be shown live on the podcast. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass as a must-fix, not a backlog item.

### B41. Legacy V3 `Inventory` path silently resets forged weapon tier to Standard — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: `WeaponHolder` now caches a sibling `WeaponInventory` reference and, in `EquipWeapon(WeaponData, WeaponTier)`, reasserts `tier` from `WeaponInventory.ActiveWeapon.Tier` whenever that's non-null — before the passed-in `tier` is used for anything. Confirmed via code trace that `WeaponPickup` (the only live world-pickup path) exclusively calls into `WeaponInventory.AddToMaterialBag`, never the V3 `Inventory`, so there is no live path where a genuinely different, non-forge-tracked weapon needs to be equipped at Standard tier while a forged weapon is also active — meaning the reassertion is safe everywhere it fires. This resolves the `BossRoomWeaponSpawner.ClearPlayerWeapons()` → `Inventory.Drop()` path directly, and also fixes the same regression via `WeaponCycler.Start()`'s unconditional `Inventory.SetEquipped()` call (which runs on every scene load, including boss rooms, and was an equally live source of the same bug). WeaponInventory's own calls (which already pass the correct tier) are unaffected — the reassignment is a no-op on that path.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by code review.** `Boxhead.Systems.Inventory` (the older V3 system, still on the player prefab alongside V4's `WeaponInventory`) calls an equip overload that resolves to `WeaponTier.Standard` regardless of the weapon's actual tier. Live call sites include `BossRoomWeaponSpawner`, which calls this **on every boss-room entry** — meaning a player carrying a forged Legendary weapon into a boss fight has their tier silently reset the moment they arrive. This directly undermines the tier-glow feature this same sprint just built (B24/B25): the two systems disagree about the weapon's tier, and the visual glow would show the wrong tier or none at all. Queued for the Sprint 0 fix pass — the fix is to have `WeaponHolder` reassert tier from `WeaponInventory` (the source of truth) whenever it re-attaches a weapon, rather than trusting whichever system called last.

### B35. `CameraOcclusion` calls `GetComponentInChildren<Renderer>()` every frame, per hit, unfiltered — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed as a bundled low-risk item in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: added a `Dictionary<Collider, Renderer> _rendererCache`, resolved once per collider (including caching a `null` result for colliders with no Renderer) via a new `ResolveRenderer()` helper, cleared in `OnDestroy()` alongside the existing per-instance material cleanup.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review.** The occlusion-system retune (B5) is a large net win overall (one raycast replacing up to 256 per-frame matrix projections), but the `Renderer` lookup that now runs on every ray hit, every frame, is unfiltered — in the old code it sat behind a viewport-rect rejection test that made it rare; now nothing filters it. This directly violates the project's own "avoid per-frame `GetComponent`" rule. **Fix:** cache the `Collider → Renderer` mapping in a dictionary once, since it never changes at runtime. Small, low-risk fix.

### B10. Boss-intro system consolidation
Two unrelated implementations: `CutscenePlayer` (610-line video player, used by SpinCycle) and `PermitPulperBossIntro` (259-line in-engine cinematic that disables the `CinemachineBrain`, drives `Camera.main` directly, and fires a Cinemachine impulse **via reflection**, with no skip).

Under boss-intros-only scope, the reusable asset is the in-engine one — currently the *less* general of the two and hardcoded to one boss. Cutting scope deletes `CutscenePlayer`'s *callers*, leaving a large video player serving one call site. Gated on D2.

Note this does **not** fix `GameManager.cs:34` `ZoneStartScene[1] = "TownSquare_Room1"` being unreachable — that's still true, because World 2 doesn't exist yet, not because of a stale Build Settings entry. That remains open, tracked separately, no ETA (World 2 is Phase 3, not yet started). — **FIXED 2026-08-31** (ADR-0005 §7 scene scaffold): `Backyard_Dojo.unity` created, registered in Build Settings, and `ZoneStartScene[1]` corrected to point at it (`ZoneIndexByScene["Backyard_Dojo"] = 1` added alongside). Zone 1 is reachable now; the scene has no authored geometry/encounters yet (game-designer/puzzle-level-designer pass, next).

## P1 — Endangers the reference worlds (continued — found during D3 investigation, pre-existing, not caused by Sprint 0)

### B44. Live bug: a null-reference exception soft-locks combat on specific Epic/Legendary weapons
**Surfaced 2026-08-19 while scoping B4/D3 — pre-existing bug, not introduced by Sprint 0.** With a fighting style active (always true from run start) and an equipped Epic/Legendary weapon whose ability trigger is `OnSpecial` but has no V3 fallback ability, pressing Special throws a null-reference exception mid-coroutine, which leaves `CombatController.State` stuck at `SpecialAttacking` — **the player cannot attack or use Special again until they dodge.** Reproduces with Bo Staff Epic, Pressure Cannon Epic/Legendary, and Magic Wand Legendary. Root cause: `CombatController.cs:786` falls through to calling `_currentAbility.Activate(...)` when `_currentAbility` is null, because the V3/V4 negotiation logic assumes a V3 ability exists as a fallback whenever the style special path isn't taken. Not fixed — the correct behavior (should the style special fire instead? should V4 alone handle it?) is a design decision, not a null-guard, and this bug structurally disappears once B4's ability-system work lands. Worth knowing this exists on specific weapons right now, independent of when B4 gets scheduled.

### B45. Live bug: Shurikens Legendary double-fires its special (V3 and V4 both trigger)
**Surfaced 2026-08-19, same investigation as B44 — pre-existing.** Shuriken is the one weapon that still has a real V3 ability, so instead of the null-reference in B44, both systems fire: V4's "Three at Once" via `OnSpecialActivated`, then the V3 shuriken throw via `_currentAbility.Activate`, back to back. Same root cause as B44 (the V3/V4 negotiation), same resolution path (disappears once B4 lands). Not fixed now.

### B46. `WeaponCycler` is documented as an editor-only debug helper but is a hard production dependency
**Surfaced 2026-08-19, same investigation.** `WeaponCycler` is disabled (`m_Enabled: 0`) on `pfb_player.prefab` and documented as an editor/play-mode helper, but its `ResolveWeapon` method is actually called by `WeaponInventory.ResolveEquipData` and `WeaponPickup` — meaning per-character weapon-variant resolution runs through a component that's marked disabled and labeled as a debug tool. Not a bug (it evidently still runs when called directly, regardless of the `enabled` flag, since Unity only skips `Update`/`MonoBehaviour` messages on disabled components, not direct method calls) — but worth relabeling so a future contributor doesn't disable/remove it thinking it's safe to.

## P2 — World 1 Room 1 build (2026-08-19) — needs a small fix before validation can finish

### B48. `pfb_AudioManager` / `pfb_AttackTelegraphService` nested under a group object in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` — breaks `DontDestroyOnLoad` — **FIXED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-19**
Both rebuilt as scene-root objects. Confirmed two ways: `m_TransformParent: {fileID: 0}` for both in the saved `.unity` file, and zero `DontDestroyOnLoad` warnings in a clean isolated Play Mode run.

### B49. Real timing gap, confirmed via A/B test — will affect EVERY future from-scratch room, not just Room 1 — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
**Confirmed 2026-08-19, elevated to P1.** `RoomManager.Start()` spawns `NavMeshAgent` enemies synchronously; `LevelBuilder`'s NavMesh bake is deferred via coroutine and completes a frame later. In every *old* scene this was invisible because `Assets/_Project/Scenes/<name>/NavMesh.asset` — a vestigial pre-baked NavMesh ADR-0002 already flags as superseded — happens to still be on disk and supplies stale-but-valid NavMesh data on frame 0, papering over the gap. A brand-new scene has no such leftover file, so the underlying race is exposed for the first time: `"Failed to create agent because there is no valid NavMesh"` fires in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` and is confirmed absent in the old `CulDeSac_Room1` — and confirmed to reappear-as-absent again if the old scene's leftover NavMesh is loaded alongside Room 1 (it's a global resource, not scene-scoped, so this proves the mechanism, not a coincidence).

**Fix chosen: option 2 (RoomManager waits for a "NavMesh ready" signal), not option 1 (eager/synchronous bake).** Investigated before picking: `BuildNavMeshDeferred`'s existing one-frame defer is a *correctness* requirement, not a mobile-performance one — its own comment says it exists "because freshly-Instantiated colliders/meshes are not fully registered on the same frame they spawn," and `NavMeshSurface.BuildNavMesh()` is called synchronously either way (no async spread across frames to protect). Forcing the whole bake into the same frame as prop-spawning would reintroduce exactly the collider-registration bug the defer was written to prevent — so option 1 was rejected as unsafe, not just unnecessary. Option 2 leaves the bake's own timing completely untouched and only resequences *when enemies are created* relative to it.

**What changed, both in `Systems/LevelBuilder.cs` and `Systems/RoomManager.cs` (shared code, not a per-room workaround):**
- `LevelBuilder` gained `public bool IsNavMeshReady` and `public event Action OnNavMeshReady`, set/fired immediately after `_navMeshSurface.BuildNavMesh()` completes inside the existing `BuildNavMeshDeferred` coroutine.
- `RoomManager.Start()` no longer calls `ActivateRoom(0)` directly. A new `BeginRoom0WhenNavMeshReady()` activates immediately only if `_levelBuilder` is null or already reports ready; otherwise it subscribes to `OnNavMeshReady` and activates from that callback (unsubscribing in both the callback and `OnDestroy`, so no dangling subscription).
- `RoomManager.Start()` also now falls back to `FindAnyObjectByType<LevelBuilder>(FindObjectsInactive.Include)` when `_levelBuilder` was never explicitly wired — the same one-time, Start()-only find-if-unassigned pattern `GameManager` already uses for its UI screens. This is what makes the fix apply to **old, Inspector-authored scenes too**, not just new RoomDataSO-driven ones — those scenes were only ever safe by accident (the leftover `NavMesh.asset`), and the owner asked for the real sequencing to be correct regardless of whether that crutch exists.

**Validated:** recompiled clean (zero new errors, only pre-existing third-party warnings). Re-tested `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` in isolation (only scene loaded, confirmed via `get_loaded_scenes`) — the NavMesh-agent errors are gone, the 2 initial `WagonWheelRoller`s still spawn correctly (now one frame later than before, imperceptible), and the camera-clearance validator still passes with zero violations. Spot-checked old `CulDeSac_Room1` in isolation (loaded read-only, force-closed afterward via `EditorSceneManager.CloseScene(scene, true)` without ever saving it — confirmed byte-identical on disk afterward via `git status`): still works exactly as before, no NavMesh-agent errors there either (it never had them).

**One harmless side effect, documented rather than hidden:** `GameManager.Start()` now logs `"[GameManager] No enemies found at Start — win condition will not trigger via enemy counter."` in every RoomManager-driven scene (confirmed in both `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` and old `CulDeSac_Room1`), because enemies no longer exist at the exact moment `GameManager.Start()` runs. This is inert: `GameManager.CheckWinCondition()` already returns early whenever `RoomManager.Instance != null` (see its own comment — "If a RoomManager is present it owns all win and room-clear logic"), which is true in every scene where this warning can now fire. Left as-is since fixing it would mean touching `GameManager.cs`, outside this fix's scope (`LevelBuilder`/`RoomManager` only, per the owner's own framing) — worth a small follow-up (e.g. skip the warning when `RoomManager.Instance != null`) but not blocking.

**Incidental discovery, unrelated to this fix, not acted on:** re-running the camera-clearance validator against the old `CulDeSac_Room1` (never checked in isolation before) surfaced `"30 of 390 NavMesh vertices have < 8.0m clear behind"` — a real pre-existing clearance gap in that reference scene. Not fixed or otherwise touched, since that scene is reference-only; noted here only because it surfaced as a byproduct of this validation pass.

### B50. Room 1 validation — **COMPLETE 2026-08-19**
Unity Editor responsive again after owner checked it. Full validation performed: console clean (aside from expected/benign lines and the B49 NavMesh warning, now understood), 2 of 3 WagonWheelRollers spawn correctly with the 3rd trickling in on a death (existing `RoomManager` refill logic, unchanged), telegraph indicator confirmed rendering correctly via screenshot (cyan circle matching `AttackTelegraphService.ParryableColor` and ADR-0003's parryable shape exactly), camera-clearance validator passed with zero violations across three separate Play Mode entries.

**Near-miss during recovery, caught and fully resolved:** while recovering from the earlier stuck-bridge incident, the engineer discovered `manage_scene`'s `save` action ignores any path override and always saves whichever scene is currently active — this once emptied Room 1's own scene to 0 GameObjects, and once wrote a small (harmless, default-value-only) diff into the protected `ForgeLoop_Test.unity`. Both were caught immediately: `ForgeLoop_Test.unity` reverted via `git checkout` (confirmed byte-identical to HEAD), Room 1 rebuilt from the engineer's own build log. Final state verified: all 6 protected reference scenes are byte-identical to their committed state (`git status` clean on all of them), Room 1's scene verified correct (1768 lines, 30 GameObjects, all wiring re-checked). No actual harm done, but recorded here for the record since it involved the protected scenes.

### B51. Genuine asset gap: no Watering Can → Six-Shooter pickup model
Of the GDD's 4 Cul-de-Sac objects (Jump Rope→Lasso, Bike Horn→Dynamite Bundle, Garden Trowel→Quickdraw Blade, Watering Can→Six-Shooter), only 3 have a raw pickup asset. The Six-Shooter has an *equipped*-weapon asset (`WeaponData_obj_sixshooter_equipped.asset`) but no pickup model/prefab in the pattern the other three follow. Confirmed real gap, not an oversight in the room build. Owner previously said they're open to generating new Meshy models for genuine gaps like this — this is one.

### B52. Saloon Facade / Saloon Sign Board placed in Room 1 — **DONE 2026-08-19**
Previously deliberately left out (see original note below) until the camera-clearance validator's output could be trusted — confirmed working across B50's three separate Play Mode entries, so this follow-up was unblocked.

**Correction to the original note:** only `pfb_env_saloon_facade` is actually on the "Building" layer (confirmed via `go.layer` on the prefab asset — `Building`, with a collider on the root also tagged `Building`, which is what `LevelBuilder._cameraClearanceMask` raycasts against). `pfb_env_saloon_sign_board` is on `Default`, not `Building` — it's a plain sign plank, not occlusion-relevant geometry, so it was never actually a clearance risk. The original note's "both" was a documentation assumption made before either prefab was inspected in Unity.

**Placement:** added both to `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset`'s `envProps[]` (the same data-driven pattern `LevelBuilder.SpawnEnvProps()` already uses for every other Room 1 prop — no scene edits, no new architecture). Orientation was verified empirically per the asset-pipeline rule against assuming a universal model-forward axis: a temporary instance + 6-angle contact-sheet screenshot showed the facade's entrance (door, "SALOON" text) faces local +Z at identity rotation, so it's placed at `(-17, 0, -2.5)` with `eulerRotation.y = 90` — against the room's west wall (`Boundary_West` at x=-20, invisible collider, no renderer, so slight depth overlap into it is cosmetically inert), entrance facing +X into the play area, clear of the covered wagon (`x=-14, z=2`), mailbox-telegraph pole (`x=-16, z=6`), and stacked crate (`x=-12, z=-4`) already in that quadrant. The sign board (blank on both faces — no unique front/back to get wrong) is mounted at `(-14.9, 4.2, -2.5)`, same `y=90` facing, just above the facade's entrance — reads as a hanging shop sign, matching the "Western main-street" ask.

**Validated:** Play Mode entry in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` (isolated), console showed `[LevelBuilder] Runtime NavMesh baked: 325 verts, 137 tris.` (up from B50's 305/127 — the added facade collider contributes new nearby geometry) with **zero** `[LevelBuilder] Camera clearance` warnings — that warning only logs when `behindViolations > 0 || aboveViolations > 0`, so its absence is the pass condition, not just silence. Confirmed visually with two in-Play screenshots (bird's-eye and street-level, both attached to this session's build log) — facade reads clearly as a saloon building with working entrance framing and the sign mounted above the door; no visible clipping into neighboring props.

**Correction, 2026-08-19 — the `(-17, 0, -2.5)` placement above turned out to be functionally invisible during real play. See B57 for the investigation, real root cause, and the position it was moved to.**

### B53. `CameraFollowTargetInjector` could permanently fail to wire the follow camera — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by the owner watching a live Play Mode session of old `CulDeSac_Room1`** (spot-checked while validating B49): the camera sat frozen at `pfb_CM_FollowCam`'s raw prefab-default transform instead of following the player at all. Confirmed via `CinemachineCamera.Follow == null` at runtime, and confirmed **not** a B49 side effect and **not** limited to the old scene — it reproduced in a genuinely clean, isolated, domain-reloaded session, and the mechanism is non-deterministic (scene root GameObject initialization order), so it was never safely scoped to only the old scene.

**Root cause:** `CameraFollowTargetInjector.Start()` called `GameObject.FindWithTag("Player")` exactly once. Scene root `Awake()`/`Start()` order across different GameObjects is not guaranteed in Unity, and this exact hazard is already known and worked around elsewhere in this codebase — `RunStartUI.Awake()` has the identical `FindWithTag("Player")` call with its own comment ("Awake() may have run before pfb_player was instantiated (scene object order)") and a lazy re-find in `Show()` to recover. `CameraFollowTargetInjector` never got the same treatment: if it lost that race, `Follow`/`LookAt` stayed null forever and the camera never moved.

**Fix:** `Assets/_Project/Scripts/Core/CameraFollowTargetInjector.cs` now retries in `Update()` until the player is found (mirroring `RunStartUI`'s established lazy-refind pattern), then disables itself (`enabled = false`) the instant it succeeds — zero ongoing per-frame cost once the camera is correctly following, only a trivial single-bool-check cost in the rare frames before that.

**Validated:** recompiled clean (zero new errors). Re-tested old `CulDeSac_Room1` in isolation (only scene loaded, freshly domain-reloaded to rule out stale state) — `Follow` correctly resolves to `pfb_player`, camera position tracks the player at a stable, consistent offset frame-to-frame (screenshot confirmed correct "behind the character" framing, both at the paused character-select screen and using `find_gameobjects`/`manage_gameobject` position reads). Re-tested `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` in isolation afterward — still works correctly, console clean, camera correctly framed.

**Also found while investigating, separate, pre-existing issue, different root cause than B49 despite superficially similar wording — FIXED 2026-08-19:** `WagonWheelRollerAI.cs` logged `"Stop" can only be called on an active agent that has been placed on a NavMesh` and `Setting linear velocity of a kinematic body is not supported` during actual combat (not at scene start). Traced to two separate state-machine bugs in that script: (1) `EnterPostAttack()` called `_agent.isStopped = true` without checking that `EnterCharging()` had already set `_agent.enabled = false` moments earlier — calling `isStopped` on a disabled agent throws exactly this error; (2) `HitRollRoutine` captured `_rb.isKinematic` at hit-start and restored it later, but wasn't cancelled by `EnterDead()` (only `_stateRoutine` was), so a hit-roll in flight when the killing blow lands could call `_rb.linearVelocity = Vector3.zero` while `EnterDead()` had already set `isKinematic = true`. Both were cosmetic Unity engine warnings (not exceptions, didn't break gameplay).

**Fix:** `EnterPostAttack()` now guards the same way `EnterDead()` already did — `if (_agent.isOnNavMesh) _agent.isStopped = true;` — since `EnterCharging()` always disables the agent immediately before `EnterPostAttack()` runs. A new `_hitRollRoutine` field (already existed, tracked but never cancelled) is now explicitly stopped and nulled at the top of `EnterDead()`, mirroring the exact pattern `OnDestroy()` already used for the same field, so a killing blow that lands mid-hit-roll can no longer race the coroutine's own kinematic restore.

**Validated:** recompiled clean (zero new errors/warnings — `validate_script` reported 0/0). Play-mode tested in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`: reflection-invoked `EnterCharging()` -> `EnterPostAttack()` back-to-back (the exact production call sequence from `ChargeDurationRoutine`/`UpdateCharge`) produced zero NavMeshAgent errors. Separately, reflection-started `HitRollRoutine` immediately interrupted with `EnterDead()` (simulating a killing blow landing mid-roll) confirmed `_hitRollRoutine` is nulled by `EnterDead()` and, waited out past the hit-roll's 0.35s duration, produced zero kinematic-body errors — stack traces confirmed via `read_console(include_stacktrace)` showed no `HitRollRoutine` frames in the console at all afterward, only the two unrelated call sites logged as B54.

**Incidental discovery while isolating the above with stack traces, not fixed here, same warning text but different call sites — see B54:** ambient combat in the same test session kept logging `Setting linear velocity of a kinematic body is not supported` from `EnterChargeWindUp()` (line 344) and from `EnterChase()` (line 329, called via `PostAttackRoutine`'s stun-end transition) — neither of these is the `EnterPostAttack`/`HitRollRoutine` pair described above.

### B54. Two more `WagonWheelRollerAI.cs` kinematic-ordering bugs, same warning as B53's fix, different call sites — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Found incidentally while isolating and validating the B53 `HitRollRoutine`/`EnterDead` fix with console stack traces on 2026-08-19 — confirmed via `read_console(include_stacktrace=true)`, not guesswork. Both threw `Setting linear velocity of a kinematic body is not supported` during **ordinary, frequent combat** (every charge wind-up, every post-attack-to-chase transition), unlike B53's pair which needed a specific interrupt-timing race — these fired far more often in practice:

1. **`EnterChargeWindUp()` (line 344)** set `_rb.linearVelocity = Vector3.zero;` immediately, but the roller is normally in `Chase` state when this is called (from `UpdateChase()`), and `EnterChase()` already set `_rb.isKinematic = true`. `EnterChargeWindUp()` never set `isKinematic = false` before touching velocity — that transition only happens later in `EnterCharging()`. Reproduced on effectively every charge attempt.
2. **`EnterChase()` (line 329)** set `_rb.linearVelocity = Vector3.zero;` *before* `_rb.isKinematic = true;` on the very next line — but when called from `PostAttackRoutine`'s stun-end transition (line 502), the rigidbody was already kinematic (set during the routine's landing step, well before the stun wait ends), so the velocity write landed on an already-kinematic body. This was an ordering bug local to `EnterChase()` itself: it wrote velocity before confirming/forcing `isKinematic = false` first.

**Fix:** guarded each velocity write behind `if (!_rb.isKinematic)` rather than reordering — a kinematic body carries no meaningful velocity for the write to clear, so skipping it is a no-op when the body is already kinematic and behaves exactly as before when it isn't. `EnterChargeWindUp()`'s write is only ever reached while `Chase`'s kinematic Rigidbody is still active, so the guard makes that line inert there (matches intent — `EnterCharging()` is what actually flips kinematic off later). `EnterChase()`'s write still runs normally for its Idle->Chase caller (non-kinematic at that point) and is skipped for its PostAttack->Chase caller (already kinematic), with `_rb.isKinematic = true` on the next line unconditionally re-asserting the invariant either way.

**Validated:** recompiled clean (`validate_script` 0/0; only pre-existing third-party `ExplosiveLLC/SuperCharacterController` serialization warnings present, unrelated). Play-mode tested in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` two ways: (1) reflection-forced each method's exact real-world precondition immediately before calling it — `EnterChase()` then `EnterChargeWindUp()` (Chase, `isKinematic=true`) for the first bug, and directly set `_state=PostAttack` + `isKinematic=true` then called `EnterChase()` for the second — both produced zero console entries (`read_console(include_stacktrace)` came back empty in both cases, confirmed via before/after field reads showing the exact preconditions were in effect). (2) Let one roller run the **entire real, untouched production cycle** (`ChargeWindUp` -> `Charging` -> `PostAttack` -> stun -> `Chase`) purely through its own coroutine timers over ~12s of real play time with no further scripted intervention — console stayed clean throughout (one unrelated pre-existing `[WeaponHolder] Cannot equip weapon 'Quickdraw Blade' — handBone is null` warning appeared from ForgePanel/WeaponHolder, unrelated to this file). Confirmed via `git status` that none of the 6 protected scenes were touched; only `WagonWheelRollerAI.cs` and this doc changed.

### B55. Player character was permanently invisible in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
**Reported by the owner:** no player character visible at all while playing Room 1. Investigated properly rather than guessing, per the owner's ask, and ruled out several plausible theories with direct evidence before landing on the real cause.

**Confirmed NOT the cause, with evidence (owner's specific theory going in):** `BoxSystem.ApplyBoxVisuals()`/`characterModels[]`/`ForceApplyBox()`. `pfb_player` has **no `BoxSystem` component at all** — confirmed three ways: `SerializedObject` lookup on the runtime instance found no such component; a text search of `pfb_player.prefab` for `BoxSystem` returns zero matches; and `RunStartUI`'s own runtime log printed `"[RunStartUI] BoxSystem not found on Player."` every time `Show()` ran. `BoxSystem` is legacy — `RunStartUI.cs`'s own comment calls it "no box swapping in V3" and every call site is already null-guarded with a reflection fallback. It is dead code for this prefab, not the active mechanism, in both `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` and the old reference `CulDeSac_Room1` (confirmed identical component list on `pfb_player` in both, read-only, via additive scene load, closed without saving).

**Actual root cause:** `pfb_player`'s 4 gender/style model roots (`NinjaMale_CharacterModel`, `NinjaFemale_CharacterModel`, `Cowboy_CharacterModel`, `Cowgirl_CharacterModel`) ship inactive by default — this is identical in the old reference scene too, and is expected: activation is meant to happen entirely through `RunStartUI.Show()` → `ApplyGender()`/`ApplyStyle()` at run start. `RunStartUI.Show()` gates its whole character-select flow behind `bool atRunStartRoom = GameManager.ZoneStartScene.ContainsValue(activeSceneName)`. `GameManager.ZoneStartScene[0]` still said `"CulDeSac_Room1"` — the old scene — so for `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` this evaluated `false`, and combined with `ProgressionSystem.Instance` being null (no `pfb_ProgressionSystem` in this scene, unlike the old one), `RunStartUI.Show()` took its silent-pass-through branch and logged `"[RunStartUI] Silent pass-through — no saved selection and not at run-start room. Skipping character apply to avoid clobbering active model."` — never showing the picker, never calling `ApplyGender()`/`ApplyStyle()`. All 4 models stayed inactive forever, with only an easy-to-miss log line, no error.

The same stale mapping also silently broke two other things sharing the same root cause, found while tracing `ZoneStartScene`'s call sites: `GameManager.InitRoomQueue()` only rebuilds the shuffled random-room queue `if (currentScene == ZoneStartScene[0])`, so entering Room 1 via `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` would never have queued `AmbushAlley`/`SaloonFront`/`MailboxRow`; and `GameManager.Restart()` calls `SceneManager.LoadScene(ZoneStartScene[0])`, so a mid-run restart from Room 1 would have sent the player back to the old, protected reference scene instead of Room 1_v2.

**Fix, `Core/GameManager.cs`:** `ZoneStartScene[0]` now points at `"CulDeSac_Room1_v2"` (the old value is documented as reference-only in a comment, not silently dropped). `ZoneIndexByScene` gained `{ "CulDeSac_Room1_v2", 0 }` as an additional entry (old `"CulDeSac_Room1"` entry kept, matching the existing pattern of multiple scene-name aliases already mapping to the same zone index). Also added `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` to **Build Settings** (it was completely absent — confirmed via `EditorBuildSettings.scenes` — so a real `SceneManager.LoadScene("CulDeSac_Room1_v2")` call, e.g. from the `Restart()` fix above, would have thrown at runtime even with the C# fix in place; appended at build index 8, after the existing 8 scenes, so no existing numeric-index-dependent code shifts — confirmed only one such call in the codebase, `PauseMenu.cs`'s self-relative `LoadScene(GetActiveScene().buildIndex)`, which is index-agnostic).

**Validated:** recompiled clean. Re-tested in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`: console showed the picker actually engage this time (`Gender selected`, `Style selected`, `ApplyGender — target=...CharacterModel`, `Hide — player input re-enabled` — no more silent pass-through line), and a runtime reflection check confirmed exactly one of the 4 `char1` `SkinnedMeshRenderer`s active (matching the picked style) plus an equipped weapon rendering in-hand. The owner independently played the same session live, picked Ninja, and confirmed they could see the character. Spot-checked old `CulDeSac_Room1` read-only (additive load, closed without saving, confirmed byte-identical via `git status` afterward) purely to compare `pfb_player`'s component list and confirm the "no `BoxSystem`" finding wasn't scene-specific.

**Not fixed, flagged only:** `ProgressionSystem`/`SaveSystem` are still absent from `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` (present as scene-root objects in the old scene, e.g. `pfb_ProgressionSystem`, `pfb_SaveSystem` — not yet added here). This didn't block the fix above (every code path that reads `ProgressionSystem.Instance` is already null-guarded), but progression saving/loading and permanent-upgrade overlays won't function in this room until those are added. Worth its own follow-up pass.

### B56. Enemy health bar and parry telegraph floated too high above `WagonWheelRoller` at the new camera angle — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
**Reported by the owner:** health bar sits noticeably above the enemy, not just over the head, in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`. Investigated rather than assumed, per the coordinator's ask.

**Confirmed the cause before touching anything:** `EnemyHealthBar._offset` (world-space, added to the enemy's local origin at ground level) was `{x:0, y:2.5, z:0}` on **all 6** enemy prefabs that have the component (`pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller`, `pfb_enemy_gnome_grunt`, `pfb_enemy_milepost_marshal`, `pfb_enemy_skeptic_grunt`, `pfb_enemy_spincycle`, `pfb_enemy_sprinkler_sentinel`) — an identical, unmodified shared value, confirming this was stale tuning debt (never revisited when `_barWidth`/`_barHeight` were corrected for the new camera), not an intentional per-enemy setting. Measured each prefab's actual model height via renderer bounds to check the value against reality: `WagonWheelRoller` topY ≈ 0.90m, `sprinkler_sentinel` ≈ 0.65m, `gnome_grunt` ≈ 1.10m, `skeptic_grunt` ≈ 1.89m, `milepost_marshal` ≈ 2.68m, `spincycle` (boss, 2x scale) ≈ 4.25m — a shared y=2.5 offset floats roughly 1.6m above `WagonWheelRoller`'s actual head height, exactly matching the report, while sitting far too low for the boss. Ruled out scale mismatch as an alternative cause — `pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller`'s root `localScale` is `(1,1,1)`, nothing unusual there.

**Fix scope — WagonWheelRoller only, by design, noted per the coordinator's "your call":** the 6 prefabs span a >4m height range, so one shared constant can never be correct for all of them — each needs its own visually-verified value, and `WagonWheelRoller` is the only one actually spawned in this room today. Changed `pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller.prefab`'s `EnemyHealthBar._offset` from `{0, 2.5, 0}` to `{0, 1.3, 0}` (≈0.4m clear above the ≈0.9m head — close without touching the model).

**Also fixed, same complaint — the parry/attack telegraph indicator (owner asked mid-session: "move the parry indicator down as well"):** `AttackTelegraphService._defaultHeightOffset` was `3.2` — far higher than the health bar's old 2.5, same root issue. Unlike the health bar this is a **single shared default on `pfb_AttackTelegraphService`**, not a per-enemy value — every `AttackTelegraphService.Show(...)` call site in the codebase (`BasicEnemyAI`, `SkepticGruntAI`, `SpinCycleAI`, `PermitPulperBossAI`, `SprinklerSentinelAI`, `MilepostMarshalAI`, `HitchingHoundAI`, `WagonWheelRollerAI`) uses the 3-argument overload and relies on this one default; none pass the optional explicit `heightOffset` the API already supports. Lowered the shared default from `3.2` to `1.8`, tuned and visually verified only against `WagonWheelRoller` (the enemy actually in this room) — **this changes telegraph height for every enemy type**, including taller ones not present in this room (`spincycle` boss ≈4.25m, `PermitPulperBoss`, `MilepostMarshal` ≈2.68m), which were not visually re-checked here and may now read too low. Flagged below rather than silently left as a risk.

**Validated:** recompiled clean (zero new errors). Re-tested in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`: reflection-forced a `WagonWheelRoller` into `EnterChargeWindUp()` (the state that shows the telegraph) and screenshotted it in Play Mode — health bar sits just above the wheel rim, telegraph circle sits a clear step higher than the bar without floating off into open sky, both clearly readable and no longer far above the model. The owner was independently playing the same live session throughout and confirmed directly: **"That is perfect."**

**Not fixed, flagged only:** the other 5 enemy prefabs (`gnome_grunt`, `milepost_marshal`, `skeptic_grunt`, `spincycle`, `sprinkler_sentinel`) still carry the stale `_offset.y = 2.5` health-bar value, each needing its own visually-tuned number given the >4m height spread — none of them are in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` today so this didn't block the ask, but it will resurface the moment any of them appear in a scene built for the new camera. Separately, `AttackTelegraphService._defaultHeightOffset = 1.8` is now tuned for `WagonWheelRoller` specifically and was not re-verified against the taller enemies sharing that same single default — worth converting to an explicit per-AI `heightOffset` argument (the `Show()` overload already supports one; no call site currently uses it) rather than one shared constant, the next time any of those taller enemies are in an active room.

### B57. Saloon Facade was invisible during real play despite passing every earlier check — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
**Reported by the owner:** the B52 facade placement (`-17, 0, -2.5`, against the west wall) can't actually be seen while playing, even though orientation was verified and the clearance validator passed. Investigated each of the coordinator's four candidate causes with evidence rather than moving it blindly.

**Ruled out:**
- **Rotation:** re-verified against the real camera's fixed 36° pitch (not an arbitrary angle) by instantiating the facade at the candidate rotation and screenshotting from a position/offset matching the actual rig. The entrance-faces-+X conclusion from B52 was correct — rotation was never the problem.
- **`CameraOcclusion` fade:** reviewed `CameraOcclusion.cs` — it only fades a Building-layer renderer that sits directly on the line between the camera and the player's torso, and only ever fades to `_fadeAlpha` (0.2), never to fully invisible. A background building behind everything, never between camera and player, is structurally not a candidate for this system.
- **Simple lateral (X) framing:** initially suspected (the facade sat ~6-8m to the side of where combat naturally clusters, right at the edge of `AspectAdaptiveCameraFraming`'s guaranteed 16m lateral width) — real, but turned out not to be the dominant effect once the actual cause was found.

**Actual root cause, confirmed numerically via `Camera.main.WorldToViewportPoint` (not visual guessing):** this camera rig (fixed 36° pitch, 45° vertical FOV, ~6.35m height, ~8.65m behind the player along a **yaw-locked -Z axis** — it never turns to face what the player is looking at) is built to frame Kid at close range, low to the ground — not to showcase tall background scenery at a distance. The facade is 7.18m tall. The camera's upper view-frustum edge sits at a fixed `pitch - vFOV/2 = 13.5°` below horizontal; for a point at height `h` to stay in frame, camera-to-point depth must be `<= (cameraHeight - h) / tan(13.5°)`. At `h = 3.5m` (the door/"SALOON" sign — the whole identity-establishing part of the prop) that ceiling is only ~11.87m of camera-to-point depth, i.e. the **player must be within ~3.2m of the facade in the camera's fixed forward (Z) axis** for the sign/door to clear the top of frame at all. My first reposition attempt (moved it to the north wall at `(5, 0, 17.2)` to fix the lateral issue) made this dramatically worse — confirmed via the same viewport check: facade-base was barely in frame at `viewport.y=0.86` while the door/sign height was already at `viewport.y=1.12` (**above** the visible frame, `>1.0`), so only the roofline peeked in and the entire recognizable part of the building was clipped off the top of the screen. The original `-2.5` Z placement was actually fine on the depth axis (close to where the player already fights) — the real fix needed to keep that depth while pulling the X in from the room's extreme edge, not push the whole prop further away.

**Fix:** moved the facade to `(-11, 0, 1)` (`eulerRotation.y = 90`, unchanged from B52 — orientation was never wrong) and the sign board to `(-8.7, 3.2, 1)` (lowered from B52's `y=4.2` to `y=3.2`, same reasoning — keeps it inside the safe viewport band without needing the player to stand exactly on top of it). This sits close enough to the existing loot-zone/crate/covered-wagon cluster (already-established combat area on that side of the room) that the door and sign clear the top of frame whenever the player is anywhere near that cluster, while staying clear of the covered wagon (`-14, 2`), stacked crate (`-12, -4`), and loot-zone weapon (`-8, -3`) already there.

**Validated:** recompiled clean. Re-ran the camera-clearance validator via a fresh Play Mode entry — `[LevelBuilder] Runtime NavMesh baked: 318 verts, 132 tris.`, zero `Camera clearance` warnings. Numerically confirmed via `WorldToViewportPoint` with the player at a realistic nearby combat position (`-8, 0.08, 0`, settled to `-7.3, 3.44` by the live session): facade base, window height, and door/sign height all landed inside `[0,1]` on both axes (only the roofline peak — taller than the camera itself, which can never be fully in frame at any distance given this pitch — fell outside, which is expected and not needed for the building to read). Confirmed visually with two real in-game screenshots (`pfb_Main_Camera`, actual HUD visible) at two different player distances — the "SALOON" text, entrance, and porch are clearly readable in both, next to live `WagonWheelRoller`s and their now-correctly-placed health bars from B56.

**Lesson for future prop placement in this room:** any prop taller than roughly the camera's own height (~6.35m) needs to sit within a few metres of where the player actually stands to view it, on this camera's fixed depth axis — "distant backdrop" is not a placement pattern this rig supports. Worth a line in `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` or the ADR-0001 consequences list so the next tall prop doesn't repeat this.

### B58. Minimap didn't work in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` — **FIXED 2026-08-20**
**Reported by the owner** while live-testing the same session as B57. Same family of bug as B55 (missing player-visibility) and the "not fixed, flagged only" note at the end of B55: `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` is missing several scene-root service/manager objects that the old `CulDeSac_Room1` carries directly in the `.unity` file rather than via any script-driven bootstrap.

**Root cause, confirmed by text search rather than assumption:** `pfb_MinimapCamera` — a plain `Camera` (no script) that renders a top-down orthographic view into the shared `MinimapRT` RenderTexture asset, which the HUD's `MinimapView` `RawImage` displays — is a scene-root object in `CulDeSac_Room1.unity` (`grep` confirmed 1 match) but was **completely absent** from `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` (0 matches). With no camera ever rendering into it, `MinimapRT` never received fresh content, so the minimap panel showed nothing live. `pfb_hud_v4` (the HUD prefab) is shared unchanged between both scenes and its `RawImage.texture` reference was already correctly pointed at `MinimapRT` — the panel and wiring were never the problem, only the missing camera that feeds it.

**Fix:** added `pfb_MinimapCamera` to `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` as a scene-root prefab instance (matching the `[Managers]`-sibling, not-nested convention already established for `pfb_AudioManager`/`pfb_AttackTelegraphService` per the `feedback_dontdestroyonload_root` pattern), at the exact same transform the old scene uses (`(0, 40, 7)`, looking straight down — unmodified from the prefab default) and with the same non-default overrides the old scene's instance carries: `cullingMask` restricted to Default + Building + the Minimap-indicator layer (`1048833`, i.e. layers 0/8/20), `nearClipPlane 0.1`, `farClipPlane 65` (the raw prefab ships with a much wider default culling mask and 1000 far clip — clearly a generic starting point, not what either scene actually ships with).

**Validated:** recompiled clean (only the pre-existing benign `RuntimeNavMeshBuilder` read-access message, unrelated). Play Mode entry confirmed `pfb_MinimapCamera.targetTexture` and the HUD's `MinimapView.texture` both resolve to the same `MinimapRT` asset and the camera is enabled and active. Screenshotted the live HUD — the minimap panel now shows real rendered top-down content (visible enemy-indicator dots matching `WagonWheelRoller`'s configured orange `MinimapIndicator` color) instead of a blank/stale texture. The owner confirmed directly, live: "Minimap is now working."

Saved `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` (not one of the 6 protected scenes — confirmed via `git status` that none of them were touched) since this is a scene-composition fix, not something expressible as a data asset or script change alone.

**Not fixed, flagged only — same root cause, still open:** per B55's own closing note, `ProgressionSystem`/`SaveSystem` are still absent from `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` as scene-root objects. Worth doing a single pass to audit every scene-root object the old `CulDeSac_Room1` carries against what `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` actually has, rather than fixing each missing one only as it's individually reported.

### B60. Character-select screen (`RunStartUI`) stopped appearing entirely in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` — **FIXED 2026-08-20**
**Reported by the owner** after playing the scene fresh: the "CHOOSE YOUR HERO" picker, confirmed working earlier the same session (B55's fix), no longer appeared at all — the run just started straight into gameplay with no character chosen.

**Confirmed NOT the cause, with evidence:** `GameManager.ZoneStartScene[0]`/`ZoneIndexByScene["CulDeSac_Room1_v2"]` (B55's fix) — read directly from `Core/GameManager.cs`, still correctly `"CulDeSac_Room1_v2"`. `RunStartUI.Show()`'s gating logic — read fresh, unchanged, and its own `Debug.LogWarning`s (`"Show() — PS=..."`, `"Show — player input disabled"`) confirmed live in the Console on every repro that it took the *show-the-picker* branch and called `gameObject.SetActive(true)` on the panel every single time, exactly as designed. No code change was needed anywhere in `RunStartUI.cs` or `GameManager.cs`.

**Actual root cause:** the currently-open Unity Editor had `pfb_hud_v4` — the HUD prefab instance under `[HUD]`, and the direct ancestor of every `RunStartUI` panel — sitting **inactive in the live, in-memory scene** (`GameObject.activeSelf == false`), with **no corresponding change ever written to `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` on disk** (confirmed via `git diff`/`git status` showing the scene untouched, and via text search finding no `m_IsActive` override for `pfb_hud_v4`'s root anywhere in the file). So `RunStartUI.Show()`'s own `SetActive(true)` call on the panel kept succeeding (`activeSelf == true`) but had no visible effect, because `activeInHierarchy` was still `false` — an inactive ancestor. This is why the bug looked code-caused (nothing to fix in script) and scene-caused (nothing to fix on disk) at the same time: it was neither — it was unsaved Play-Mode-adjacent editor state left in memory, the same general class of problem as the `FollowOffset`/camera-rotation stray overrides already caught twice this session, except this time the state was never flushed to disk at all. Most likely origin, per the task's own framing: an earlier session got blocked on this exact picker with no click tool available while testing the forge-VFX flow, and very plausibly disabled `pfb_hud_v4` (or its `[HUD]` ancestor chain) directly in the Hierarchy to get past it, then stopped Play without it reverting — this project's Enter Play Mode configuration does not revert scene object state on Stop (independently reconfirmed below).

**Fix:** re-enabled `pfb_hud_v4` (`GameObject.SetActive(true)`) in the live scene and saved `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity`. While preparing that save, caught a second, unrelated piece of the same unsaved-editor-state baggage before it could be committed alongside the real fix: `pfb_player`'s root local position had drifted from its committed `(0, 0, 0)` to `(0, 0, -2.5)` — reverted to `(0, 0, 0)` before the final save. Confirmed via `git diff` that the saved scene now contains exactly two intentional changes (`pfb_hud_v4`'s `m_IsActive: 1` override, and an inert `m_IsActive: 0` override on the nested `pfb_run_end_screen` — traced via `PrefabUtility.GetCorrespondingObjectFromSource` to confirm it's the same already-correct "hidden by default" state every other screen panel in the HUD ships with, not a new problem) and nothing else.

**Validated twice, the second time from a genuinely clean load:** first pass — cleared the Console, entered Play Mode, confirmed `RunStartUI`'s own logs fired the show-picker branch, confirmed live via script query that the full ancestor chain (`StyleSelectPanel` → `HUD_Canvas` → `pfb_hud_v4` → `[HUD]`) was active, and screenshotted the Game view showing "CHOOSE YOUR HERO" with Male/Female, Ninja/Cowboy, Easy/Medium/Hard, and Start, alongside a fully-rendered HUD (health bar, minimap, joystick, action buttons, cardboard counter, bag button). Second pass, specifically to rule out lingering in-memory state per the task's own instruction: reloaded `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` from disk (`isDirty: false` confirmed before entering Play), entered Play Mode fresh, and got an identical successful result and screenshot.

**Not fixed here, flagged only:** `ProgressionSystem.Instance` is still `null` in this scene (`Show()` logs `PS=NULL HasSelection=N/A` every time) — same open item B58 already flagged. Harmless for a fresh run-start (falls through to correctly showing the picker) but means the "silently restore prior selection on scene re-entry" branch can never exercise in this scene until `ProgressionSystem` is added as a scene-root object. Also flagged, pre-existing and unrelated to this regression (present unchanged since at least commit `4dc7e9c9`, long before this session): `pfb_hud_v4.prefab` carries **9 duplicate, fully-wired `StyleSelectPanel` GameObjects** under the same `HUD_Canvas`, and `GameManager._runStartUI` has no explicit scene-level wiring, always falling back to `Object.FindAnyObjectByType<RunStartUI>()` among those 9 identical candidates. It happened not to matter here (the resolved instance was correctly wired either way), but it's fragile non-determinism worth cleaning up — either delete the 8 redundant duplicates or explicitly wire `GameManager._runStartUI` to one specific instance — before it causes a harder-to-diagnose bug than this one.

### B61. UMA (Unity Multipurpose Avatar) considered and rejected for character pipeline — decision recorded so it isn't re-litigated
**2026-08-20.** Owner asked about adopting UMA specifically to stop having to re-tune weapon grip positioning per character every time a new one is added. **Decided against it.** UMA's core value is runtime avatar customization (mixing body parts, sliders, races) — BoxForged has a small, fixed, hand-crafted cast with a specific "cardboard and marker" aesthetic already built through Meshy; the game doesn't need players building avatars from parts. Adopting UMA would mean migrating every existing character into its skeleton/slot system for a feature set (customization) the game doesn't use.

**What's being done instead:** a consistently-named weapon-socket transform convention (e.g. `WeaponSocket_R`) added once per character's hand bone, with `WeaponHolder` looking it up by name rather than relying on a per-character hardcoded offset. This is a one-time setup cost per character (same manual tuning the owner already does — but saved as a permanent, discoverable Transform on the prefab instead of code) rather than a framework migration. Being proven out on Cowboy and Ninja (male) first; see the dedicated entry this produces for the exact repeatable steps to apply to new characters.

### B62. Female Ninja and Cowgirl models being regenerated — visual design correction, not new canon
**2026-08-20.** Owner: the current Female Ninja and Cowgirl Meshy models read as too old/sexualized for what should be a children's game — both are supposed to be the same ~8-12 age range as Kid (see `docs/CREATIVE_STATE.md`/`docs/design/GDD-V2.md`). This is a visual-execution fix, not a change to either character's locked personality, backstory, or role — same characters, same story, corrected proportions/clothing/design language (chunky kid-proportioned, fully covered, matching the established "cardboard and marker" style guide already used for Kid). New Meshy prompts given to the owner 2026-08-20, explicitly specifying child body proportions and full coverage. Once the new models come back from Meshy and are rigged, apply the B61 weapon-socket convention to them as part of import — don't let this regress back into per-character offset tuning.

## P3 — Worth doing, not urgent

### B14. Save migration path
`Core/SaveSystem.cs` has a `version` field used purely as a corruption sentinel (`:70-78`) — no migration branch. Bumping it would silently do nothing. `Data` is exposed as a mutable reference and `ProgressionSystem.cs:252` mutates without saving (per-kill Spark flushes only on upgrade, conversion, or `OnApplicationPause`). Persistence is also split across two backends — JSON for `SaveData`, PlayerPrefs for `CutsceneFlags`.

Low severity pre-release; expensive the moment real players have saves. **Do before first release.**

### B15. Mutable state on shared ScriptableObject assets
e.g. `TheFirstStrikeBehaviour._firstHitReady`, `_cachedCombat`. A single-player-only assumption — and `docs/CREATIVE_STATE.md` records co-op as designed-in from day one. Directly incompatible.

### B16. `MaterialPropertyBlock` guidance is inverted for URP
`Mobile_RPAsset` has `m_UseSRPBatcher: 1`. Under the SRP Batcher, per-instance `Material` copies still batch (same shader variant), while **`MaterialPropertyBlock` breaks SRP batching**. The comment at `Enemy/SpinCycleAI.cs:1123` endorsing MPB is a performance trap on this pipeline.

### B17. Shadow distance vs camera framing
**Corrected 2026-08-19 — the original numbers here were slightly off.** `Mobile_RPAsset.asset` actually has `m_ShadowDistance: 50` (main light) with a **1024×1024** main-light shadowmap (256×256 was the additional-lights low tier, not the main light) and 1 cascade. Against the new camera's actual relevant ground depth (~25m, computed from the implemented rig: 5.5m height, 7.57m back, 36° pitch, 45° vertical FOV), one 1024² cascade stretched over 50m wastes roughly half the atlas in each axis. **Dropping `m_ShadowDistance` to 25 roughly doubles effective shadow texel density at zero cost, and culls shadow casters beyond the camera's reach — which also reduces draw calls.** Directly thermal-relevant given the 10–15 minute run length. Not yet done.

### B18. Weapon attach pooling
`Player/WeaponHolder.cs:164-167` does `Destroy` + `Instantiate` on every equip, with a standing `// TODO: replace Destroy/Instantiate with an object pool`. Pool only if profiling shows it matters.

### B19. Unreferenced components that were written and never attached
**Partially resolved 2026-08-19.** `Systems/WeaponForgeAnimation.cs` — GUID was in zero prefabs and zero scenes; `OnWeaponForged` / `OnWeaponUpgraded` now have a live subscriber (`Systems/ForgePresenter.cs`, added in Sprint 0), which supersedes what `WeaponForgeAnimation` attempted. `WeaponForgeAnimation.cs` is now confirmed fully redundant — candidate for deletion, not attachment. `Player/WeaponEquipController.cs` is still not on the player prefab, so `WeaponHolder.cs:251` still calls it through a null-conditional and its two events still never fire — that half is still open. Attach or remove.

### B20. `SaveTester` must not ship
`Core/SaveTester.cs` is a 149-line IMGUI `OnGUI` debug panel. Strip from release builds.

### B21. Documentation/reality drift — **DONE 2026-08-19**
The camera was documented as `(0, 12, -8)` with a hard look-at for an entire phase; the prefab had `(7.879929, 11, -10)` with a **−38.2° yaw** appearing in no document. On a project where docs are the AI's primary context, a wrong doc actively propagates errors. `docs/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` and GDD items 22/41 corrected now that ADR-0001 is Accepted and implemented. Prefer citing prefab/asset values over prose going forward.

### B22. `PlayerController` camera caching
`Player/PlayerController.cs:184` disables movement entirely when `_mainCamera` is null, and `_mainCamera` is cached once in `Awake` (`:69`) and never re-resolved. Any future rig that *replaces* the Main Camera GameObject at runtime silently freezes the player.
