### B24. Forge presentation — art/prefab wiring needed before the moment is visible
**Surfaced 2026-08-19, implementation complete. UPDATE 2026-08-20: functional wiring now done and live-tested in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`; only final art polish remains.**

**Now wired:**
- `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/VFX/pfb_ForgeRevealText.prefab` — new, a world-space `TextMeshPro` (3D) using TMP's built-in default font (LiberationSans SDF), simple outline, no final art — assigned to `ForgePresenter._revealTextPrefab` on `pfb_player`. Placeholder copy only (existing `_forgeRevealFormat`/`_upgradeRevealFormat` strings, unchanged — still a writer decision).
- `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/Weapons/pfb_wpn_lasso.prefab` (the Lasso's real equipped prefab, shared by all character-gender variants via their `baseEquippedData` resolution — see B25's note on this path) gained two child `ParticleSystem`s (`EpicGlowVFX` orange, `LegendaryGlowVFX` purple, both inactive by default, simple functional materials under `Assets/_Project/Materials/VFX/`) plus a `WeaponTierGlow` component on its root wired to both. Chosen because it's one of this room's three forgeable weapons (`WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset`) and, at `rarity: Legendary`, is the only one of the three that can reach both Epic and Legendary tiers (GardenTrowel/Quickdraw Blade is Rare-capped at Epic; BikeHorn/Dynamite Bundle is also Legendary-rarity and could get the same treatment later the same way).
- `ForgePresenter` component added to `pfb_player` (requires `ForgeController`, already present). `_weaponHolder`/`_weaponInventory` left unassigned (auto-resolved from sibling components in `Awake`, per the component's own designed fallback) and `_imaginationVolume` left unassigned too — `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` already has an `ImaginationRestore_Volume` GameObject, which `ForgePresenter`'s own by-name `GameObject.Find` fallback picks up automatically at runtime.

**Live-tested, real forge/upgrade calls via `ForgeController.TryForge`/`TryUpgrade` (the actual production methods, not a mock) in a live Play Mode session:** forged a Lasso from Standard through Epic to Legendary; confirmed via direct component inspection that `WeaponHolder.Attach`'s real `WeaponTierGlow.SetTier(Legendary)` call activated exactly the Legendary child (epic child confirmed inactive) on the actually-equipped weapon instance — **and confirmed visually**, a close-up screenshot shows the purple Legendary glow rendering on the held weapon in-game. The timed ghost/transform/colour-bloom/reveal-text beat could not be caught in a screenshot in the same session (the remote Editor-automation round-trip is slower than the ~1-2s beat, and repeated `execute_code` calls ended Play Mode mid-session more than once, a known tooling quirk — see `[[feedback_unity_mcp_scene_save_safety]]`-style notes), but it fired with zero console errors both times `OnWeaponForged`/`OnWeaponUpgraded` were dispatched, with `_revealTextPrefab` confirmed non-null, so nothing in the sequence's own guard conditions should have skipped it.

**UPDATE 2026-08-20 (later same day) — owner played the real flow and saw the reveal text but NOT the glow. Root cause found and fixed; it was never a wiring/code-path bug.**

**Part 1, ruled out: no divergence between the UI-driven and API-driven paths.** Traced `ForgePanel.Forge(int)`/`Upgrade(int)` (the private methods a real button click invokes) line by line — both call `_forgeController.TryForge(bagIndex)` / `TryUpgrade(slotIndex)`, the *exact same public methods* called directly in the earlier test. `ForgePanel` is a thin UI wrapper; it adds no branching around the forge/upgrade call itself. `WeaponInventory.AddToWeaponSlot`/`TryUpgrade`/`SetActiveSlot` all write the new `WeaponInstance` (with its new tier) into the slot array *before* calling `WeaponHolder.EquipWeapon`, so `WeaponHolder.Attach`'s unconditional `tierGlow.SetTier(tier)` call always sees the correct tier regardless of entry point. There is no code path from a real click that skips or diverges from what a direct API call exercises.

**Part 2, confirmed as the actual cause: the glow was genuinely rendering, but invisible at real gameplay distance/settings.** Re-verified with a screenshot from the *actual live gameplay camera* (not a hand-positioned close-up like the previous update's proof shot) at the weapon's normal held distance — the Legendary glow, while active, was not perceptible at all. Inspecting the particle system and material turned up two compounding practical issues, exactly as suspected going in:
1. **Both glow materials had `_Surface: 0` (Opaque) with no sprite texture assigned** — `RenderType: Opaque`, `_ZWrite: 1`, depth-writing, no alpha falloff. An existing working VFX material in the project (`Assets/_Project/Materials/VFX/MAT_SpinCycleSparks.mat`, same shader — `Universal Render Pipeline/Particles/Unlit`) uses `_Surface: 1` (Transparent), `_ZWrite: 0`, `_SrcBlend: 5`/`_DstBlend: 10` (standard alpha blend), `RenderType: Transparent`, render queue 3000, with `DepthOnly`/`SHADOWCASTER` passes disabled — the glow materials had none of this, so they rendered as small hard-edged opaque squares rather than a soft glow.
2. **Particle scale/size was too small for the current camera framing** — `startSize` 0.12m (Epic) / 0.17m (Legendary), which was sized against a close-up test shot, not against this project's actual (now heavily pulled-back, per the 2026-08-20 camera-yaw work) live gameplay camera distance.

**Fix applied:** `EpicGlowVFX_Mat.mat`/`LegendaryGlowVFX_Mat.mat` reconfigured to match `MAT_SpinCycleSparks.mat`'s proven transparent-alpha-blend setup exactly (`_Surface:1`, `_ZWrite:0`, `_SrcBlend:5`, `_DstBlend:10`, `RenderType: Transparent`, render queue 3000, `DepthOnly`/`SHADOWCASTER` passes disabled, `_SURFACE_TYPE_TRANSPARENT` keyword) via the Material API (not hand-edited YAML, to avoid getting a shader-keyword/queue combination subtly wrong). Particle `startSize` increased to 0.35m (Epic) / 0.5m (Legendary), shape radius increased proportionally, emission rate increased (35/45 per second) and `maxParticles` raised to 60 so the higher rate isn't clipped. **Re-verified with a fresh screenshot from the real, unmodified live gameplay camera** (same forge-to-Legendary sequence, same camera the player actually sees) — the glow is now clearly visible around the held weapon at actual gameplay distance, where before the fix it was not.

**Also noticed, not confirmed as a real bug — flagging rather than filing:** while reproducing via the real `WorkbenchProp` proximity trigger (moving the player near the live workbench GameObject rather than calling `ForgeController` directly), the `ForgePanel` did not visibly open even after `WorkbenchProp.PlayerInRange` confirmed a fresh entry transition and `Open` was confirmed subscribed to `OnPlayerEntered` via reflection. This could be a real Awake/OnEnable ordering race (the same class of bug this codebase has hit before — B53, `CameraFollowTargetInjector`) or could be an artifact of teleporting the player via raw `transform.position` (bypassing `CharacterController`) during automated testing. Not chased further because it doesn't match the owner's own report — they did get the panel to open, use it, and see the reveal text, so whatever this is didn't block their actual session. Worth a real manual walk-up test to rule out.

**Still outstanding (art/content only, matches `docs/SPRINT.md`'s remaining checklist item 4):**
- No actual `_forgeRevealSfx`/`_upgradeRevealSfx` audio clips exist yet (B29, same root cause as the telegraph audio — `AudioManager.Play()` no-ops safely without them)
- Reveal text prefab uses TMP's default font/plain outline — functional, not final art
- Glow VFX are now visible but still basic colored particle blobs — functional, not final art; may want a proper soft round sprite texture instead of the shader's default (currently untextured) quad
- Only the Lasso has glow wired; GardenTrowel and BikeHorn (this room's other two forgeable weapons) do not yet — BikeHorn (also Legendary-rarity) is the natural next one to wire the same way
- A genuine manual playtest (walk to the workbench, use the real `ForgePanel` UI, watch the full beat with your own eyes) still has not been done by this agent — testing continues to drive the real API/reflection-invoked methods and confirm end state via screenshot, not a literal click-through

**UPDATE 2026-08-20 — owner played the real forge UI flow: reported the whole animation feels too fast, and no particles are visible during the sequence (separate from the persistent tier glow already fixed above). Root cause found and fixed; feel pass, not a final lock.**

**Root cause, read directly from `ForgePresenter.cs` before touching anything (per the task's own instruction not to guess):** the anticipation ("household object rises") and transformation ("becomes the weapon") beats had **zero particle VFX of their own** — `SpawnAnticipationGhost` only instantiates the raw-object mesh and lerps its position/rotation, and `TransformationBeat` only scales/rotates the equipped weapon's transform. Neither beat ever spawned a single particle. The only VFX in the whole forge feature was `WeaponTierGlow`'s *persistent* Epic/Legendary glow (the thing already fixed for visibility on 2026-08-20 earlier the same day) — a completely different, separately-triggered effect that isn't part of this timed sequence at all. So "not seeing particles" was accurate and structural, not a visibility bug in an existing effect.

**A second finding, worse than a simple gap: a fully-authored particle prefab for exactly this purpose already existed and was already broken the same way B24's glow was.** `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/VFX/pfb_forge_vfx.prefab` — gold/orange sparks, single 60-particle burst, `gravityModifier: -0.3` (rises), 0.8-1.4s lifetime, self-destroying via `ParticleSystem.stopAction = Destroy` — was sitting in the project referenced by nothing (confirmed via `grep -rl` across every `.prefab`/`.unity`/`.asset`/`.cs` file: only its own `.meta`). Its material, `MAT_Spark.mat`, had the **exact same bug** already found and fixed once this sprint for `EpicGlowVFX_Mat`/`LegendaryGlowVFX_Mat`: `_Surface: 0` (Opaque), `_ZWrite: 1`, same shader family as the already-proven-working `MAT_SpinCycleSparks.mat`. Its particle `startSize` (0.04–0.12m) was also smaller than the glow's pre-fix sizes. Even if this prefab had been wired in as-is, it would have rendered as invisible hard-edged squares — same failure mode, same fix.

**Fixes applied:**
1. **`MAT_Spark.mat`** fixed via the Material API (mirroring `MAT_SpinCycleSparks.mat`'s exact proven settings, not hand-edited YAML): `_Surface: 1`, `_ZWrite: 0`, `_SrcBlend: 5`/`_DstBlend: 10`, `RenderType: Transparent`, render queue 3000, `DepthOnly`/`SHADOWCASTER` passes disabled, `_SURFACE_TYPE_TRANSPARENT` keyword enabled.
2. **`pfb_forge_vfx.prefab`** particle `startSize` raised from 0.04–0.12 to 0.18–0.5 (via the `ParticleSystem` API on a loaded prefab instance, not hand-edited curve YAML) — same "too small for real gameplay distance" bug class as the glow fix, roughly a 4x bump since these starting values were even smaller than the glow's pre-fix ones.
3. **`ForgePresenter.cs`** gained `_anticipationVfxPrefab`/`_transformationVfxPrefab` (`ParticleSystem` fields, both wired to `pfb_forge_vfx` on `pfb_player`) and a `SpawnBeatVfx()` helper that instantiates one at the anchor at the start of each beat, rotated so the prefab's local +Z (its shape module's emit direction) points world-up to match its authored negative-gravity "rising sparks" look. Fire-and-forget by design — no pooling/tracking needed since forge frequency is low and the prefab self-destroys.
4. **Timing widened across the whole sequence** — 0.35s/0.5s beats with nothing to look at (now, something to look at) still read as "too fast" per the owner's explicit feedback: `_anticipationDuration` 0.35→0.8, `_transformDuration` 0.5→0.9, `_bloomFadeInDuration` 0.25→0.35, `_bloomHoldDuration` 0.4→0.5, `_bloomFadeOutDuration` 0.6→0.7, `_revealHoldDuration` 1.1→1.3, `_upgradeDurationScale` 0.6→0.75 (upgrades were shrinking already-short beats to sub-0.3s, unreadable). Full forge sequence is now ~0.8+0.9=1.7s to weapon reveal, +1.3s hold+0.4s fade of text ≈ 3.3s total (up from ~2.35s) — readable without becoming a multi-second wait.
5. **Above-head anchor added as a toggleable option, defaulted on.** New `PresentationAnchor` enum (`WeaponMuzzle` = original hand-height staging via `WeaponHolder.MuzzlePosition`, `AboveHead` = `transform.position + Vector3.up * _aboveHeadHeight`) with `_aboveHeadHeight` defaulting to 2.1m (`pfb_player`'s `CharacterController` is 1.8m tall). Defaulted to `AboveHead` per the owner's stated reasoning (small on-screen character at the post-B27/B59 pulled-back, 35°-yaw camera makes hand-height easy to miss). Flipping the one inspector field back to `WeaponMuzzle` is a direct, no-rewrite comparison. `_revealTextOffset` lowered from `(0, 1.4, 0)` to `(0, 0.4, 0)` so the reveal text doesn't end up stacked ~3.5m in the air on top of the new anchor.
6. All of the above were also written directly onto **`pfb_player.prefab`'s live `ForgePresenter` component** (not just the C# defaults) — this project has hit the stale-prefab-serialization bug (B8/B9/B39) enough times that it's now standard practice to verify: read the component's actual serialized values back from disk before assuming a C# default change took effect on an existing prefab instance. Confirmed via `git diff`: only the intended fields changed, no collateral reformatting.

**Live-verified, through the real production API (`ForgeController.TryUpgrade`, the same method `ForgePanel`'s button calls — no mock), in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`:** placed a BikeHorn (Legendary-rarity, so it can actually reach Legendary unlike the Rare-capped GardenTrowel) at Epic in a weapon slot, made it active, called `TryUpgrade` — real `OnWeaponUpgraded` → `ForgePresenter.Dispatch` fired. A screenshot taken from the actual unmodified live gameplay camera immediately afterward shows a small gold/orange particle cluster that was **not present** in an identical-framing screenshot taken before the trigger — direct visual confirmation the fixed VFX renders, in the right color, at real gameplay camera distance, where before this fix nothing existed to render at all.

**Honest limitation of this verification pass, not hidden:** the MCP screenshot tool appears to capture a frame that doesn't advance across rapid successive requests in this headless automation session (four screenshots taken across roughly a minute of real wall-clock time, including tool round-trips, came back pixel-identical) — the same "remote automation is slower than the beat" friction already noted in this project's history. This means the single differing screenshot is solid evidence the VFX now exists and renders, but does **not** by itself prove the full anticipation→transformation→bloom→text sequencing plays smoothly frame-to-frame, nor does it confirm timing "feels right" — that judgment needs the owner's own eyes on a real playthrough. A literal walk-up-and-click `ForgePanel` test (not API-driven) was attempted first this session — `WorkbenchProp` (confirmed via its own source comment to be pure `Update()`-polled `Vector3.Distance`, not a physics trigger, specifically because `CharacterController` without a `Rigidbody` doesn't reliably fire trigger events in Unity 6 — so the earlier "not confirmed as a bug" flakiness noted in `[[project_room1_v2_build]]` was never really about triggers) — but blocked on `RunStartUI`'s character-select screen not being dismissible without a UI click tool this session has; the screen's GameObject (`pfb_hud_v4`) was deactivated directly to unblock the camera view for screenshots instead, which is why the direct-API path was used for the actual forge/upgrade trigger, consistent with the same fallback the previous B24 session already used and documented.

**Still outstanding after this pass:**
- Owner asked for another look once they've played this round — timing/anchor/VFX values above are tuned by judgment (existing prefab's authored color/shape, roughly-quadrupled size, roughly-doubled durations), not owner-confirmed final.
- `_transformationVfxPrefab` and `_anticipationVfxPrefab` both point at the same `pfb_forge_vfx` — intentionally kept as two separate fields so the beats can diverge (e.g. a sharper "impact" look for the reveal vs. a softer "gather" look for the rise) without another wiring pass, but they render identically today.
- `pfb_forge_vfx`'s spark color (gold/orange) doesn't vary by tier the way the persistent glow does (orange Epic / purple Legendary) — worth considering for a future pass if tier-specific "gathering" color reads as more coherent with the glow.
- A genuine manual playtest by the owner (or a session with real UI-click tooling) is still the only way to judge "does this actually feel right now," not just "does something render."

### B25. Epic/legendary weapon-visual prefabs don't reach most weapons yet
The new `epicWeaponPrefab`/`legendaryWeaponPrefab` read path (`WeaponHolder.ResolveTierPrefab`) works, but most authored weapons resolve through a `baseEquippedData` override (a V3 `WeaponData` asset) before `WeaponHolder` ever sees the `WeaponObjectSO` — confirmed live against `WeaponObject_Broomstick.asset`. So the new tier-prefab path only takes effect for weapons *without* that override. This is downstream of the existing V3/V4 ability-system split (D3, still open) — full coverage needs that reconciliation, not a fix in isolation. The persistent glow (B24) is unaffected by this and applies regardless of which data path resolved the weapon.

### B26. Forge panel no longer reopens automatically after a successful forge
`UI/ForgePanel.cs` now closes on every successful forge/upgrade to hand off to the in-world moment (the owner's locked staging decision). If the owner wants faster repeat-forging without walking away and back to the workbench, that's a small follow-up — not implemented, not requested, just noting the behavior changed.

### B28. `BuildingOcclusionFader` deleted — it had a real bug beyond the reason it was removed
**Resolved 2026-08-19.** Per ADR-0001's required occlusion-system consolidation, `CameraOcclusion.cs` was kept and `Systems/BuildingOcclusionFader.cs` was deleted. Reasons: it wasn't attached to anything in any scene/prefab (dead code), **and** it mutated shared `Material` assets in place (`rend.sharedMaterials[m].SetFloat(...)`) rather than instancing them — meaning if it had ever run, it would have permanently converted every building sharing that material to Transparent, project-wide, with no way to undo short of re-importing the asset. Worth remembering as a general lesson: dead code doing an unsafe thing is still worth removing, not just ignoring. `CameraOcclusion.cs` was retuned to a single torso-height raycast (replacing an 8-corner viewport-AABB test) — correct for the new low camera pitch and cheaper.

### B29. No audio assets exist yet for the new attack-telegraph sound cues
`Core/SoundData.cs` gained four new `SoundEvent` entries (`TelegraphMeleeParryable`, `TelegraphMeleeUnparryable`, `TelegraphAreaUnparryable`, `TelegraphProjectile`) as part of the new telegraph system (ADR-0003). No actual audio clips are authored or wired to a `SoundData` asset yet — `AudioManager.Play()` is a safe no-op until they exist, so nothing is broken, but the telegraph's audio channel (explicitly required by the ADR as occlusion-proof reinforcement) is currently silent. Needs an audio-authoring pass.

### B30. Dynamically-resolved parryability collapsed to the conservative shape
SpinCycle's Haymaker (parryable only when the drum window faces the player) and PermitPulperBossAI's ShredSpin (parryable based on player-facing at hit time) both resolve parryability *after* the wind-up telegraph would need to appear — a static icon can't honestly represent a state that hasn't resolved yet. Both were classified as their un-parryable shape (the safe choice) with the real dynamic tell (pendulum facing / facing check) left untouched as the actual skill-expression mechanism. Not a bug, but worth knowing: the shape/icon on these two specific attacks is deliberately conservative, not a precise readout.

### B31. `PermitPulperAI` / `NoticePusherAI` cannot receive a telegraph without a balance change
Both have zero wind-up window today — the hit lands the same frame the attack starts. Adding a telegraph would require giving them one, which is a balance/design decision, not something folded into the Sprint 0 telegraph work. Connects to the still-open D1 decision (both are currently dormant per owner decision, not used in Worlds 1–2) — relevant only if either is ever activated.

### B23. Persistent singleton count
Five `DontDestroyOnLoad` static-instance MonoBehaviours (`AudioManager`, `SaveSystem`, `ProgressionSystem`, `DifficultyManager`, `CutscenePlayer`). `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` records two and says not to add more by default. A service-locator refactor is not worth its risk now — but the count should stop growing. (`AttackTelegraphService` adds a sixth — self-bootstrapping, same pattern, noting the count moved rather than treating it as new debt.)

### B36. Forge reveal text is instantiate-and-destroy, not pooled
**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review.** Frequency is low enough (only on forge/upgrade) that this isn't a throughput concern, but `TextMeshPro` instantiation is expensive per-call (mesh allocation, material resolution, font atlas binding), and it happens at exactly the moment the forge transformation's emotional peak plays — a single-frame hitch there is the most noticeable place in the whole game for one to happen. **Fix:** instantiate one label once, keep it deactivated, reuse it. Cheap fix, worth doing whenever this file is next touched.

### B37. `GameObject.Find` retried every forge if the Imagination volume is missing from a scene — **FIXED 2026-08-19**
Fixed as a bundled low-risk item in the Sprint 0 bug-fix pass: added a `_imaginationVolumeSearchAttempted` bool guard so `ColorBloomBeat` only ever calls `GameObject.Find(ImaginationVolumeName)` once per `ForgePresenter` instance, regardless of how many forges/upgrades happen with the volume still absent.

**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review.** `ForgePresenter` caches the Imagination-restore volume reference on success, but on failure (volume absent from the scene) the cache never populates, so the scene-wide `Find` call repeats on every subsequent forge — a standing violation of the project's own no-runtime-`Find`-in-hot-paths convention, low frequency but easy to fix with a `bool` guard.

### B38. HDR is enabled on the mobile render pipeline asset — bandwidth cost, not part of Sprint 0
**Surfaced 2026-08-19 by performance review as an adjacent finding, not part of the reviewed diff.** `Mobile_RPAsset.asset` has `m_SupportsHDR: 1`, which forces FP16 render targets — roughly doubling color bandwidth on tile-based mobile GPUs. Memory bandwidth is the primary driver of sustained thermal throttling, which is exactly the failure mode the 10–15 minute run length is designed to catch (see B1). `m_RenderScale` is already 0.8, suggesting the team is already bandwidth-aware — this is a natural next lever. Needs an on-device A/B test, not a blind toggle.

### B59. Over-the-shoulder camera offset (2026-08-20) — first-pass numbers, not owner-confirmed, and narrows one side's warning room on narrow aspects
`pfb_CM_FollowCam.prefab`'s `FollowOffset` moved from `(0, 5.5, -7.57)` through two owner-driven iterations: `(2.25, 7.425, -10.22)`, then — after the owner said the first round still felt "really really close" and under-corrected on both axes — `(3.75, 10.02, -13.79)`. Pitch (36°) and vertical FOV (45°) are unchanged throughout, so ADR-0001's F/R/top-ray floors only ever improve with further pull-back (a uniform scale-up per the existing aspect-adaptive derivation); only the lateral split needs rechecking each time. Worst-case left-side width (the side the camera leans away from, always tightest at 4:3, the narrowest supported aspect):
- Iteration 1 `(2.25, 7.425, -10.22)`: left 5.42m / right 10.58m at 4:3
- Iteration 2 `(3.75, 10.02, -13.79)`: left **5.67m** / right 13.17m at 4:3 — pulling back this far means the base rig alone now clears the 16m width floor at every supported aspect (4:3 through 21:9), so `AspectAdaptiveCameraFraming`'s runtime scaling never engages at all with this offset; left-width is minimized by the *aspect* alone, not by any floor-clamping.

Both iterations stay well clear of the enemy-telegraph-derived floor ADR-0001 uses for rear visibility (3.3m hard floor / 4m target — SkepticGrunt 3 m/s approach, 0.6s wind-up): iteration 2's worst case (5.67m) is 72% above the hard floor and 42% above target. Not yet owner-confirmed as final — flag before locking into ADR-0001.

**UPDATE 2026-08-20 (same day, third iteration): direction changed entirely — owner sent a mobile-Hades reference and asked for a rotated/diagonal isometric framing, not just a bigger over-the-shoulder offset.** This is a **yaw** change (the fixed rig now points down a diagonal instead of world -Z), explicitly still "never rotates during actual gameplay" — the fixed constant just points a different way. Tried yaw = 35° (pitch 36°/vFOV 45° still unchanged). Implementation: `pfb_CM_FollowCam`'s local rotation became `Quaternion.Euler(36, 35, 0)`, and `FollowOffset`'s horizontal (X,Z) component was rotated by the same 35° (rigid rotation, magnitude-preserving) to `(-4.84, 10.02, -13.45)` — rotating the offset alongside the rotation is required, not optional: `CinemachineFollow`'s WorldSpace binding mode adds `FollowOffset` in raw world axes regardless of the vcam's own authored rotation, so if only the rotation changes, the camera keeps sitting on the *old* straight-behind axis while pointing down a new diagonal one, decoupling position from view direction.

Real consequence confirmed, not treated as a bug: `PlayerController`'s movement is camera-relative and yaw-projected (`camForward`/`camRight` with `.y` zeroed), so this rotates the movement-input mapping by the same 35° — joystick-up no longer moves the player straight up-screen, matching how the reference game's own controls behave. This is the same mechanism ADR-0001 already flagged when the rig moved from the old undocumented -38.2° yaw to 0°.

**Found and fixed a second real bug, this one in `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance`:** its "behind" clearance raycast direction was `Vector3.back` (world -Z), hardcoded on the assumption yaw is always 0 (explicit in its own old comment). With yaw≠0 this would have silently validated the wrong axis — passing rooms that are actually unsafe along the *real* camera axis, or failing rooms that are actually fine. Fixed by adding a serialized `_cameraYawDegrees` field to `LevelBuilder` (must be kept in sync with the rig's authored yaw — same manual-sync risk the original hardcoded constant already had, just now visible/settable instead of silently wrong) and deriving the behind-direction as `Quaternion.Euler(0, _cameraYawDegrees, 0) * Vector3.back` at validation time. Set to `35` on `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`'s `LevelBuilder` instance. Confirmed: F/R/top-ray/W numbers themselves are **unaffected by yaw** — they're derived from pitch/height/vFOV/horizontal-FOV only, all camera-relative quantities, not world-axis-relative, so B59's iteration-2 acceptance numbers still hold verbatim under yaw=35°.

**Also found (again) while implementing:** the scene's `pfb_CM_FollowCam` prefab *instance* in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` carried hard `PrefabInstance` overrides pinning `m_LocalRotation.x/y/z/w` to the old yaw-0 quaternion — a third occurrence of the same override-drift pattern this session already found once for `FollowOffset` (see above) and once historically (`[[project_room1_v2_build]]`). Removed. **Given this has now happened three times in one session, a scene that carries an unexpected `PrefabInstance` override on this specific camera prefab's `Transform` or `CinemachineFollow` component should be treated as a standing risk, not a one-off** — worth a quick audit pass across the other Cul-de-Sac scenes before this rig is touched again.

**Not yet done, flagged per the task:** Room 1's already-placed env props (including the saloon facade, positioned in an earlier fix specifically around the camera's old -Z depth axis) have not been re-checked for composition under the new diagonal angle. The facade is visible but reads partially cropped at frame-left in the validation screenshot. If yaw=35° (or any non-zero yaw) is the direction the owner wants to pursue, Room 1's prop placement needs a full composition re-check, not just this smoke test — this was explicitly out of scope for this exploratory pass.

Also found and fixed in the same pass, unrelated to the value change above: `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` carried a stray `PrefabInstance` override on `FollowOffset.y`/`.z` (values `6.285466`/`-8.651087`, no `.x` override) that didn't match any authored rig value in the ADR or prefab — almost certainly a Play-Mode-computed `AspectAdaptiveCameraFraming` result that got baked into the scene during an earlier validation session (see `[[project_room1_v2_build]]`-style history) rather than an intentional per-scene override. ADR-0001 explicitly states no scene should override `FollowOffset`. Removed; worth a quick check of the other Cul-de-Sac scenes for the same drift next time one of them is opened.

## P2 — `WeaponGripPoint` socket convention (2026-08-20)

### B63. Weapon grip attachment was per-character-variant-data-driven, not socket-driven — socket convention established for Cowboy + Ninja Male; per-character duplication retired for both — **RESOLVED 2026-08-20 (round 3)**
**Trigger:** owner reported the Female Ninja's held weapon misplaced (again) — the Nth time a character's weapon grip has needed manual repositioning. **Follow-up trigger (same day):** owner tested the first round's fix directly and reported Ninja Male *and* Ninja Female both holding weapons incorrectly, contradicting the first round's claim that Ninja Male was fixed/verified.

**Root cause, fuller than the original bug report assumed.** There were two layered mechanisms:

1. `WeaponHolder.FindHandBone()` already preferred a child Transform named exactly `WeaponGripPoint` over the raw `LeftHand` bone, on all four existing character prefabs. **But the socket was doing nothing** on Cowboy/Ninja Male: it sat at local identity — functionally identical to attaching straight to the raw bone.
2. The actual per-character correction was happening one layer up, in `WeaponCycler.characterWeaponSets` (on `pfb_player`) — a full duplicate `WeaponData` asset set per character (`ScriptableObjects/Weapons/{Cowboy,NinjaMale}/`, 19 assets each — Cowgirl never had one; she always resolved to the shared/default set), resolved via `WeaponCycler.ResolveWeapon` by matching the active model's GameObject name.

**Round 1 (same day, earlier)** tuned `WeaponGripPoint`'s local position+rotation on `pfb_char_cowboy.prefab` and `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab` against the shared `WeaponData_obj_bostaff_equipped.asset`, and deliberately left `WeaponCycler.characterWeaponSets` untouched, "verified" via `WeaponObject_BikeHorn`. **That verification was hollow, root-caused this round:** `WeaponObject_BikeHorn` has no `_cb`/`_nm` variant, so `ResolveWeapon` was already silently falling back to the shared asset for that one test — round 1 never actually exercised the per-character-variant path it was trying to validate.

**Real root cause of the reported regression, confirmed this round (not a guess), with numeric + screenshot evidence, not just Cowboy vs. Ninja Male comparison:**
- Live-inspected both sockets in the open Editor before touching anything: `WeaponGripPoint`'s local position/rotation on both `pfb_char_cowboy.prefab` and `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab` exactly matched the committed prefab values from round 1 — **no drift, not a `[[feedback_play_mode_no_revert]]` recurrence this time.**
- The `Cowboy`/`NinjaMale` variant `WeaponData` assets' `gripPositionOffset`/`gripRotationOffset` were hand-tuned back when the socket was still identity. Once the socket itself started carrying a real correction (round 1), every one of those 19-per-character offsets **doubled up** with the socket's own correction. Reproduced directly: equipping the `_nm`/`_cb` bostaff and sixshooter through the real `WeaponCycler.ResolveWeapon` → `WeaponHolder.EquipWeapon` path swung the weapon down near the ankle/pointed backward off the hip on **both** Cowboy and Ninja Male — the "Cowboy looks fine" owner report reflected which weapons happened to get spot-checked, not that Cowboy's variant path was actually sound.
- Equipping the exact same weapons through the **shared/default** `WeaponData` (bypassing the variant resolution) on the same sockets landed correctly on both characters — bostaff held naturally across the body instead of at the ankle, confirmed by screenshot.
- Systematically re-verified with a numeric sweep (weapon-origin-to-socket distance) across **all 19** shared/default weapons on **both** Cowboy and Ninja Male: every one landed within 0.03–0.32m of the grip socket, and the per-weapon numbers were byte-identical between the two characters (proof the socket's `lossyScale`-normalisation convention is working as designed for both). Spot-checked bostaff, sixshooter, lunchboxshield, and `WeaponObject_BikeHorn` visually on both characters through the real gameplay resolve path.

**What changed this round:**
- `WeaponCycler.characterWeaponSets` entries for `Cowboy_CharacterModel` and `NinjaMale_CharacterModel` removed from `pfb_player.prefab` (Ninja Female's entry, and Cowgirl's absence of one, both left untouched — see below).
- `Assets/_Project/ScriptableObjects/Weapons/Cowboy/` and `.../NinjaMale/` deleted (19 `WeaponData` assets each, 38 total, plus folder `.meta`s) — confirmed via GUID grep across every `.asset`/`.prefab`/`.unity`/`.controller` in the project that `WeaponCycler.characterWeaponSets` was the *only* referencer of any of them before deleting.
- `WeaponHolder.cs` class doc comment rewritten to record the retirement and the double-offset root cause for future readers.
- Cowboy and Ninja Male now equip purely through the shared/default `WeaponData` set + their tuned sockets — architecturally identical to how Cowgirl already worked (see below), which is exactly why Cowgirl never had this bug.

**Ninja Female — diagnostic only, deliberately not fixed (new model incoming, would be wasted effort):** her `WeaponGripPoint` is still local identity (never tuned), parented to `mixamorig:LeftHand` (note the `mixamorig:` prefix — a different rig-export convention than Cowboy/Ninja Male/Cowgirl's unprefixed `LeftHand`; `WeaponHolder.FindHandBone()` already handles a namespaced bone name via its `EndsWith(":LeftHand")` check, so this alone isn't the bug). Her `characterWeaponSets` entry (19 `_nf`-suffixed assets, left untouched per task scope) is tuned against that identity socket, so she inherits the exact same "no baseline correction, everything floats near the raw bone" problem Cowboy/Ninja Male had *before* round 1 — not the double-offset problem they had *after* round 1. Fixing her is just: give her `WeaponGripPoint` real tuned values (checklist below), at which point her existing `_nf` variant assets would very likely start double-offsetting exactly like Cowboy/Ninja Male's did, and should be retired the same way rather than kept.

**Cowgirl — confirmed already correct, useful precedent, left entirely untouched per task scope:** her `WeaponCycler.characterWeaponSets` entry resolves to the *shared/default* `WeaponData` assets already (no `_cg`-suffixed folder ever existed) — she was already "sockets-only" before this task, which is exactly the target end-state for every other character and is why she never exhibited this bug.

**Round 3 (same day, later still): round 2's "RESOLVED" was declared before rotation was actually re-verified — `WeaponGripPoint`'s *rotation* on Cowboy and Ninja Male was still wrong, only the position/distance was checked.** Owner reported via a Scene-view screenshot (gizmo on `WeaponGripPoint`) that the weapon's rotation looked wrong in-hand on Ninja Male; on testing, the owner also confirmed Cowboy's rotation was wrong. Root cause: round 2's own verification was itself a repeat of the exact mistake `[[feedback_verify_test_exercises_path]]` documents — the "numeric sweep" in round 2 only checked **weapon-origin-to-socket distance**, and the one weapon spot-checked for visual rotation (the bostaff, held roughly centre-gripped and horizontal) happens to still look *plausible* even with the wrong rotation applied, because a symmetric staff held sideways doesn't obviously look "broken" the way a directional weapon does. Equipping a **sixshooter** (small, directional, asymmetric) on Cowboy immediately exposed it: the revolver floated disconnected up near the shoulder/collar, clearly wrong.

**Actual root cause of the rotation bug:** round 1's tuned `WeaponGripPoint.localRotation` — `(324.54, 197.41, 203.77)` Euler, identical on both `pfb_char_cowboy.prefab` and `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab` — was simply the wrong value on both prefabs. It was never derived from either character's own rig; empirically it does not match either character's hand-bone convention. `pfb_char_cowgirl.prefab`'s `WeaponGripPoint` (confirmed correct, never touched by this task) uses a completely different rotation — `(10.16, 186.79, 141.24)` Euler — and, tested live against three different shared weapons (bostaff, sixshooter, katana) on **both** Cowboy and Ninja Male, that exact same rotation value produces a natural-looking hold on all three characters: sixshooter holsters naturally at the hip (matching Cowgirl's own sixshooter hold), bostaff hangs/rests naturally, katana points forward from the grip. Comparing each character's `LeftHand` bone's *local* rotation relative to its own parent (`LeftForeArm`) confirms Cowboy, Ninja Male, and Cowgirl all share the same underlying rig-authoring convention (values within a few degrees of each other) — Cowboy's hand *looked* differently oriented in world-space only because his current idle/armed stance (a raised boxing-guard fist) differs from Cowgirl/Ninja Male's relaxed idle, not because his rig uses a different axis convention. The correct fix is therefore the same rotation value across Cowboy, Ninja Male, and Cowgirl, since all three share the same rig convention.

**What changed this round:** `WeaponGripPoint.localRotation` set to `(10.16, 186.79, 141.24)` Euler (byte-identical quaternion to Cowgirl's, verified via git diff) on both `pfb_char_cowboy.prefab` and `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab`, replacing the wrong `(324.54, 197.41, 203.77)` value from round 1. `localPosition`/`localScale` were left untouched (position was already confirmed correct by the owner). Verified in Play Mode against three shared weapons per character (bostaff, sixshooter, katana) via the real `Attach()` code path, screenshotted from the actual Game-view camera (not Scene-view gizmos) from multiple angles. Cowgirl's and Ninja Female's prefabs were not touched.

**Checklist for the next new character (Female Ninja re-rig, new Cowgirl model, or anyone after) — now simpler, no weapon-data duplication step:**
1. Import/rig the model as usual (`asset-pipeline` skill — validate orientation/scale/pivot per-model, never assume a prior character's fix carries over).
2. Add one child GameObject named exactly `WeaponGripPoint` under the model's hand bone (the bone `WeaponHolder.WeaponHandBone` looks for — currently `"LeftHand"` or any bone ending in `:LeftHand`, e.g. `mixamorig:LeftHand`).
3. If the character's armature root scale isn't ~1 (i.e. `LeftHand.lossyScale` isn't close to `(1,1,1)` — check via `execute_code`), set `WeaponGripPoint`'s own `localScale` to the inverse (e.g. `100` if the bone's lossy scale is `~0.01`) so its own `lossyScale` normalises to ~1.
4. In Play Mode, equip a **shared/default** `WeaponData` (no character suffix, e.g. `WeaponData_obj_bostaff_equipped.asset`) via `WeaponHolder.EquipWeapon(data)` and iterate `WeaponGripPoint`'s local position + rotation (not the weapon's own offset) until the weapon sits naturally in the hand from multiple camera angles.
5. Copy the final `localPosition`/`localRotation`/`localScale` from the live scene instance into the character's **prefab asset** (not just the scene instance — see `[[feedback_play_mode_no_revert]]`).
6. Re-check the socket against **several** weapons of different shapes (a long weapon like the bostaff and a compact one like the sixshooter both stress rotation correctness in different ways), not just one — this exact gap (testing only one weapon, and one that happened to bypass the mechanism being tested) is what let this bug ship twice. **A symmetric weapon like the bostaff can still look "plausible" when the rotation is actually wrong** (round 3, this section) — always include at least one small/asymmetric/directional weapon (sixshooter, katana) in the check, and judge *rotation*, not just that the weapon is near the hand.
7. Do **not** create a `WeaponCycler.characterWeaponSets` entry or a per-character `WeaponData` folder for this character. If a specific weapon still looks wrong after the socket is tuned, that's a per-*weapon* correction (fine to keep on the weapon's own `WeaponData` asset if it's a genuinely odd model) — not a reason to duplicate the whole set per character again.
8. If this character already has an existing `characterWeaponSets` entry from before this convention (e.g. Ninja Female today), retire it in the same pass once the socket is tuned and re-verified — don't leave a now-redundant duplicate set in place.
9. Verify with a screenshot holding at least one real forgeable weapon from a room's `WeaponDropTableSO` (not just the shared reference weapon used for tuning).

**Round 4 (2026-08-20, later still): first real per-*weapon* correction applied on top of the now-stable per-character sockets — useful precedent, kept separate from the socket work above.** Owner hand-tuned the Lasso's local Transform directly on the live `pfb_wpn_lasso(Clone)` instance while equipped on Cowboy and found: position `(0.048, -0.19, 0.017)`, rotation `(-78.971, 23.258, -164.616)` Euler, scale `0.235` uniform. Checked the architecture first rather than assuming a new mechanism was needed: `WeaponData.gripPositionOffset`/`gripRotationOffset`/`gripScale` (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/Systems/WeaponData.cs`) already exist as exactly the "per-weapon, applied on top of the per-character socket" correction this needs — `WeaponHolder.Attach()` sets the instantiated weapon's `localPosition`/`localRotation` directly from these fields and derives `localScale` from `(prefabBakedScale / handBone.lossyScale.x) * gripScale`, so they compose with (not duplicate) the character's `WeaponGripPoint` socket. This is the architecturally correct place — not a new per-character variant, which would have reintroduced exactly the duplication this session already retired for Cowboy/Ninja Male.

Read `WeaponData_obj_lasso_equipped.asset` (the shared/default asset both Cowboy and Ninja Male already resolve to, since neither has a `characterWeaponSets` entry any more — confirmed via GUID grep: it's referenced only by `pfb_player.prefab`'s shared `defaultWeapons` list and by `WeaponObject_JumpRope.asset`'s `baseEquippedData`, i.e. the Lasso model is also the JumpRope's forged-equipped visual). Its `gripRotationOffset` (`-78.971, 23.258, -164.616`) and `gripScale` (`0.235`) already matched the owner's hand-tuned values exactly — only `gripPositionOffset` differed (`-0.017, -0.104, 0.089` vs. the owner's `0.048, -0.19, 0.017`). Patched just that one field via `manage_scriptable_object` (`modifyso`, targeting the asset by path, `SerializedObject`-based — not a hand-edited YAML diff) so the change is the minimal, isolated one-line diff it should be.

**Verified through the real gameplay path, both characters, with Game-view screenshots (not Scene-view/gizmo shots):** entered Play Mode in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` (not one of the 6 protected scenes; never saved), and since this room's `pfb_player` has no `BoxSystem` (character switching here goes through `RunStartUI`'s reflection fallback, not `BoxSystem.NotifyModelChanged`), replicated that exact fallback sequence by hand — `SetActive` the target character model, then reflection-invoke `WeaponHolder.OnModelChanged()` / `CombatController.RefreshAnimator()` / `PlayerController.RefreshAnimator()` (all private, mirroring `RunStartUI.OnStartClicked()`'s own no-`BoxSystem` branch), then call the real, public `WeaponHolder.EquipWeapon(WeaponData)` — the same method `WeaponCycler`/`WeaponPickup` call in production. Confirmed numerically identical resulting local transform on both characters (`localPosition (0.048, -0.19, 0.017)`, `localScale (0.235, 0.235, 0.235)` uniform, byte-identical rotation quaternion on both — proof the B63 socket convention is holding for a third, independently-added weapon). Screenshotted via `manage_camera` (the real `pfb_Main_Camera`/Cinemachine rig, both a normal-distance gameplay shot and a closer positioned shot for grip detail): the lasso's coiled rope sits naturally gripped in the left hand on both Cowboy and Ninja Male, hanging correctly, not floating disconnected and not clipping through the leg.

**Tooling notes worth keeping for next time:**
- `manage_scriptable_object`'s `modifyso` action takes `target: {path: "Assets/..."}` and `patches: [{path: "<fieldName>", value: {...}}]` — confirmed it resolves and reports back the asset's GUID, and is the right tool for a single-field ScriptableObject edit instead of hand-editing YAML.
- Manually rendering a camera via `camera.Render()` + `RenderTexture`/`ReadPixels` inside `execute_code` **does not work under URP** — it produced a perfectly flat frame of the camera's own `SolidColor` clear color (`RGBA(0.55, 0.4, 0.22, 1)`, the same brown seen in a blocked-UI shot) on every attempt, because a manual `Camera.Render()` call bypasses URP's `ScriptableRenderContext` pipeline rather than compositing through it. `manage_camera`'s own `screenshot` action (including its `view_target`/`view_position` positioned-capture mode) renders correctly and is the only reliable way to capture Play Mode visuals in this project.
- Confirmed again (see `[[feedback_play_mode_no_revert]]`) that `execute_code` calls made *while Play Mode is running* are unreliable — one call in this session silently ended Play Mode (detected only via `manage_scene get_active`'s `rootCount` dropping back to the edit-mode count), while a later, larger single call that did all of one character's setup *and* wrote its own PNG to disk synchronously completed fine before Play Mode ended afterward. The robust pattern: do the minimum in any one `execute_code` call, verify `rootCount` immediately after, and prefer non-code tools (`manage_camera`, `manage_gameobject`) for anything that can be done without them once Play Mode state is confirmed live.
- `NinjaMale_CharacterModel` was left `SetActive(true)` in the live scene by this manual character-switching (expected, per `[[feedback_play_mode_no_revert]]` — Play Mode doesn't revert this) and was explicitly restored to `false` via `manage_gameobject` before ending the session, matching every other character model's original inactive state. The `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` scene was never saved.
- Also newly observed, not fixed (out of scope for this task, likely pre-existing): `PlayerController` throws `UnassignedReferenceException` from `Move()` (`Animator.SetFloat` on a null/unassigned `_animator`) every frame while no character model has been activated yet at Play Mode start (before `RunStartUI`'s picker is resolved). Worth a follow-up look at `PlayerController`'s own animator-refresh wiring alongside `[[project_weapon_grip_socket]]`, but did not touch it here since it's unrelated to weapon grip.

### B64. Child-safe Female Ninja / Cowgirl re-import — **IN PROGRESS 2026-08-20, not yet fully validated**
Follow-up to B62 (visual-design correction for the two Meshy models regenerated with explicit child body proportions and full coverage). Source zips: `raw-assets/models/zips/Ninja_child_rig_biped.zip`, `Cowgirl_child_rig_biped.zip` (base rigged FBX + bundled Walking/Running FBX + PBR texture set each).

**Findings:** both new rigs use plain (non-namespaced) `LeftHand` bone naming, same as Cowboy/Ninja Male/old Cowgirl — no `mixamorig:` prefix like the old Ninja Female. A bone literally named `headfront` (child of `Head`) gave an unambiguous, evidence-based visual-forward check: at root identity rotation, `headfront` sits at local +Z from `Head`, i.e. these new rigs' own mesh-forward is `+Z` — the **opposite** convention from whatever the old Cowboy/Ninja Male/Cowgirl/Ninja Female FBX exports had (all four needed `(0,180,0)` on their `_CharacterModel` container to look right). Despite the opposite native mesh-forward, **the new characters still need `(0,180,0)` on their container** — not because of mesh-forward, but because `PlayerController.Move()` (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/Player/PlayerController.cs:210-224`) hardcodes `Quaternion.LookRotation(-moveDir)` on the player root plus an assumed per-character 180° child flip; verified algebraically (rotation composition) that a character whose own mesh-forward is `+Z` needs the *same* `(0,180,0)` child correction as one whose mesh-forward is `-Z`, given this specific root-facing formula. Confirmed via owner playtest report: without the fix the new Ninja Female visibly ran backwards; applying `(0,180,0)` to the container is the fix (not yet re-confirmed by the owner after the fix landed — **needs a live retest**).

**Real bug found and fixed, not anticipated going in:** Unity's importer-generated Humanoid Avatar did not recompute cleanly after changing `ModelImporter.globalScale`/`useFileScale` on an already-imported FBX — `Animator.humanScale` read back as `~80` (should be ≈1), and the character rendered ~60+ Unity units in the air, i.e. invisible from any normal camera position. This is *why* the owner initially saw no Female Ninja on screen at all — not a missing-reference or scale-on-import problem, a stale-avatar-after-reimport problem. Fixed by toggling `ModelImporter.animationType` `Human → None → Human` (forces a full Avatar/HumanDescription regeneration) and reimporting; `humanScale` came back to `~0.81` and the character now renders at the correct position/scale. **Record this as a verified, asset-specific gotcha**: after changing `globalScale`/`useFileScale` on a Humanoid FBX import, don't trust `SaveAndReimport()` alone to refresh the Avatar — force it by cycling `animationType` through `None` or explicitly re-checking `Animator.humanScale` (~1 is sane; anything wildly off means a stale bind pose).

**Animation integration decision:** confirmed by inspecting all four existing character prefabs' Animators that this project does **not** use per-character bundled animation clips at all — Cowboy, Ninja Male, old Cowgirl, and old Ninja Female all share the exact same `AC_Player_V2.controller`, and every clip it references lives under `Assets/ExplosiveLLC/RPG Character Mecanim Animation Pack FREE/`, retargeted per-character via each character's own Humanoid Avatar. The Meshy-bundled `_Animation_Running_withSkin.fbx`/`_Animation_Walking_withSkin.fbx` files are **not used for gameplay** by any existing character and were not wired into the new ones either, to stay consistent — importing them as reference/backup is still worth doing but wasn't completed this pass. Confirmed the pattern transfers cleanly: assigning `AC_Player_V2` + each new rig's own auto-generated Humanoid Avatar reproduces the same shared-controller/per-avatar-retarget setup as the existing cast.

**What was built:**
- New FBX imports at `Assets/_Project/Models/Characters/NinjaFemale/2026-08-20_ChildSafe/` and `.../Cowgirl/2026-08-20_ChildSafe/` (dated subfolder chosen deliberately over replace-in-place, so the transition is unambiguous and the old files — `NinjaFemale_weighted_fixed.fbx`, `Cowgirl.fbx`/`Cowgirl_rigged_v2.fbx`, their materials and textures — are untouched on disk). `ModelImporter.globalScale=1`/`useFileScale=true` per project convention; textures capped at `maxTextureSize=2048` matching the existing (not-yet-B1-fixed) per-character import convention, not a new one-off policy.
- New URP/Lit materials (`MAT_NinjaFemale_ChildSafe.mat`, `MAT_Cowgirl_ChildSafe.mat`) following the existing `_BaseMap` + `smoothness=0.3`/`metallic=0` convention seen on the old characters' materials.
- New character prefabs `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/Characters/pfb_char_ninjafemale_childsafe.prefab` and `pfb_char_cowgirl_childsafe.prefab` — root `Animator` (controller `AC_Player_V2`, own avatar, `applyRootMotion=false`) + `Boxhead.Core.FootstepReceiver`, matching `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab`'s exact component makeup. A `WeaponGripPoint` child was added under each rig's `LeftHand` (no scale compensation needed — both rigs' `LeftHand.lossyScale` reads `(1,1,1)`, unlike Ninja Male/old Cowgirl's 100x-compensated convention), seeded with the historical shared rotation guess `(10.16, 186.79, 141.24)` Euler as a starting point — **not yet empirically re-tuned per B63's checklist against multiple weapons on the new rig's own proportions; treat the seeded value as unverified.**
- `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/Player/pfb_player.prefab`: the old nested `NinjaFemale_CharacterModel`/`Cowgirl_CharacterModel` prefab instances were **renamed** to `NinjaFemale_CharacterModel_OLD`/`Cowgirl_CharacterModel_OLD` (kept, permanently inactive, fully preserved — not deleted; a direct GameObject-delete on `pfb_player.prefab` was attempted first but refused by the session's own permission classifier as a destructive edit needing explicit approval, so this rename-and-add approach was used instead, which has the side benefit of being non-destructive by construction) and two new same-named containers (`NinjaFemale_CharacterModel`, `Cowgirl_CharacterModel`) were added as nested instances of the new `_childsafe` prefabs, at local identity position/scale and `(0,180,0)` rotation, inactive by default — matching every sibling container's convention. `RunStartUI`'s `transform.Find("NinjaFemale_CharacterModel")`/`Find("Cowgirl_CharacterModel")` therefore now resolves to the new models with no code changes needed. `pfb_player.prefab` itself has no `BoxSystem` component (confirmed directly), so there is no separate `characterModels` array anywhere on this prefab to reconcile.

**Verified so far:** both new characters render at the correct scale/position/pose once the Avatar fix landed (Ninja Female confirmed via Game-view screenshot in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`, selected through the real `RunStartUI` flow — `OnGenderSelected(1)` → `OnStyleSelected(0)` → `OnStartClicked()`); she auto-equips the default weapon (Lasso) via the real `WeaponHolder` path with no console errors.

**Follow-up pass, same day, later:** attempted a second engineering verification of item 1 and started item 2. Both were cut short — item 1 by the harness's own instability, item 2 by an explicit owner instruction to stop mid-check — so neither is fully closed; see below for the honest state of each.

1. **Orientation/"running backwards" — still not cleanly engineering-verified, but the owner has now visually confirmed the models directly.** Mid-session, the owner reported "Both of the female characters look good now" after looking at the running Editor themselves — this is real, direct signal and is the actual authoritative check the asset-pipeline discipline asks for (a human looking at representative gameplay movement), stronger evidence than anything this pass produced synthetically. However, the synthetic follow-up attempt (driving `PlayerController`'s private `_moveInput` field via `execute_code` reflection while Play Mode ran, to avoid needing a real input device) was badly contaminated and should not be treated as either confirming or refuting the fix on its own: (a) Play Mode silently restarted/reset to spawn at least twice mid-session with no error surfaced, matching item 5's domain-reload churn exactly; (b) something was actively feeding real, non-zero values into the same `_moveInput` field between calls (observed `(1, 0)` immediately after explicitly setting `(0, 1)`, with no code on our side writing that value) — disabling the player's `PlayerInput` component for the duration stopped that specific symptom; (c) even with `PlayerInput` disabled, one measurement showed rotation changing frame-to-frame while position stayed frozen for a full 2-second window, which doesn't match any single code path read directly (`Move()`'s translate and rotate happen in the same call) and was not root-caused before time ran out. **Net:** treat orientation as owner-confirmed-by-eye and worth shipping on that basis, but the engineering-level reflection-based verification technique this project has now tried twice (this pass and the original B64 pass) has failed both times for environment reasons, not asset reasons — do not reach for it a third time; if this needs re-checking, do it with a real playtest (actual input device / Play Mode session watched live), not synthetic field injection.
2. **`WeaponGripPoint` tuning — started, not completed, stopped by the owner before any value changed.** On Ninja Female only, equipped the shared bostaff (`WeaponData_obj_bostaff_equipped`) and screenshotted it: sits diagonally across the back, gripped at the left hand, looks passably natural at the *seeded* rotation `(10.16, 186.79, 141.24)` with only a small existing local-position offset — screenshot evidence, not a numeric pass/fail. Equipped the sixshooter next specifically because it's the asymmetric/directional weapon B63 round 3 says a symmetric weapon like the bostaff can't validate — the follow-up screenshot still showed the bostaff rather than the sixshooter (visual didn't update in the same frame the equip call returned, most likely a casualty of the same domain-reload/timing instability as item 1; not chased down). The owner then said "Stop testing the weapons for now" and testing stopped immediately, mid-check, per that instruction. **No `WeaponGripPoint` value on either `_childsafe` prefab was changed this pass — both are still exactly the seeded `(10.16, 186.79, 141.24)` rotation / zero position from the original B64 pass.** This remains genuinely open, not just unverified: do not treat the bostaff screenshot as a pass, and do not resume tuning without the owner's go-ahead.
3. Cowgirl has still not been run through the same live selection/render/weapon test as Ninja Female (fixes applied identically to both, but only Ninja Female has ever been screenshotted post-Avatar-fix, in either pass).
4. Bundled Meshy Walking/Running FBX clips were not imported for reference/backup.
5. This session hit repeated Unity domain-reload churn mid-Play again this pass (confirmed twice more: a full silent Play Mode reset to spawn state with no error, and console lines reading `"Port 6401 not released yet after reload; retrying same port"` appearing spontaneously mid-session with no script edit or reimport triggered by us) — same symptom as the original pass, still not root-caused. Getting increasingly confident this is a real, recurring environmental instability in this project's Editor/MCP-bridge combination during Play Mode `execute_code` use, not a one-off. Worth a dedicated look before relying on scripted Play Mode verification again for anything in this project.

Old assets confirmed preserved and undisturbed: `NinjaFemale_weighted_fixed.fbx`/`MAT_NinjaFemale.mat`, `Cowgirl.fbx`/`Cowgirl_rigged_v2.fbx`/`MAT_Cowgirl.mat`, `pfb_char_ninjafemale.prefab`, `pfb_char_cowgirl.prefab`, and their `characterWeaponSets`/`_nf`-suffixed `WeaponData` folder (Ninja Female's legacy per-character weapon set was **not** retired this pass since her socket isn't yet proven — do that in the same pass as item 2 above, per B63's own instruction).

### B65. Child-safe Male Ninja / Cowboy re-import — **DONE 2026-08-20, owner-confirmed in live gameplay**

Follow-up to B64, replacing/complementing the existing Male Ninja and Cowboy with new Meshy child-rig exports matching the same generation settings and child-safe visual direction (B62). Source zips: `raw-assets/models/zips/Cowboy_child_rig_biped.zip`, `Ninja_child_boy_rig_biped.zip` (base rigged FBX + bundled Walking/Running FBX + PBR texture set each).

**Findings — inspected independently, not assumed from B64:** both rigs use plain (non-namespaced) `LeftHand`/`Head`/`Hips` bone naming (same family as old Cowboy/old Ninja Male, not the `mixamorig:`-prefixed old Ninja Female). Both meshes are low-poly (Cowboy: 1012 verts / 2041 faces; Ninja Boy: 1000 verts / 2009 faces — well inside the ~20k player budget), Z-up in Blender with feet at the origin, height ≈1.75 Blender units, no degenerate transforms — no Blender-side normalization was needed for either (verified via evaluated-mesh world bounds matching raw local bounds, i.e. the FBX importer's own Y-up→Z-up conversion artifact — object-level 90° rotation + 0.01 armature scale — nets out cleanly with no visible distortion).

**Orientation — resolved with direct, same-method, same-session comparison against the already-shipped predecessors (not inherited from B64's cross-gender case):** both new rigs have a `headfront` bone (child of `Head`) exactly like B64's evidence method. Measured directly in **Unity** (not just Blender) by instantiating each raw FBX at identity container rotation and reading `headfront.position - Head.position`: both new rigs read `(0, 0, +0.26)` — headfront at `+Z` from Head. Critically, the **same measurement was repeated against the currently-shipped `Cowboy.fbx` and `Ninja.fbx`** (not assumed from prose) and came back identically `+Z` for both. Since the old characters are already shipped, working, and carry `(0, 180, 0)` on their `_CharacterModel` container, and the new rigs measured identically to their own direct predecessors by the same method in the same session, the same `(0, 180, 0)` was applied to the new containers — this is a same-generation-family match, not a blind copy of B64's convention (B64's Cowgirl/Ninja Female measured the opposite `-Z`... or at least were reported that way by a different measurement method; that discrepancy is worth a dedicated look some day but didn't block this pass since Cowboy/Ninja Boy's own predecessors gave a clean, direct, apples-to-apples answer). Note: this also surfaces a real (harmless) documentation inconsistency — `PlayerController.cs:222`'s comment claims "CharacterModel child has Y=180° to face the FBX's -Z forward," but direct measurement this pass found old Cowboy/Ninja Male's own `headfront` at identity reads `+Z`, not `-Z`. The comment's prose is stale/wrong; the actual shipped `(0,180,0)` value is still correct and was reproduced here — not fixing the comment this pass, flagging it so nobody re-derives from it as if it were ground truth.

**Humanoid Avatar:** both imported clean on the first pass (`globalScale=1`, `useFileScale=true`, `animationType=Human`, avatar created from model) — `humanScale` came back sane (Cowboy ≈0.75, Ninja Boy ≈0.83, both `isValid=true`/`isHuman=true`), no B64-style stale-avatar bug this time (that bug was specific to *re-importing* an already-imported FBX after changing scale settings; these were fresh imports with correct settings from the start).

**Weapon socket scale convention — checked per-rig, not assumed:** `LeftHand.lossyScale` reads `(1,1,1)` on both new rigs (matching old Cowboy and B64's Cowgirl/Ninja Female — no compensation needed), **not** matching old Ninja Male's `(0.01,0.01,0.01)` residual-scale convention (a 100x scale artifact apparently specific to how that one older FBX was originally imported). Confirms scale convention must be checked per rig, not inherited from the same-named predecessor.

**What was built:**
- New FBX imports at `Assets/_Project/Models/Characters/Cowboy/2026-08-20_ChildSafe/` and `.../Ninja/2026-08-20_ChildSafe/` (base rig FBX renamed `Cowboy_ChildSafe.fbx`/`NinjaBoy_ChildSafe.fbx`, plus the bundled Meshy `_Reference_Running`/`_Reference_Walking` FBX copied in as reference/backup — completing the B64 item 4 gap for these two characters). `ModelImporter.globalScale=1`/`useFileScale=true`, textures capped at `maxTextureSize=2048`, matching established convention.
- New URP/Lit materials `MAT_Cowboy_ChildSafe.mat` / `MAT_NinjaMale_ChildSafe.mat`, same `_BaseMap` + `smoothness=0.3`/`metallic=0` convention as every other character material.
- New character prefabs `pfb_char_cowboy_childsafe.prefab` / `pfb_char_ninjamale_childsafe.prefab` — same flattened-hierarchy structure as B64's `_childsafe` prefabs (Animator with own avatar + `AC_Player_V2` controller + `applyRootMotion=false`, plus `Boxhead.Core.FootstepReceiver`), matching `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab`'s component makeup. `WeaponGripPoint` added under each rig's `LeftHand` at local identity position/scale with the historical shared seed rotation `(10.16, 186.79, 141.24)` Euler — **left exactly at the seed value, not tuned**, per explicit instruction to defer weapon-grip tuning for all four new characters to one unified pass later.
- `pfb_player.prefab`: `NinjaMale_CharacterModel`/`Cowboy_CharacterModel` renamed to `NinjaMale_CharacterModel_OLD`/`Cowboy_CharacterModel_OLD` (kept, inactive, fully preserved — not deleted), two new same-named containers added as nested instances of the new `_childsafe` prefabs at local identity position/scale and `(0,180,0)` rotation, inactive by default — identical convention to every sibling container, verified directly from the saved prefab file after a prefab-stage edit (not just trusted from the tool's success message). `RunStartUI`'s `transform.Find("NinjaMale_CharacterModel")`/`Find("Cowboy_CharacterModel")` therefore resolves to the new models with no code changes needed.

**Verification — two independent, honest layers, not conflated:**
1. **Isolated Edit-Mode animation-retargeting check (clean, engineering-level, no Play Mode involved):** instantiated each new `_childsafe` prefab outside Play Mode and used `AnimationMode.SampleAnimationClip` to sample the shared `AC_Player_V2` controller's `Unarmed-Run-Forward` clip directly across its full length. `Hips` bone rotation changes smoothly and cyclically on both rigs, with the first and last samples matching almost exactly (a clean loop) — no NaN, no bone explosion, no degenerate pose. This confirms the shared-controller/per-character-avatar retargeting pattern (established in B64) transfers cleanly to both new rigs, verified directly rather than assumed.
2. **Play Mode / live orientation and character-select verification — did not get a clean, isolated engineering-level pass, same tooling limitation as B64, further compounded by concurrent live interaction.** Entered Play Mode in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` (not a protected scene) and drove `RunStartUI`'s real public methods (`OnGenderSelected`/`OnStyleSelected`/`OnStartClicked`) directly rather than reflection-injecting private fields, specifically to avoid B64's `_moveInput` contamination pattern. The console log showed **the owner's own UI clicks interleaved with this script's calls in the same live session** (a `Female`→`Ninja` then `Male`→`Cowboy` selection sequence that doesn't match this script's single Male/Cowboy call alone), and the same recurring `"Port 6401 not released yet after reload"` domain-reload churn B64 flagged repeatedly recurred again. A follow-up state query came back with all character-model containers inactive — inconclusive on its own, and not chased further as a synthetic repro, because **the owner directly reported mid-session: "I was able to start a game with all of the 4 characters and they look good."** Per this project's own asset-pipeline standard, a human directly watching representative gameplay is the strongest evidence available, stronger than anything a scripted single-session probe produced this pass. **Net: treat orientation/animation as owner-confirmed-by-eye for all 4 characters (the 2 new ones plus B64's 2), not as independently engineering-verified this pass** — the isolated Edit-Mode retargeting check above is the one piece of clean, non-contaminated engineering evidence obtained this session; live Play-Mode verification hit the same environmental instability as B64 and should not be re-attempted a third time via scripted `execute_code` — any future re-check should use a real, human-watched playtest.

Old assets confirmed preserved and undisturbed: `Cowboy.fbx`/`MAT_Cowboy.mat`, `Ninja.fbx`/`MAT_Ninja.mat`, `pfb_char_cowboy.prefab`, `pfb_char_ninjamale.prefab`, and their existing `characterWeaponSets` entries (untouched — `git status` confirms no modifications to any of the four).

This didn't change the outcome (identity was set unconditionally on all four regardless of what the read reported, then independently re-verified via a fresh reload), but flag it as a concrete instance of "verify the actual diff, don't trust a single live read" — a `PrefabUtility.LoadPrefabContents` immediately followed by `GetComponentsInChildren<Transform>` read gave three wrong answers in a row on the very first pass this session.

These are shared assets used by **all four characters, old and new alike** — zeroing them would visibly break every weapon's hold on the two already-shipped, owner-confirmed-working old characters (Cowboy, Ninja Male via B63; Cowgirl/Ninja Female via B64/B65), not just reset the 4 childsafe characters. Treated this as a real conflict between the task's assumption and actual repo state and stopped short of executing it — see chat response for the full per-asset list and recommendation (reset scope should most likely be the 4 characters' `WeaponGripPoint` sockets only, which is what was actually broken, not the long-standing per-weapon corrections).

Saved the scene; `git diff` confirms a clean 116-line addition with zero deletions — nothing else in the scene was touched. `read_console` showed no new errors/warnings from the change.
