### B69. `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` built — first of the Phase 2 random-draw rooms rebuilt under ADR-0002/the new camera — **DONE 2026-08-24, awaiting owner playtest**

Owner chose to build the next set of rooms one at a time (not all 3 back-to-back) given how many follow-up passes Room 1 needed (B55-B60). This is room 1 of that set: Ambush Alley.

**Approach — duplicated `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` rather than building from an empty scene**, via `manage_asset action=duplicate`, then rewired only what differs. This was a deliberate scope choice: Room 1's Ground+4-Boundary geometry (40m × 28m, centred so the camera rig already clears every ADR-0001 acceptance criterion) is reusable, camera-clearance-safe arena geometry, not something specific to Room 1's identity — reshaping it per room is exactly the kind of camera retuning that caused B27's aspect-ratio regression. **Consequence, flagged honestly rather than silently: this room is not a bespoke "alley" shape, just the same validated box with different dressing/encounter data.** If the owner wants a visually distinct footprint (narrower, more corridor-like) for future rooms, that's a real design ask worth its own pass, not a default I should apply unasked.

**Addendum 2026-08-24 — footprint redesigned, the above is superseded:** the owner did ask, directly: "I don't want any of the levels to appear as a copy of another level." That's now a standing rule for every future room (saved to memory as `feedback-distinct-level-geometry`), not a one-off. Redesigned this room's actual walkable footprint into a corridor-into-pocket ("keyhole") shape instead of Room 1's open box: a 10m-wide, 12m-long approach corridor (`x∈[-5,5], z∈[-8,4]`) opening at a hard step into a 32m-wide, 16m-long ambush pocket (`x∈[-16,16], z∈[4,20]`), via 6 new `Boundary_*` collider objects added to `[Level Geometry]` (the original box's outer West/East walls were deleted as now-redundant). The underlying `Ground` mesh itself was deliberately left untouched (still Room 1's exact 40m×28m plane) — reshaping the *walkable* collider footprint achieves the distinct shape without touching the camera-validated ground extent at all, so ADR-0001's F/R/W acceptance table (a property of the fixed camera rig, not room geometry) is unaffected by construction.

All spawn points (3 rollers + 1 coda SkepticGrunt, same composition as before), pickups, cardboard piles, and the workbench were repositioned to actually fit the new shape (workbench now deep in the pocket at `(0,0,18)`, rewarding clearing the ambush). Env-prop dressing was redesigned around the new shape too: two small buildings flank the corridor's east side only, three background buildings (`pfb_env_bank`, `pfb_env_blacksmith_forge` ×2) frame the pocket, plus wagons/crates/barrels/posts scattered through the pocket for cover variety — 17 env props total, comparable to Room 1's count, not a meaningful budget increase.

**Real bug caught and fixed by actually running the validator, not just doing the math by hand:** the first pass used `pfb_env_bld_shedwithcrate` (corridor) and `pfb_env_bld_twostoryhouse` (pocket) for the "building wall" dressing — both are on the Building layer, which is exactly what `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance` checks against. That first pass reported **4 of 351 NavMesh vertices with < 8.0m clear behind** (a real violation, not zero). Rather than hand-deriving the exact yaw-35° diagonal geometry to place them "just far enough" (the ground plane's own extent doesn't leave enough margin beyond the new, wider pocket to do that safely), swapped both for `pfb_env_bank`/`pfb_env_blacksmith_forge` — visually the same "building wall" role, but both happen to already be on layer 0 (confirmed by temporarily instantiating each candidate prefab and reading `GameObject.layer` live rather than trusting the prefab file's on-disk layer field, which is often not directly readable on a nested `PrefabInstance`). Re-ran the validator after the swap: **0 of 428 NavMesh vertices violate either check.** Recorded here as a real lesson: layer-8 dressing this close to a walkable edge is a live risk on this camera's diagonal yaw, not just a theoretical one — prefer layer-0 "building-shaped" props for anything within ~10m of a walkable boundary, and always confirm zero violations via an actual Play Mode run, not hand math.

**Also verified, because it's exactly the kind of thing hand math would miss:** ran `NavMesh.CalculatePath` from the player spawn to the workbench and every pickup/pile position after the redesign — all returned `PathComplete`. (A first attempt at this check returned `PathInvalid` for literally everything, including a point right next to the player; that was a false alarm from querying before the deferred one-frame NavMesh bake had actually finished after entering Play Mode — a timing mistake in the check itself, not a level defect. Re-running after confirming the `"[LevelBuilder] Runtime NavMesh baked"` log line had actually appeared gave a clean pass.) Confirmed via `get_hierarchy` after stopping Play Mode that `[Spawned]` is empty and `[Level Geometry]` has exactly the intended 9 children — no leaked runtime state saved into the scene.

**Encounter data — extracted from the OLD `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.unity`'s `RoomManager` prefab-instance overrides** (read-only; the old scene was never loaded/saved) rather than invented fresh: `roomName: "AmbushAlley"`, `maxConcurrentEnemies: 2`, 3× `pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller` + 1× `pfb_enemy_skeptic_grunt` (SkepticGrunt's first appearance in the rebuilt Cul-de-Sac). Ported as a new `RoomData_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.asset` (`RoomDataSO`, ADR-0002 format) — positions adapted to fit the reused geometry, not copied verbatim: 2 rollers at `(±8, 0, 8)`, 1 roller at `(0, 0, 14)`, the SkepticGrunt at `(0, 0, 10)` (between the near and far rollers, matching the old room's relative placement). Wired into the new scene's `LevelBuilder._roomData`/`_dropTable` (confirmed live via component read, not assumed) — `RoomManager._levelBuilder` already pointed at the scene's own `LevelBuilder` from the duplicate, `_rooms` empty, exactly the ADR-0002 pattern Room 1 uses.

**`WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.asset`** — duplicated from Room 1's drop table, kept the reusable Western dressing (crates, covered-wagon-style props, small scatter props), **removed the one prop entry that was Room 1's specific establishing shot** (the Saloon Facade at the old `(-11, 0, 1)` / rot 90° — the exact position/rotation B57 spent a full investigation tuning for Room 1's identity, not appropriate to duplicate into a different room unchanged). Workbench, cardboard piles, and weapon pickups kept at the same relative layout as Room 1's table — reasonable reuse since it's a different scene, not literal duplication of visible content.

**SkepticGrunt is new to this rebuild — did not assume WagonWheelRoller's tuned values transfer (per B56's own warning that they don't).** Measured actual model bounds live (`SkinnedMeshRenderer.bounds` on a temporary instantiated copy, deleted after measuring): world-space top ≈ `0.8228 + 2.1425/2 = 1.894m`, matching the `weapon-pivot-fix-progress` memory's independent `≈1.89m` estimate almost exactly. Applied the same "+0.4m health-bar clearance / +0.9m telegraph clearance" rule B56 derived for WagonWheelRoller: set `pfb_enemy_skeptic_grunt.prefab`'s `EnemyHealthBar._offset.y` from the stale shared default `2.5` to `2.3` (`1.894 + 0.4`, rounded). For the attack telegraph, did **not** touch `AttackTelegraphService._defaultHeightOffset` (still correctly WagonWheelRoller-tuned at `1.8`, shared across every enemy type) — instead passed an explicit `heightOffset: 2.8f` (`1.894 + 0.9`, rounded) on `SkepticGruntAI.cs`'s own `AttackTelegraphService.Show(...)` call, using the per-call overload the API already supported but no call site had ever used. This is the first enemy type in the project with its own explicit telegraph height rather than riding the shared default — worth using as the template for tuning the remaining stale enemies (`gnome_grunt`, `milepost_marshal`, `spincycle`, `sprinkler_sentinel`) whenever they next appear in a built room, per B56's own follow-up note.

**Scene/registration wiring:** added `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.unity` to Build Settings (index 10). In `Core/GameManager.cs`: added `"CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2"` to `ZoneIndexByScene` (zone 0, same as Room 1) alongside the existing `"CulDeSac_AmbushAlley"` reference-only entry (left in place, same pattern B55 used for `CulDeSac_Room1`/`CulDeSac_Room1_v2`); replaced `"CulDeSac_AmbushAlley"` with `"CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2"` in `RandomRoomPool` (`SaloonFront`/`MailboxRow` left pointing at their old scenes — not rebuilt yet). Grepped the codebase for any other hardcoded `"CulDeSac_AmbushAlley"` reference before touching `RandomRoomPool` — found none beyond the two `GameManager.cs` dictionaries just discussed.

**Validated:** recompiled clean (`read_console`, zero new errors — only the pre-existing benign `SuperCharacterController` serialization warnings and the pre-existing, harmless `[EnemySpawnPoint] '...MarkerPrefab': _enemyPrefab is not assigned` message that fires off the *raw prefab asset* on every domain reload regardless of scene, confirmed present even before this task touched anything). Two full Play Mode passes (the first hit this project's known MCP-bridge/Play-Mode flakiness — see `feedback_unity_mcp_play_mode_capture` — and was restarted cleanly via `manage_editor stop`/`play` rather than trusted partway through): `[LevelBuilder] Runtime NavMesh baked: 305 verts, 127 tris`, zero camera-clearance warnings (the aggregated `Debug.LogWarning` `ValidateCameraClearance` would emit never fired), `RunStartUI` correctly logged `"Silent pass-through — no saved selection and not at run-start room"` (confirms this scene does **not** wrongly show the character picker — it isn't a zone-start room), exactly 2 `pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller(Clone)` alive at once (confirms the `maxConcurrentEnemies: 2` cap and spawn-point refill logic work against the new data), `SaveSystem` logged a successful load+save cycle, and the pickup/workbench/cardboard-pile/env-prop set (24 objects under `[Spawned]`) matches the new drop table exactly. Did not observe the SkepticGrunt or third roller spawn in-session (concurrency cap only seeds 2 initially — the remaining 2 arrive as refills after a kill, not observed in this short automated pass). Cleaned up fully before the final save — confirmed zero `(Clone)` residue in the saved `.unity` file via `grep`, and confirmed `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` and the old `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.unity` are both byte-unchanged by this task via `git status`/`git diff`.

**Not done, flagged for the owner's actual playtest (this is what the "awaiting owner playtest" status means):** the automated pass above could not observe the SkepticGrunt's wind-up telegraph/health-bar readability live, could not confirm the `heightOffset: 2.8f`/`_offset.y: 2.3` values *look* right (only that they're the same formula that worked for WagonWheelRoller — B56's own numbers were owner-confirmed "That is perfect" after a live look, this pass's SkepticGrunt numbers are not), and could not confirm the reused Western dressing doesn't read as an obvious copy of Room 1 to a human eye. Manual test checklist: enter `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` directly (or via Room 1's random-room advance once wired into a real run), confirm the picker does NOT appear, fight through all 4 enemies (kill 2, confirm the SkepticGrunt/third roller refill in), specifically look at the SkepticGrunt's health bar and wind-up telegraph height, forge a weapon at the workbench, confirm the 5 pickups (Broomstick/FoamSword/CardboardTube/GardenTrowel/JumpRope, fixed by code review — see above) actually feel new coming out of Room 1, and sanity-check the dressing doesn't feel like reused Room-1 set dressing. **Note:** entering this scene directly (rather than clearing it via a real Room-1 run) leaves the shuffled random-room queue empty, so clearing it will load `CulDeSac_BossArena` next rather than another random room — that's correct by design (`GameManager.InitRoomQueue` only builds the queue from a real zone-start room), not a bug, but worth knowing before it looks like one mid-playtest.

Not touched (out of scope / explicitly protected): `CulDeSac_SaloonFront.unity`, `CulDeSac_MailboxRow.unity` (next rooms, not this one), `WeaponGripTest.unity`, any gun weapon or `WeaponData` grip correction (owner-paused), the `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` B68 fix from earlier this session (confirmed byte-unchanged).

**Performance-review addendum (2026-08-24):** SkepticGrunt is the first *animated, skinned* enemy in the rebuilt Cul-de-Sac (WagonWheelRoller has no Animator at all), so Room 1's performance approval gives it zero coverage — flagged as the one real open risk, not something already cleared. Two low-risk, high-confidence fixes applied immediately based on the review: `pfb_enemy_skeptic_grunt.prefab`'s `Animator.m_ApplyRootMotion` was `1` (movement is entirely `NavMeshAgent`-driven and the FBX imports with `importAnimation: 0` — root motion here was pure waste, and SkepticGrunt was the *only* animated enemy prefab in the project with it enabled; every other one is `0`) — set to `0`. `SkepticGrunt.fbx`'s `isReadable` was `1` (keeps a full CPU-side mesh copy for a 27-bone skinned mesh; `WagonWheelRoller.fbx` is correctly `0`) — set to `0`. Both verified via `read_console` post-reimport: no new errors/warnings.

**Code-review addendum (2026-08-24) — real defect found and fixed, plus three small cleanups:**
- **Real defect, fixed:** the new `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.asset` was left pointing at the *exact same* pickups as `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset` (JumpRope, GardenTrowel, BikeHorn) — B69's stated "ported from the old room" methodology was applied to the enemies but never actually applied to the loot. Since `GameManager.LoadNextRoom` carries the player's loadout into the next room, this meant Room 2 offered the player nothing they didn't already have. Fixed: swapped in the OLD `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.unity`'s actual distinct pickup set (Broomstick, FoamSword, CardboardTube scattered + GardenTrowel, JumpRope loot-zone — read from its untouched `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.asset`), positions adapted to the reused arena bounds.
- `SkepticGruntAI.cs`'s telegraph height was a bare literal (`2.8f`) in a class where every other tunable is a `[SerializeField]` — the one place B69 itself flagged as "not owner-verified, may need adjusting" was the one value that would've required a code edit + recompile to adjust. Promoted to `[SerializeField] private float telegraphHeightOffset = 2.8f`.
- `RoomData_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.asset`'s `roomName` was `AmbushAlley` (old scene's internal string) vs. Room 1's display-style `The Arrival` — changed to `Ambush Alley` for consistency in case it's ever surfaced.
- A cosmetic column-alignment nit in `GameManager.cs`'s `ZoneIndexByScene` literal (the new entry broke the existing alignment) — realigned.
- **Reviewed and left as-is:** the SkepticGrunt spawning strictly last (`RoomManager.TrySpawnNext` walks spawn points in array order, no reshuffling) initially looked like an authoring accident, but the OLD `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.unity`'s own spawn-point order was checked and is identical — 3 rollers then the grunt last. This room's pacing (a "coda" enemy) faithfully reproduces the original design rather than introducing a new one.
- **Resolved 2026-08-24 — no longer flagged:** the reused-arena-shape-and-coordinates issue above is superseded by the footprint redesign addendum further up this entry. The owner gave a direct answer ("I don't want any of the levels to appear as a copy of another level") rather than leaving it open — the room now has its own distinct corridor-into-pocket shape and entirely repositioned dressing.
- **Left alone, noted rather than reverted:** the stray TextMeshPro fallback-font atlas change (one auto-generated glyph from Play Mode testing, `Assets/TextMesh Pro/Resources/Fonts & Materials/LiberationSans SDF - Fallback.asset`) is harmless dynamic-atlas regeneration and has churned before — reverting it would just have it regenerate again next time that character renders. Named here explicitly rather than silently included in the commit.
- Updated the manual playtest checklist below with a room-queue caveat the review caught: entering this scene directly (not via a real Room-1 run) leaves `GameManager`'s shuffled room queue empty, so clearing it will route to `CulDeSac_BossArena` next, not another random room — correct by design (`GameManager.cs` only builds the shuffled queue from an actual zone-start room), but worth knowing going in so it doesn't read as a bug.

**Not applied, left as follow-up (real but needs care, not a quick edit):** `optimizeGameObjects: 0` on the same FBX — the biggest remaining CPU win, but this project uses hand-tuned bone-socket weapon grips, and enabling it strips bone transforms unless explicitly exposed first; needs verification that nothing depends on SkepticGrunt's hand bones before flipping it. Static batching for env props is configured (`BatchingStatic` flag set) but never actually applied, since `LevelBuilder` instantiates them at runtime and static batching is build-time-only for scene objects — SRP Batcher still applies (all env props are single-material, GPU-instancing-enabled, same URP Lit shader), so draw-call count itself isn't reduced by the existing flag; `StaticBatchingUtility.Combine` on an env-prop-only subroot is the available lever if a real device profile shows draw calls are the bottleneck. WagonWheelRoller's own textures (3 textures, `maxTextureSize: 2048` on every platform, `overridden: 0` — no per-platform reduction at all, plus `sRGBTexture: 1` wrongly set on its Metallic map) are pre-existing, already-approved-Room-1 debt, not new to this room — belongs under B1 (texture import policy), not fixed here. Recommend a real on-device profiling pass on *this* room specifically (not Room 1) once playtested, since it's the first to carry an animated/skinned enemy at all — per `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` §3.7, a minute-1-vs-minute-12 thermal comparison.

**Tooling gotcha caught, but the on-call diagnosis/fix was itself wrong — corrected during the coordinating session's verification pass, 2026-08-24:** registering the new scene in Build Settings via the Unity MCP `manage_build` tool's `scenes` action, passed the full existing scene-path list plus the one new path (no "add a single scene" mode was found on this tool version — only a full-list set). This pass believed the call corrupted 4 unrelated scenes' GUIDs and "restored" them by hand — but verification against `git log`/each scene's own `.meta` file found the restore landed on the **wrong** value for `CulDeSac_Room1`: it wrote back `fd664346dde034b208e022ed9e6c8727`, which matches no `.meta` file anywhere in the project, while `CulDeSac_Room1.unity.meta`'s real, current guid (`7b8170878b7604bfd920755e967f2d76` — confirmed via `git log`, present since commit `372e5bbc`, long before this session) is the value this pass mistook for the corruption and reverted away from. In other words: `CulDeSac_Room1`'s Build Settings entry has been pointing at a guid matching no actual asset since at least commit `9694accc` (already pushed) — a genuine pre-existing bug, unrelated to this task, that this pass stumbled into and then (with good intentions) made no better. Corrected directly in `EditorBuildSettings.asset` to the real guid. `LoadingScreen.unity`/`TownSquare_Room1.unity`/`TownSquare_BossHall.unity` reference scenes that don't exist on disk yet (Phase 3/future content) — their guids couldn't be independently verified against a `.meta` file and were left untouched; low risk since nothing loads them today. **Recommendation stands regardless of the misdiagnosis: don't use `manage_build action=scenes` with a full path list to add a single scene — hand-edit `EditorBuildSettings.asset` directly (plain YAML, safe to edit like any other `-text` asset) and append one entry, and verify any resulting GUID against each scene's own `.meta` file (not just "does git diff show a change") before trusting it.**

### B70. `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` rebuilt to match the authoritative V4 blueprint (`docs/v4/levels/World1/cul-de-sac-room1/unity-blueprint.md`) — **DONE 2026-08-24, awaiting owner playtest**

Owner rejected the room as it stood: not reachable in all corners of the visible ground, duplicate buildings placed oddly, and — critically — didn't match a real reference design they'd already produced (`docs/v4/levels/World1/cul-de-sac-room1/unity-blueprint.md` + `gdd.md`, plus a matching infographic for Ambush Alley). The room previously had the right-sized Ground/boundary box but essentially no street dressing (14 scattered clutter props, no buildings or wagons at all) and enemy spawn points that didn't match the blueprint. This is a build-to-spec rebuild, not a redesign — every position below is the blueprint's own stated coordinate except where noted.

**Geometry reset to the blueprint's own absolute coordinates** (previous scene's coordinate origin didn't reconcile cleanly with the blueprint's stated spawn/gate positions): `Ground` → position `(0,0,0)`, scale `(4,1,3)` = 40m×30m, spanning X[-20,20] Z[-15,15]. Four `Boundary_*` colliders moved to X=±21 / Z=±15 (matching the blueprint's stated wall positions — deliberately a hair outside the visible ground edge, not inside it, so nothing visible is ever unreachable). `pfb_player` moved to `(0,0,-10)`.

**RoomData_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset:** `maxConcurrentEnemies` 2→3 (blueprint's 3-enemy variant, already the shipped configuration per project memory). Three `WagonWheelRoller` spawns at the blueprint's stated `(-6,0,+2)`/`(+6,0,+2)`/`(0,0,+5)` — **adjusted the two flank spawns to `(-6,0,0)`/`(6,0,0)` after live-testing found the blueprint's literal Z=+2 sits inside the footprint of the Covered Wagon placed right next to it** (`pfb_env_covered_wagon`'s MeshCollider is 4.74m×2.5m — confirmed via a live `Instantiate` + bounds check, not assumed), making that exact point off-NavMesh (`NavMesh.CalculatePath` returned `PathInvalid`). Moved 2m south into the gap between the two wagon pairs — same flank, same "near the wagon" intent, now actually reachable. Re-verified `PathComplete` after the move.

**WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset — full rebuild to blueprint:** 3 weapon pickups (Broomstick/Ruler/CardboardTube, the blueprint's recommended starting set) at `(-5,0,-5)`/`(0,0,-2)`/`(5,0,-5)`; 2 cardboard piles at `(±3,0,2)`; workbench at `(0,0,-12)`; `lootZoneObjects` emptied (blueprint specifies no loot zone for Room 1, only scattered pickups). `envProps` rebuilt from 1 stray Saloon Facade to the full blueprint set: 4 Saloon Facades at the blueprint's exact 4 positions (`(±12,0,+6)`/`(±12,0,-2)`, correct Y rotations) — **this repetition (2 facades per side) is the actual design, not a duplication bug**, it's what makes the street read as a row of buildings; 2 saloon sign boards (on facades A/C, elevated); 3 wanted posters (facades A/B/C); 4 Covered Wagons at the blueprint's exact positions/rotations; 4 hitching posts (one per wagon); 2 lamp posts flanking the north end; 3 rain barrels; 2 mailbox/telegraph posts; 4 tumbleweeds scattered; 1 water trough; 1 gallows frame (background, far NE, per blueprint).

**Deviated from the blueprint, both minor, both flagged rather than silently invented:**
- Lamp posts, rain barrels, mailboxes, tumbleweeds, water trough, sign-board height, and wanted-poster offsets are NOT individually tabulated in the blueprint (only counts + a rough ASCII layout) — placed them at reasonable positions consistent with the ASCII diagram and with Ambush Alley's own blueprint's analogous street-furniture conventions. Worth an owner pass to nudge exact spots once seen in-game, but the counts and general zones match spec.
- The blueprint's `[Polyworks Props]` (rocks, cacti, bushes, fences, junk boxes — ~50 individual pieces) and `[Craftsmanship Dressing]` (GDD §9, undefined) reference asset names that **do not exist anywhere in the project** (confirmed via full-repo search). Skipped entirely rather than substituting something unasked. Real content gap — would need new Meshy generation or a scope decision to drop them from the spec.
- The blueprint's `aggroDelay = 3f` "three seconds of pure atmosphere before enemies aggro" beat has no corresponding field anywhere in the current codebase (`RoomManager`, `RoomDataSO`, `LevelBuilder` — confirmed via full search, zero matches for `aggroDelay`). This is a real, never-implemented mechanic from the original V4 design, not something this pass could wire up as a geometry/data change — flagging as a gap for a future production task, not silently dropping it from the record.

**Correction, 2026-08-24, same day — the "4 facades is the actual design" claim above was wrong:** the owner produced an updated, corrected infographic (`raw-assets/images/CulDeSac.png`) showing the real intended layout: **2 Saloon Facades total** (one west, one east, both at `z=0`, "faces street"), not 4. The original text blueprint's 4-position table was apparently superseded by this later design pass and never reconciled back into the `.md` file. Fixed directly: removed the 2 extra facade entries, moved the remaining 2 to `(-12,0,0)`/`(12,0,0)` (matching the corrected image exactly), moved both sign boards to sit above the same consolidated positions, and redistributed the 3 wanted posters across the 2 buildings' sides (2 on west, 1 on east) instead of 3-of-4 facades. Verified via `read_console` (no new errors) and a live screenshot after moving the player mid-room — confirmed one saloon reads per flank, not two overlapping ones. Wagons/hitching posts/barrels/mailboxes/lamp posts/tumbleweeds/water trough/gallows frame were untouched — the corrected image's counts for a couple of those (hitching posts: 2 vs the 4 currently placed; tumbleweeds: 3 vs 4) don't fully match what's built either, but the owner's stated complaint was specifically about the saloons, so those were left as-is rather than reinterpreted unasked — worth an explicit owner call if those should also be trimmed.

**Second correction, same day:** owner asked to drop the saloon sign boards entirely and use a different building for variety instead of two identical saloon facades. Removed both `pfb_env_saloon_sign_board` entries. Swapped the east facade (`(12,0,0)`) from `pfb_env_saloon_facade` to `pfb_env_bank` (same guid already proven camera-clearance-safe in the Ambush Alley pass), same position/rotation — west side keeps the saloon (matches the room's "SALOON" identity from the reference image and the GDD's saloon theme). Wanted posters (a separate prop category, not literally "signs") were left alone. Verified: clean recompile, live screenshot confirms no floating signage on either building, `NavMesh.CalculatePath` still `PathComplete` to both buildings and the workbench after the swap.

**Validated for real, not asserted:**
- `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance` (ADR-0001 §2.7): 0 of 511 NavMesh vertices violate the ≥6m top-ray clearance. **15 of 511 (~2.9%) read <8m clear-behind** — traced this to the street's own building/wagon spacing (opposing rows placed 6–8m apart, exactly per blueprint, to read as a dense Main Street) rather than to the boundary or any single misplaced prop. This specific check is explicitly logged as "diagnostic only, does not block Play Mode," and a two-sided building-lined street inherently produces some tight gaps between opposing cover — concluded this is an expected, minor trade-off of matching the reference image rather than a defect, but flagging the exact number rather than asserting a clean pass. If the owner wants this at zero, the fix is widening the gap between opposing wagon/facade pairs, which trades off against street density.
- `NavMesh.CalculatePath` confirmed `PathComplete` for the workbench, all 3 pickups, both cardboard piles, all 3 (corrected) enemy spawns, near-gate, and all 4 room corners.
- 40 objects present under `[Spawned]` after a Play Mode pass, matching the full pickup/pile/workbench/prop/enemy count with nothing missing or duplicated.
- Recompiled clean, zero new errors/warnings. Confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` that `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.unity` and the old reference `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.unity` are both untouched by this pass, and no leaked Play Mode `(Clone)` state was saved into `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity`.
- **Environmental note, not a defect in this work:** this session's Unity MCP bridge dropped Play Mode silently and repeatedly mid-check (`Application.isPlaying` flipping to `false` with no console error) — already catalogued in memory as a known instability. Every validation above was re-confirmed live (checked `Application.isPlaying`/`Time.frameCount` immediately before trusting a result) after at least one silent drop, not taken from a single pass.

**Not done here, flagged for the owner's actual playtest:** whether the rebuilt street *looks* right is inherently a human call — specifically whether the two Saloon Facade pairs read as "a row of buildings" rather than "the same building pasted twice" from actual in-game camera angles, whether the un-tabulated street-furniture placements (lamp posts, barrels, etc.) look reasonable, and whether reachability now genuinely covers the whole visible ground with no ground-visible-but-unreachable strip anywhere (structurally this should be fixed by construction — walls now sit outside, not inside, the visible ground edge — but wasn't walked corner-to-corner by a human).

Not touched (explicitly out of scope): `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.unity`, `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley.unity`, `WeaponGripTest.unity`, any gun weapon/`WeaponData` grip correction. Lighting was checked and left mostly alone (Sun rotation/intensity were already correct per blueprint; Sun color was white instead of the blueprint's warm `#FFB347` amber — fixed. `VolumeProfile_CulDeSac`'s Color Adjustments override from the blueprint's post-process spec — Saturation +15, Color Filter `#FFF0D8` — was found completely absent (`components: - {fileID: 0}`, i.e. never actually authored despite being in the blueprint since 2026-08-05); attempted to add it via `manage_graphics volume_add_effect` but the tool's `profile_path` targeting didn't behave as documented (added the effect to the wrong Volume, and the change didn't persist to either profile asset on disk after a project save) — left unresolved rather than fought further, flagging as a tool-behavior gap worth a fresh attempt via direct asset editing rather than the graphics tool.

Not committed, per this project's approval rule.

### B71. Real root cause of the recurring "invisible wall / can't reach the forge bench" reports — a hardcoded 8.5m circular movement clamp on `pfb_player`, unrelated to any collider — **FIXED for `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` 2026-08-24, same bug still latent everywhere else**

Owner reported "can't reach the forge bench" three separate times across this session, each time after a fix attempt appeared to resolve it. Every physics-level check (`NavMesh.CalculatePath`, and eventually direct step-by-step `CharacterController.Move()` simulation from two different starting points, both reaching within 0.5m of the workbench with zero blocking `CollisionFlags`) came back clean — because none of those checks exercise the actual bug. The real cause isn't a collider at all.

**`PlayerController.cs`'s `ClampToArenaBounds()`** (called every frame) force-overwrites `transform.position` to stay within `_arenaBoundaryRadius` of `_arenaCenter` — a plain position teleport-back, not a physics collision, which is exactly why `CharacterController.Move()` simulations never showed a blocked flag. `pfb_player.prefab`'s serialized default is `_arenaBoundaryRadius: 8.5`, `_arenaCenter: (0,0,0)` (already correct history per B8/B27 — tuned deliberately small so a fight always stays inside the fixed camera's ~16-17m visible width, see ADR-0001). That default was never revisited when Room 1 was rebuilt to the real blueprint's much larger 40m×30m footprint: the blueprint's own spawn `(0,0,-10)`, workbench `(0,0,-12)`, and exit gate `(0,0,+12)` are all **outside an 8.5m radius circle centered on world origin** — meaning the player has been getting invisibly teleported back toward the room's center every single frame they try to walk south of roughly `z=-8.3`, since before this session's rebuild even started. This is a pre-existing latent bug that the old sparse Room 1 box never surfaced (its old enemy/prop placements likely never asked the player to go far enough from origin to hit it) — the blueprint rebuild is what exposed it.

**Fix applied:** set a scene-level override on `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity`'s `pfb_player` instance, `_arenaBoundaryRadius: 8.5 → 20` (via `manage_components set_property`, verified via a live component read, scene saved). `_arenaCenter` left at `(0,0,0)`, which is already the room's real geometric center (`Ground` spans X[-20,20] Z[-15,15]). 20m comfortably covers every gameplay-relevant point (workbench/gate at 12m, buildings/wagons at ≤14m) with margin, while still falling short of the room's literal corners (~25m) — judged fine since nothing gameplay-relevant sits there. Not verified with a live human playtest yet (owner asked to test in an uninterrupted session next) — verified by direct math/property-read only: every named POI's distance from origin is now less than the new radius, so `ClampToArenaBounds()` cannot fire for any of them.

**This is not just a Room 1 bug — flagging clearly rather than letting it resurface as a new mystery:** `pfb_player.prefab`'s shared default (8.5m) still applies to every other scene, including `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.unity`, which has no per-scene override either and has its own points well outside 8.5m of origin (its rebuilt corridor-into-pocket shape spans well past that on the Z axis). The real fix is architectural — this radius should be driven by each room's actual `RoomDataSO`/geometry (similar to how `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance` is already per-scene-configured via `_cameraYawDegrees`), not a single hardcoded default nobody revisits per room. Filed here rather than silently patched room-by-room forever; the per-scene override above is a real, working fix for Room 1 specifically, not a workaround to be dismissed.

**Third correction, same day — two real content defects found from live screenshots:**
- **The "bank" building is a genuine scale mismatch, not a placement issue.** Measured both prefabs' actual renderer bounds: `pfb_env_saloon_facade` is 7.60×7.18×4.43m; `pfb_env_bank` is only 1.90×1.89×1.34m — roughly 4x too small, reading as a toy-sized building next to the saloon and wagons. Fixed by setting its `envProps` `localScale` to `(4,4,4)` (7.6/1.9 = 4.0 exactly), bringing it to 7.6×7.56×5.36m — closely matching the saloon. Not re-imported/recalibrated at the source (that's a real asset-pipeline task, not a scene-data fix) — flagged as a proper follow-up.
- **All 3 wanted-poster entries removed per owner instruction** ("not in the correct places, just remove them" + a screenshot showing one floating detached in mid-air with no visible support). Zero wanted posters remain in `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset`.
- **Owner asked for more building variety ("I think I need more meshy prompts for buildings").** Found `docs/v4/levels/World1/cul-de-sac-buildings-meshy-prompts.md` already exists, dated 2026-08-05, status "Ready for generation" — 6 fully-written, under-800-character Meshy prompts (Sheriff's Office, Bank, General Store, Blacksmith Forge, Barber Shop, Stables) with a shared palette/style/poly-budget spec, never actually generated. Notably the doc's own "existing buildings, do not duplicate" list does NOT include `pfb_env_bank` — meaning the bank asset currently in the project was created some other way after this doc was written, likely explaining its uncalibrated scale. Surfaced to the owner rather than silently kicking off Meshy generation (real external-API cost/time, belongs to the `asset-pipeline` workflow with owner sign-off) — awaiting a decision on whether to generate some or all of the 6 already-prompted buildings.

**Separately, still unresolved:** the owner's other report from the same clean-session retest — no visible health loss when hit by an enemy — was investigated in depth (traced the full `PlayerStats.TakeDamage` → `CombatController.TryReceiveAttack` → `HealthBar3D` pipeline, confirmed every step works correctly via direct scripted calls in an isolated test) but not yet reproduced with a *real* enemy-driven hit (only a direct `TryReceiveAttack` method call, which bypasses whatever WagonWheelRollerAI's actual charge/hit-detection does). Given the arena-boundary bug just found was severe enough to make the player's own position fight itself every frame near spawn, it's plausible — not yet confirmed — that the same clamp was destabilizing combat state too. Needs a real AI-driven hit test now that the boundary is fixed, before concluding whether it's a separate bug.

### B72. Ground material was the raw URP default (flat grey, no texture) — never actually authored — **FIXED 2026-08-24**

`CulDeSac_Room1_v2`'s `Ground` renderer was assigned `Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.universal/Runtime/Materials/Lit.mat` — the literal package-default material, `_BaseColor` 0.5 grey, zero texture. Not a regression, just never replaced. The project already had a purpose-named `Assets/_Project/Materials/MAT_Ground.mat` sitting unused (only referenced by `ForgeLoop_Test.unity`, itself untextured) — clearly the intended asset, just never finished. Owner supplied a seamless hand-drawn dirt texture (`raw-assets/images/Western-dirt-texture-seamless.png`, matching the cardboard/marker art style and the GDD's "cracked terracotta, dirt patches" description). Imported to `Assets/_Project/Materials/Tex_WesternDirt_Ground.png` (1024×1024, wrap Repeat), applied to `MAT_Ground.mat`'s `_BaseMap` with an 8×8 tiling scale, and assigned that material to Room 1's `Ground` renderer. Verified via live property read (correct texture bound, correct scale) — visual confirmation only partial (screenshots at this camera distance don't clearly show tiling detail; color read as intended warm terracotta rather than flat grey). Same fix should be applied to Ambush Alley and every future room once each has its own `Ground` renderer.

### B73. New building batch (B70/asset-engineer pass) had colliders ~100x oversized on all 6 — caught before any placement, **FIXED 2026-08-24**

Before placing any of the 6 newly-generated buildings (see prior asset-engineer task), ran a live `Physics.OverlapBox` test using each prefab's actual collider bounds as a pre-placement sanity check — a practice adopted after this session's repeated "trust the physics, not the self-report" lessons. Found every one of the 6 (`pfb_env_bld_sheriffs_office/bank/general_store/blacksmith_forge/barber_shop/stables`) had a `BoxCollider.m_Size` matching the correct real-world dimensions (e.g. Sheriff's Office `6.086×3.203×4.186`) sitting on a prefab root with `m_LocalScale: (100,100,100)` — meaning the actual physics collider was ~100x too large in world space (608×320×418m for a 6m building), while the visual mesh rendered correctly at the intended size. The asset-engineer's own bounds check (`Renderer.bounds`) never caught this because renderer bounds already account for the scale correctly — only the collider was wrong, and only a live physics overlap test surfaces it.

**Root cause, not yet fixed at the source:** the raw Meshy FBX exports for this batch are ~100x smaller than intended (a classic cm-vs-m unit mismatch) and something in the import/prefab-build step scaled the *root transform* up 100x to compensate, rather than fixing the import scale factor — exactly the "scale workaround to repair a scale problem" pattern the `asset-pipeline` skill's hard rules warn against. The collider was then authored against the *correct* real-world target size without accounting for that compensating parent scale.

**Fix applied (pragmatic, not the root fix):** divided each of the 6 prefabs' `BoxCollider.m_Size`/`m_Center` by 100, verified via live `Physics.OverlapBox`/`Renderer.bounds` comparison post-fix that collider and visual bounds now match exactly for all 6. **Proper fix, not done here:** normalize each prefab's import scale so the root transform is `(1,1,1)` per the asset-pipeline skill's own stated preference — this pragmatic fix unblocks placement today but the underlying 100x-scaled root remains and could bite again if anyone adds a second collider or a script that assumes scale=1.

### B74. 3 of the 6 new buildings placed in `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`; 3 held back — room genuinely doesn't have clean space for all 6 — **DONE 2026-08-24**

Owner asked to place the new buildings in Room 1 "to help fill it out." Placed, each verified overlap-free via live `Physics.OverlapBox` against the *actual* spawned instances (not just isolated test copies): swapped the undersized placeholder `pfb_env_bank` at `(12,0,0)` for the properly-scaled `pfb_env_bld_bank` (same position/rotation, `localScale` back to `1,1,1` — B71.3's ×4 workaround is no longer needed); added `pfb_env_bld_sheriffs_office` at `(-12,0,-6.5)` rot Y=90 and `pfb_env_bld_blacksmith_forge` at `(12,0,-6.5)` rot Y=270 — both in the one genuinely open gap in the room (between the wagon cluster at z≈±3/-4 and the south boundary/barrel at z=-11).

**Not placed — General Store, Stables, Barber Shop.** Tried multiple candidate layouts (a "second row" further out in X, extending the row further along Z) — every one produced a real, physics-confirmed overlap with either existing wagons/clutter, the other new buildings, or the boundary walls. Room 1's existing prop density (4 wagons, hitching posts, barrels, mailbox, lamp posts, tumbleweeds, water trough, gallows frame — all already placed) plus its 40×30m footprint doesn't have room left for 3 more building-scale objects without moving or removing existing dressing, which wasn't asked for. Recommending these 3 go to Saloon Front / Mailbox Row instead once those rooms are built to their own blueprints — genuinely useful there rather than crammed in here, and consistent with the standing "no room may reuse another's layout" rule (`feedback-distinct-level-geometry`) since those rooms need their own distinct building mix anyway.

**Process note, worth remembering:** mid-task, live-tested candidate placements by instantiating real prefab copies directly in the shared Play Mode session, then calling `GameObject.Destroy()` between iterations. `Destroy()` defers to end-of-frame in Play Mode, so rapid successive test calls left multiple leftover clones visibly stacking up in the owner's own live view before cleanup — genuinely confusing, correctly flagged by the owner as "buildings overlapping/not oriented correctly" when it was actually test debris, not a real defect. Cleaned up via `DestroyImmediate` (works even in Play Mode) and confirmed zero clones remained. For any future live placement testing: either test in a position far below/outside the visible playspace, or accept the one-test-at-a-time cost of `DestroyImmediate` from the start rather than batching multiple candidates per Play Mode entry.

### B75. `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`'s entire static layout rotated 35° to align with the camera's real yaw — **DONE 2026-08-24**

Owner: the room looked "off" relative to the camera and asked for the buildings/props to sit "on more of an angle to match the camera angle," confirming via a reference screenshot (isometric arena footage, ground/boundary reading as a clean diagonal/diamond shape relative to the screen) that they wanted a rotated-layout look, not a camera change. Scoped to content-only (owner's own choice, offered as an option): keep the room's already-validated ground/boundary shape and gameplay layout, just rotate everything together as one rigid body — not a redesign.

**Root context, already established this session, not re-derived:** the camera rig has a real, pre-existing 35° yaw (`LevelBuilder._cameraYawDegrees: 35`, confirmed matching the actual `pfb_CM_FollowCam` rig, predates this session — a 2026-08-20 "diagonal/isometric framing" decision that ADR-0001's own text was never updated to reflect, since the ADR's prose still says "yaw = 0°, deliberately"). The camera itself was untouched here — only room content was rotated to visually align with that pre-existing fixed yaw.

**Transform applied:** rigid rotation of +35° around Y, pivot at world origin `(0,0,0)` (already the room's own geometric center). Every position `(x,z)` mapped via `x' = x·cos(35°) + z·sin(35°)`, `z' = -x·sin(35°) + z·cos(35°)` (Unity's standard Y-rotation convention, verified against `Quaternion.Euler(0,90,0)*Vector3.forward = Vector3.right`), and 35° added to every object's own Y-rotation/facing value. Sign chosen empirically, not assumed: rendered the actual gameplay camera view after applying +35° (character-selected via `RunStartUI`, screenshotted via `manage_camera`) and confirmed the Blacksmith Forge's roofline and the ground/boundary edge now read as clean diagonals against the screen frame, matching the reference composition's direction — a qualitative visual read, not a pixel-perfect match against the reference, but a clear, correct-direction improvement over the pre-rotation "flat" composition. −35° was not additionally tested given how unambiguous the +35° result was.

**Files touched, everything moved together:**
- `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity`: `Ground` (rotation only, already at pivot), all 4 `Boundary_*` transforms (position + rotation), `pfb_player`'s spawn position + rotation (new spawn `(-5.736, 0, -8.192)`, facing rotY 35°). No `RoomGate_Exit` object exists in this scene (checked first, per instruction — skipped, nothing to rotate there). `_arenaBoundaryRadius`/`_arenaCenter` on `pfb_player` untouched — center `(0,0,0)` is still correct since the pivot didn't move.
- `RoomData_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset`: all 3 `WagonWheelRoller` spawn points, position + `facingY` (180→215).
- `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset`: every `scatteredObjects`/`cardboardPiles`/`workbenchPositions`/`envProps` entry (25 env props, position + `eulerRotation.y` where applicable).
- Camera rig, `_cameraYawDegrees`, and all script/gameplay logic deliberately untouched.

**Re-validated live, not assumed:**
- Reachability: `NavMesh.CalculatePath` from the new rotated spawn to the workbench, all 3 scattered pickups, both cardboard piles, and all 3 enemy spawns — `PathComplete` on all 9, confirmed post-rotation.
- Camera clearance: `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance` (diagnostic-only, doesn't block Play Mode) now reports 52/469 NavMesh vertices under the 8m "clear behind" diagnostic threshold, versus 15/511 before rotation — a real, quantifiable increase (top clearance unaffected: 0/469 both before and after). Expected and explainable: that check raycasts along the camera's *fixed world-space* yaw direction, and rotating the room's content changes each point's relationship to that fixed direction non-trivially, even though the room's internal relative geometry is unchanged. Since it's diagnostic-only per the code's own log message, not treated as a blocker, but flagged with real numbers rather than glossed over.
- Overlaps: **a real methodology problem was found and worked around, not a room defect.** `Physics.OverlapBox` using `Collider.bounds.extents` combined with a rotation parameter double-counts a rotated box's AABB inflation, producing false positives at any non-90°-multiple rotation — confirmed by re-running the *original pre-rotation* Bank/Blacksmith Forge placement (already visually verified clean earlier this session) through the same test method and getting the same false "overlap," plus false overlaps against a rain barrel/gallows frame/hitching post/wagon that were never remotely close. Corrected the query to use the `BoxCollider`'s actual local `size`/`center` scaled by `lossyScale` instead of `bounds.extents`, but the corrected method *still* flagged Bank-vs-Blacksmith-Forge as overlapping on the known-clean original placement — pointing at a second, more fundamental issue: **these prefabs' 100x-parent-scale + 100x-divided-down local collider size (the pragmatic fix from B73) appears to make `Physics.OverlapBox` queries unreliable at that scale ratio**, independent of rotation. Given a rigid rotation is a mathematical isometry (preserves every pairwise distance and relative angle), and the pre-rotation layout was already confirmed non-overlapping by direct screenshot inspection earlier this session, the post-rotation layout is provably still non-overlapping — verified instead by direct visual screenshot inspection of the rotated result (no clipping visible on Blacksmith Forge, Saloon, or Bank from the angles checked) rather than trusting the physics query. **Recommend B73's "proper fix" (normalize prefab import scale to 1) get done for real** — this is now two separate observed side effects (a prior console-only concern, now a concrete reproducible query failure) of leaving that workaround in place.

**Not done / flagged:** did not re-test at −35° given the +35° visual result was unambiguous, so no formal side-by-side comparison exists — if the owner's own eyes disagree once they see it live, that's the first thing to revisit. `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.unity` was explicitly out of scope for this pass (per instruction) and still has the pre-rotation, axis-aligned layout — same treatment should follow once the owner confirms Room 1's result is correct. Not committed, per this project's approval rule.

**Verification correction, same day (coordinating session, independent recheck):** re-ran the Bank-vs-Blacksmith-Forge overlap question with a proper 2D oriented-rectangle separating-axis test (exact rotated-rectangle corners, not a bounding-box approximation) using each building's real half-extents and their actual post-rotation position/rotation. Result: **no overlap, definitively** (`SAT overlap=False`). Both `Physics.OverlapBox` and a plain AABB-intersection check (also independently re-verified, also a false positive) are known to over-report on rotated content — an inflated axis-aligned bounding box can intersect another inflated box even when the true oriented rectangles don't touch, which is a generic limitation of loose bounding-volume tests at non-90°-multiple rotations, not necessarily specific to B73's 100x-scale workaround as the prior pass guessed. B73's underlying import-scale defect is still real and still worth fixing properly, but it is not confirmed as the cause of this specific false positive — the false positive would very plausibly occur on correctly-scaled prefabs too, at this rotation angle. Also independently confirmed via live `NavMesh.CalculatePath` (all 9 points `PathComplete`) and a live gameplay screenshot post-rotation: reachability and visual composition both check out.

### B76. Owner hand-tuned Room 1's building/prop placement live in Play Mode; positions captured and persisted — **DONE 2026-08-24**

Owner reported the post-rotation building facing still "going in the same direction as before" and proposed the right fix given how many rounds of computed-rotation guessing this had already taken: adjust live in Play Mode by hand, then have the coordinating session read the final transforms back into the data before exiting (since these are `LevelBuilder`-spawned runtime clones — they don't exist at all in Edit Mode and vanish the instant Play Mode stops, so nothing persists unless captured first). Same pattern already proven for weapon-grip tuning earlier in the project.

**Blocker hit and fixed along the way:** the 3 buildings (and, once the owner asked to adjust more, all 22 other static env-prop clones) couldn't be moved in the Scene view — traced to `isStatic: true` on the spawned instances (matches their prefab's `StaticEditorFlags.BatchingStatic`), which can visually freeze a static-batched renderer even while the Transform itself is being dragged. Fixed live, both times, via `gameObject.isStatic = false` on the affected instances (recursively, for the second pass) — cosmetic/runtime-only, not written back to any prefab or data asset, since it only needs to be true during this one hands-on session.

**Captured and written to `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset`** (position/rotation/scale, matched to the correct `envProps` array entry via a live `AssetDatabase.LoadAssetAtPath` read — see the guid-swap catch below for why array-order alone wasn't trusted):
- `pfb_env_bld_sheriffs_office`: repositioned `(-13.559,1.559)→(-15.760,-0.150)`, scaled up `100→122.98` (rotation unchanged at 125°).
- `pfb_env_bld_blacksmith_forge`: repositioned `(6.103,-12.208)→(5.020,-13.750)` (rotation/scale unchanged).
- 3 of 4 `pfb_env_covered_wagon` repositioned and/or re-rotated (one moved+rotated to 200.6°, one moved+rotated to 253°, one repositioned only); 1 unchanged.
- 1 of 4 `pfb_env_hitching_post` re-rotated (35°→126.1°); others unchanged.
- 1 of 2 `pfb_env_lamp_post_western` repositioned slightly; other unchanged.
- Both `pfb_env_mailbox_telegraph` scaled up `1→1.51`.
- `pfb_env_water_trough` scaled down `1.7778→1.18`.
- `pfb_env_gallows_frame` (already scaled once this session to fix an unrelated "too small" report) further adjusted by the owner to a final `1.6603` scale and re-rotated `235°→357°`.
- `pfb_env_bld_bank` and `pfb_env_saloon_facade`: touched/inspected but left at their post-rotation values — no change.
- **Deliberately excluded: all 4 `pfb_env_tumbleweed_static`.** Their captured positions differed wildly from spawn (up to ~12m) with non-zero Y values, consistent with ordinary physics settling/rolling on a prop literally named "tumbleweed_static" rather than deliberate placement — writing a random mid-roll snapshot into the data would be actively wrong. Left at their pre-existing rotated values.

**Real data-integrity catch, worth remembering:** while mapping captured instances back to `envProps` array entries, direct comparison against `AssetDatabase.LoadAssetAtPath(...).envProps[i].prefab.name` revealed this session had the `pfb_env_mailbox_telegraph` (guid `88ffa08a9dddd490d922505fc5fb2f6e`) and `pfb_env_lamp_post_western` (guid `fa6beacecd623485a9779fd4ab3c95df`) guids **swapped** in its own mental model since very early this session — confirmed definitively against each prefab's own `.meta` file. All *positions* written for these two prop types earlier in the session were still correct (guid-keyed edits landed on the right entries regardless of the wrong name attached to them in commentary), but this could easily have caused a real mix-up during this capture pass had it not been caught by verifying against the live loaded asset rather than trusting either the array-order assumption or memorized guid-to-name pairs. **Lesson: for any future edit touching these two prop types by guid, verify against the prefab's own `.meta` file, don't trust a memorized mapping — even one used correctly several times already.**

Not committed, per this project's approval rule. `RoomData_CulDeSac_Room1_v2.asset` (enemy spawn points) was explicitly out of scope for this pass per the owner's own answer ("just the 3 buildings" → later broadened to "other assets" meaning props, not enemies) — untouched.

**Continuation, same day — multiple rounds, several real mistakes caught and fixed along the way:**

1. **Wrong assumption, real data loss:** `pfb_env_covered_wagon`'s captured `localScale` of `0.7` was initially treated as "the prefab's native default, no write needed" — checked live against `AssetDatabase.LoadAssetAtPath(...).transform.localScale` and it's actually `(1,1,1)`. The owner had deliberately scaled all 4 wagons down to 0.7 and it was almost silently dropped. Fixed: wrote `localScale: (0.7,0.7,0.7)` to all 4 wagon `envProps` entries. **Lesson generalized: never treat a captured value as "just the native default" without checking the actual prefab asset — this session already had one scale-mismatch incident (B73) and treating "looks like a round number" as "must be default" repeated the same class of error.**

2. **Workbench move lost twice to environment instability, not data-mapping error:** the owner's first workbench reposition was captured *after* it had already been made, correctly, but a subsequent `refresh_unity` (triggered by an unrelated file edit) caused a domain reload that reset the live scene, silently reverting it before the owner could confirm. Second attempt appeared to also fail, but the deeper cause was that the owner's Scene-view click was selecting the workbench's `Visual` child object rather than its `pfb_workbench` root (which is what `WorkbenchProp`'s interaction-radius check actually reads via its own `transform.position`) — moving the child relocates the mesh but not the trigger. Root cause explained to the owner directly (select the root in the Hierarchy panel, not the mesh in Scene view); third attempt succeeded and was captured immediately: `workbenchPositions[0]` → `(-1.110, 0, 12.390)`.

3. **Static flag reverts on every domain reload, not just once:** the `isStatic=false` fix applied to unblock manipulation only lasts until the next scene reload (each reload re-instantiates from the prefab, which still has `isStatic=true` baked in) — this bit the owner a second time on the hitching posts specifically, surfacing as "I am not able to adjust them." Re-applied per-session; flagging as a standing fact for any future hands-on tuning pass in this project, not a one-time fix.

4. **Final captured deltas, all verified against live `Physics`/`NavMesh` queries before writing:** 3 of 4 hitching posts repositioned/re-rotated, both mailbox telegraphs re-rotated (`35°→219.8°/226.8°`), workbench repositioned as above. One hitching post's new position (`-5.8,6.53`) sits in a genuine but minor NavMesh pinch point between itself and the adjacent wagon (confirmed via `NavMesh.SamplePosition` at several offsets) — not a path blocker for any real traffic (core spawn↔workbench↔pickup↔pile routes all independently re-verified `PathComplete` after every round of changes), just a small dead zone immediately around those two props. Not fixed, flagged as cosmetic/low-priority.

5. **Owner reported item pickups "not visible"; root-caused rather than assumed.** Investigation found this was **not** a level-data problem: `WeaponInventory.MaterialBag` on the live player already held 3 items (matching the exact 3 weapon-pickup types) and both cardboard piles were similarly inactive — both `WeaponPickup.cs` and `CardboardPickup.cs` correctly `SetActive(false)` themselves once collected (`OnTriggerEnter`), and this session's many hours of prior testing (character selection, combat tests, teleporting the player around repeatedly) had already collected all of them, persisting through `SaveSystem`'s save file across the many Play Mode entries/exits today. Confirmed by checking the live `MaterialBag` count directly rather than guessing. **This is a testing-session artifact, not a bug** — a fresh save would show every pickup active from the start. The owner separately clarified the real ask was about occlusion/placement regardless, so repositioned all 3 weapon pickups and 1 cardboard pile to open ground clear of nearby props (confirmed via `Physics.OverlapSphere`, no colliders within 2m) and re-verified `NavMesh.CalculatePath` reachability on the new spots.

**Final state, verified live after the last refresh + fresh Play Mode entry (not assumed from the edited file):** `LevelBuilder` recompiled/spawned clean, `ValidateCameraClearance` unchanged in character (51/550 vertices under the diagnostic-only 8m threshold, 0/550 above — same ballpark as the last full check), all repositioned reachability targets (workbench, all 3 weapon pickups, both cardboard piles) independently `PathComplete` from a fresh spawn. Not committed. `git status`/`git diff` confirm only the 3 already-expected files changed and zero `(Clone)` residue in the saved scene.

### B77. `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` given the same ground-material + 35° rotation treatment as Room 1 (B72/B75) — **DONE 2026-08-24**

Direct continuation of B75: Room 1's rotation pass had explicitly left Ambush Alley out of scope ("still has the pre-rotation, axis-aligned layout — same treatment should follow once the owner confirms Room 1's result is correct"). Owner confirmed Room 1 and asked to move on to the Alley level; applied the identical two fixes, proactively re-using every lesson from the Room 1 saga (verify scale/guid identity against the live asset, don't trust `Physics.OverlapBox`/AABB checks on rotated content since a rigid rotation is a mathematical isometry, watch for `isStatic` reverts, re-verify camera clearance live rather than assuming).

**Ground material:** swapped the scene's `Ground` renderer from the raw URP `Lit.mat` default (guid `31321ba15b8f8eb4c954353edc038b1d`) to the project's `MAT_Ground.mat` (guid `8d5a229fa7456493288860d5855f8512`, same dirt texture already used in Room 1).

**Rotation:** same +35° rigid rotation around world origin as B75 (`x' = x·cos(35°)+z·sin(35°)`, `z' = -x·sin(35°)+z·cos(35°)`, quaternion `{x:0, y:0.300706, z:0, w:0.953717}` added to every object's own facing). Applied to:
- `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.unity`: `Ground` and all 8 `Boundary_*` transforms (position + rotation), plus `pfb_player`'s spawn rotation (position stayed `(0,0,0)`, already at the pivot).
- `RoomData_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.asset`: all 4 enemy spawn points (3× `WagonWheelRoller`, 1× `SkepticGrunt`), position + `facingY`.
- `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2.asset`: all 24 position/rotation entries (3 scattered weapons, 2 loot-zone weapons, 2 cardboard piles, 1 workbench position, 17 env props — each env prop's `eulerRotation.y` also bumped +35°).

**Re-validated live, not assumed:** loaded the rotated scene in the Unity Editor, entered Play Mode, confirmed clean compile/spawn (`[LevelBuilder] Runtime NavMesh baked: 483 verts, 219 tris.`, no errors). `ValidateCameraClearance` logged **nothing** — the diagnostic only fires when violations exist, so zero log output means 0 of 483 NavMesh vertices are under either the 8m-behind or 6m-above threshold, a cleaner result than Room 1's. Took a live gameplay screenshot: buildings, wagons, and props all read as a consistent diagonal composition matching the camera's fixed yaw, ground shows the dirt texture. Exited Play Mode, saved the scene, confirmed zero `(Clone)` residue and no player-position drift in the saved file.

**Not done:** the same B73 proper-fix (normalizing the 6 new buildings' import scale to 1 instead of the collider-size-divide-by-100 workaround) remains outstanding — this pass didn't touch those prefabs. Hands-on Play-Mode tuning (the B76 treatment) has not been applied to this room yet — the rotation math was computed and machine-verified but not hand-refined by the owner. Not committed, per this project's approval rule.

### B78. B73's 6 CulDeSac buildings also carry a baked root **rotation** (`X=270°`), not just the known 100x scale — must be preserved, not zeroed, or the building renders squashed/on-its-side — found 2026-08-25 building `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`

Extends B73, same 6 prefabs (`pfb_env_bld_bank/stables/general_store/barber_shop/sheriffs_office/blacksmith_forge`). Each prefab's root transform default is `localPosition: (15,0,0)`, `localRotation euler: (270,0,0)`, `localScale: (100,100,100)` — B73 only documented the scale (and its collider consequence); the `(15,0,0)` position is harmless (any explicit instance `position` overrides it), but the `X=270°` rotation is not: it is the correction that makes the model stand upright (its raw mesh is authored lying down / wrong up-axis, same family of defect as the scale issue — almost certainly the same sloppy prefab-capture-from-a-staging-scene root cause B73 already named).

**What went wrong first, for the record:** placed all 6 using a plain absolute `eulerAngles = (0, yaw, 0)` per building (to face them along the street), which fully replaces the baked `(270,0,0)` rather than adding to it. Every building still *looked* like a plausible building in a screenshot (roof on top, door below) but was actually rendered through the wrong axis mapping — real symptom, confirmed by the owner watching the live Editor directly ("laying on their side," "scale doesn't match"), and independently confirmed by comparing `Renderer.bounds` before/after: e.g. Bank's live height read `7.55m` with the naive fix vs its true, correct `4.59m` once fixed (a `BANK`-signage screenshot at the true baseline made the correct orientation unambiguous — the naive version's "front" was actually a side/back face that happened to still have windows).

**Correct pattern (empirically verified, matches how `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`/B74 already used these same prefabs successfully — `rot Y=90` in that log almost certainly meant "change only the Y euler component," which implicitly preserves this baked X tilt):** compose the desired street-facing yaw as a world-space rotation on top of the baked base, don't overwrite it — `finalRotation = Quaternion.AngleAxis(desiredYaw, Vector3.up) * Quaternion.Euler(270f, 0f, 0f)`, set via `transform.rotation` (not a partial `eulerAngles.y = ...` write, which is safer but was not available through the MCP tool used here). At `yaw=0` all 6 face world `+Z`; `yaw=90` faces `+X`, `yaw=270` faces `-X` (confirmed empirically, not just derived).

**Same defect, different base values, also found and fixed this session:** `pfb_env_bld_porchcabin` and `pfb_env_bld_shedwithcrate` (older buildings, `Models/ENV/Buildings/PorchCabin`/`ShedWithCrate`, not part of the B73 CulDeSac batch) bake `(270, 180, 0)` — same `X=270` correction plus an extra baked 180° yaw. `pfb_env_stacked_crates` and `pfb_env_train_sign` (props, not buildings) bake the identical `(270,180,0)` and need the same compose-not-replace treatment if given a directional yaw.

**Recommend:** when B73's "proper fix" (normalize prefab import scale to `1,1,1`) is finally done for real, zero the baked rotation and position at the same time — a clean prefab (`position 0,0,0` / `rotation 0,0,0` / `scale 1,1,1`) removes this whole class of trap for whoever places these next. Until then, anyone placing any of these 6 (or `porchcabin`/`shedwithcrate`/`stacked_crates`/`train_sign`) must read the prefab's actual default rotation first and compose on top of it — don't assume identity.

### B79. `pfb_env_western_house_tall` mesh is genuinely undersized (~1-2m, not a real "tall house") — broken as-is, no working substitute for that roster slot — found 2026-08-25

`Assets/_Project/Models/ENV/Buildings/WesternHouseTall/western_house_tall.fbx`, committed in `60123e26` (old, predates the CulDeSac batch), imports with correct-looking `ModelImporter` settings (`globalScale: 1`, `useFileScale: 1`, matching the working `TwoStoryHouse.fbx`) but the actual mesh geometry measures only `0.97 × 1.90 × 1.11` at scale `(1,1,1)` — a real, verified defect at the source mesh/export, not an importer misconfiguration (ruled out by comparing importer settings directly against a known-good sibling). Never placed in any scene to date (grepped `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity`, found no reference), so this was never caught in production use.

Not fixed here per the asset-pipeline rule against compensating with an arbitrary instance scale — flagged instead. **Practical impact:** `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` (this session's new scene) ships with only 10 of the 11 requested unique buildings; "Western House Tall" has no usable asset today. Needs an `asset-engineer` pass (re-export from source at correct scale) before it can be placed anywhere.

### B85. Cross-building scale is inconsistent within the older (non-CulDeSac-batch) building prefabs — normalized per-instance in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`, not at the source

Separate from B78's rotation bug: `pfb_env_bld_porchcabin` (a "small cabin") and `pfb_env_bld_shedwithcrate` (a "shed") each imported at a footprint *larger* than the Bank or General Store — `porchcabin 8.84×9.48`, `shedwithcrate 7.40×9.49`, vs. `bank 7.55×7.54`/`general_store 6.79×8.54` — and `pfb_env_bld_twostoryhouse` imported at `9.49m` tall, noticeably taller than every other building including the visually-similar two-story `pfb_env_bld_barber_shop` (`5.12m`). None of these are individually "broken" (each renders at a real, deliberate default scale, unlike B79/B80), they're just mutually inconsistent as a set, which reads oddly once several are placed side-by-side on one street (a "porch cabin" bigger than the bank). Owner caught this directly in the live Editor.

**Fixed for this scene only, as an instance-level `localScale` multiplier** (on top of each prefab's existing default scale, not touching the prefab asset): `porchcabin ×0.6`, `shedwithcrate ×0.55`, `twostoryhouse ×0.8`, `barber_shop ×1.1`. Resulting heights now cluster `3.2-5.6m` for single-story buildings and `~7.2-7.6m` for the two tallest (Saloon, Two Story House), which reads as a coherent town. **Not fixed at the source** — any other scene placing these same prefabs fresh will hit the same inconsistency and need the same (or a properly-authored) correction. Worth a real `asset-engineer` normalization pass across the whole building set (CulDeSac batch + older buildings) against one shared real-world-scale convention, rather than each scene re-discovering and re-fixing it independently.

### B86. `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`'s entire static layout rotated 45° to the right (clockwise) around world origin — **DONE 2026-08-25**

Owner asked for the whole level rotated 45° "to the right." Applied the same rigid-rotation pattern as B75/B77 (`x' = x·cos(45°)+z·sin(45°)`, `z' = -x·sin(45°)+z·cos(45°)`, θ added to every object's own yaw), but via a cleaner mechanism specific to this scene's hierarchy: `[ENV - Static]` (parent of `Ground`, `[Buildings]`, `[Street Props]`) and `[Player]` (parent of `pfb_player`) both sit at exactly world-origin position with identity rotation, so rotating **only these two parent transforms** (`transform.rotation = Quaternion.AngleAxis(45, Vector3.up) * transform.rotation`) is mathematically identical to rotating every descendant's position around the origin and composing 45° onto its own yaw — Unity's transform hierarchy does the composition automatically, including correctly preserving the B78 baked-`X=270°` rotation on the 6 CulDeSac buildings (verified live post-rotation: Bank `Y 90°→135°`, Blacksmith Forge `Y 270°→315°`, baked `X=270°` untouched on both). Verified the math against live `Renderer`/`Transform` reads (Ground, Bank, Saloon, Blacksmith Forge world position/rotation) to the decimal before trusting it.

**10 buildings + 34 street props + Ground all rotated as one rigid body** (live-counted, not assumed from memory — the scene's actual current prop count is 34, lower than an earlier session's "58," reflecting a later re-clustering pass; `[Lighting]/Sun` deliberately left untouched, direction is a lighting choice independent of level layout).

**Camera rig (`pfb_Main_Camera`/`pfb_CM_FollowCam`) deliberately NOT rotated, after checking ground truth rather than guessing:** inspected the live `CinemachineFollow` component on `pfb_CM_FollowCam` and found `TrackerSettings.BindingMode = World Space` with a fixed `FollowOffset (-4.84, 10.02, -13.45)` — meaning the camera's real Play-Mode framing is driven entirely by this fixed world-space offset from the Follow target, not by the vcam GameObject's own scene-authored Transform (which Cinemachine fully overrides every frame). This is architecturally the same pattern as B75/B77's `LevelBuilder._cameraYawDegrees`: a fixed camera reference frame that room/level content rotates underneath, not something that rotates with the content. Rotating the camera's edit-time Transform anyway would have been harmless-but-misleading (doesn't match actual runtime behavior) — left both camera GameObjects' Transforms completely untouched, consistent with precedent.

**Direction verified empirically, not assumed:** hand-computed two independent reference points through the applied rotation formula and cross-checked against the standard top-down (bird's-eye, north=+Z up, east=+X right) clock convention — a point due north of origin moves toward north-east under this rotation, and a point due south (the player spawn) moves toward south-west; both are unambiguously the *clockwise* direction. This matches "rotated to the right" as the owner meant it (things ahead of the player at spawn sweep toward the player's right, like a clock's 12 sweeping toward 3). Cross-verified via oblique and bird's-eye positioned screenshots pre/post rotation (screenshots alone were visually ambiguous at this resolution/texture density — the coordinate math was the decisive check, consistent with [[feedback_baked_transform_compose_rotation]]'s lesson to trust direct numeric comparison over screenshot-only judgment).

**NavMesh rebaked** (legacy static bake, `UnityEditor.AI.NavMeshBuilder.BuildNavMesh()`, same agent settings as prior sessions): `1240 verts/544 tris → 1313 verts/603 tris`. Modest increase is expected/explainable — NavMesh voxelization is axis-aligned in world space, so rotating content relative to that fixed grid shifts triangulation slightly even though the true walkable topology is an isometry of the original. **Reachability re-confirmed**, not assumed: `NavMesh.CalculatePath` from the new spawn `(-41.01, 0, -41.01)` to the northmost reachable navmesh vertex `PathComplete`s (4 corners); a coarse-to-fine grid sweep (289–1075 sample points at 1-2m spacing) found failures clustering exactly on prop/building footprints (hitching posts, wagons, barrels — correctly non-walkable) and the two already-documented B-series dead zones (saloon porch, blacksmith forge corner — pre-existing, owner-placement-caused, left alone per the task's own instruction), not as new gaps in open street.

Played the scene once post-rotation to confirm a clean console and get a real gameplay-camera screenshot — see B87 for a real, pre-existing player-controller defect found and worked around (non-destructively, runtime-only) during that verification. Scene saved; remains untracked in git (never committed), not committed this pass either per project rule.

### B87. `PlayerController._arenaBoundaryRadius` default (8.5m, centered at world origin) is unusable in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` — player free-falls forever in Play Mode — found 2026-08-25, **FIXED 2026-08-25**

`Assets/_Project/Scripts/Player/PlayerController.cs` `ClampToArenaBounds()` forcibly clamps the player's XZ position to within `_arenaBoundaryRadius` (default `8.5f`) of `_arenaCenter` (default `Vector3.zero`) every frame — a reasonable guard for the small ~20-40m Room1/AmbushAlley combat arenas (see B75's untouched `_arenaBoundaryRadius`/`_arenaCenter` note), but `pfb_player`'s instance in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` never overrode these defaults (confirmed live via `SerializedObject`), while its spawn sits ~58m from world origin on this scene's street. Every frame, the clamp snapped the player's XZ back to within 8.5m of origin — a spot with no ground geometry under it — while gravity kept pulling it down independently, so the `CharacterController` free-fell indefinitely (observed falling past `Y=-344`).

**Fixed as a `pfb_player` prefab **instance override** scoped to `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity` only — the shared `pfb_player.prefab` asset is untouched** (verified: `git status` shows zero diff on the prefab asset; the scene's `PrefabInstance.m_Modifications` block gained exactly three new entries — `_arenaCenter.x`, `_arenaCenter.z`, `_arenaBoundaryRadius` — alongside the pre-existing spawn-position override). `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` and `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` are unaffected; their `pfb_player` instances still use the prefab's own defaults (8.5m / origin).

**Values set:** `_arenaCenter = (-25.2437, 0, -25.2437)`, `_arenaBoundaryRadius = 23`.

Both were derived from the *live* scene geometry, not estimated — the task's own framing ("rotation preserves radius if center stays at world origin") turned out to be a false lead once measured: the `Ground` plane itself is **not** centered on world origin (its true transform position is `(-25.2437, 0, -25.2437)`, confirmed via `Renderer.bounds`/`MeshFilter` and cross-checked against the independently-computed `NavMesh.CalculateTriangulation()` AABB center, which matched almost exactly). Using world origin as `_arenaCenter` would have required a ~62m radius reaching almost entirely over void in most directions. Centering on the Ground plane's own geometric centroid instead is the mathematically optimal choice for a circular clamp on a rectangular street (it maximizes the inscribed-circle safety margin).

Precise raycast sweeps against the `Ground` `MeshCollider` (from height 100, stepping outward from center) established the street's true local rectangle: half-width (flank-to-flank) ≈ 20m, half-length (south spawn to north end) ≈ 23.75–24m, confirmed to the nearest 0.25m at the cardinal directions. Key measured distances from the chosen center: spawn `22.30m`, north-street-centerline `~23.75m` (true void edge), pure west/east-flank-perpendicular direction `~20.25m` (true void edge). `_arenaBoundaryRadius = 23` was chosen as the tightest value that (a) keeps spawn safely inside (0.7m clearance) and (b) stays under the north/south void edge (0.75m clearance) — verified empirically in Play Mode (see below), not just computed.

**Known residual limitation, inherent to using a single circular clamp on a non-square playable area (not fixable via instance override alone):** the true void boundary is direction-dependent (~20.25m straight out past a flank building's outer corner vs. ~23.75m north/south along the centerline). At `radius=23`, a player who deliberately walks in the narrow pure-perpendicular-flank direction — past a building's outer face, into the empty dressing margin beyond it, rather than along the street or toward the building's front — could in principle reach the true ~20.25m void edge before the 23m clamp would engage. This is not on any of the scene's intended walking paths (verified: the actual building-flank test points, at the buildings' outer face ~13–16m from center, sit nowhere near this edge case) and was not observed during manual verification. Flagging for awareness if `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` architecture is later formalized (ADR-0004) — a proper fix would need either a non-circular containment shape or invisible boundary colliders at the `Ground` plane's true edges, both real script/architecture changes out of scope for this instance-override fix.

**Play Mode verification (2026-08-25):** entered Play Mode, confirmed spawn position and live component values matched the override (`_arenaCenter (-25.24, 0, -25.24)`, `_arenaBoundaryRadius 23`).
