Directly invoked the private `ClampToArenaBounds()` method (via reflection, for deterministic testing without needing live input simulation) after teleporting the player to each of: spawn, the street's north end, the west-flank point near `pfb_env_bld_bank`, the east-flank point near `pfb_env_bld_sheriffs_office`, and two additional flank points near other buildings — **zero clamping occurred at any of these points** (`moved=0.00` in all cases) and a downward raycast confirmed solid ground/building geometry under every one (no void). Also confirmed the clamp is still genuinely active (not effectively disabled): a point placed well beyond the radius (local north, distance 40) was correctly pulled back to exactly `distFromCenter=23.00`. Console clean on Stop (only benign `MCP-FOR-UNITY` port-reconnect noise, no gameplay errors). Player position correctly reverted to its authored spawn `(-41.01, 0, -41.01)` on Stop. Scene saved.

### B88. `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` given the same room/zone-independent core scaffolding every other playable scene has — **DONE 2026-08-26**

Ground truth was read live from `CulDeSac_Room1_v2.unity` (not assumed from ADR-0004 or any planning doc) before touching `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`: Room 1's actual root-level set is `pfb_AudioManager`, `pfb_AttackTelegraphService`, `pfb_MinimapCamera`, `pfb_ProgressionSystem` (which also carries `DifficultyManager` on the same prefab — no separate `pfb_DifficultyManager` object exists), `pfb_SaveSystem` (all five as true scene roots, not nested — confirmed via `grep -rl DontDestroyOnLoad`: only `AudioManager`/`AttackTelegraphService`/`ProgressionSystem`/`SaveSystem` actually call it; `GameManager` does not, so it's safely nested under `[Managers]`), plus a `[Managers]` group (`pfb_GameManager`, `pfb_RoomManager`, a hand-authored `LevelBuilder` GameObject) and a `[HUD]` group (`pfb_hud_v4`, `pfb_run_end_screen`, `pfb_MetaScreen`). No `UpgradeScreen`/`ShopScreen` instances exist anywhere in Room 1 — `GameManager._upgradeScreen`/`_shopScreen` are simply null there, consistent with a single-room scene that has no room-to-room screen transition to show.

**Added to `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`, matching Room 1 exactly except the two explicitly-excluded objects:** `pfb_AudioManager`, `pfb_AttackTelegraphService`, `pfb_MinimapCamera`, `pfb_ProgressionSystem`, `pfb_SaveSystem` (scene roots), a `[Managers]` group containing only `pfb_GameManager`, and a `[HUD]` group containing `pfb_hud_v4`, `pfb_run_end_screen`, `pfb_MetaScreen`.

**`LevelBuilder` and `RoomManager` deliberately left out, not added-empty.** Investigated rather than guessed: `grep`ed every call site of `RoomManager.Instance`/`FindAnyObjectByType<RoomManager>`/`FindAnyObjectByType<LevelBuilder>` across `Assets/_Project/Scripts/`. The only two call sites are `GameManager.CheckWinCondition()`'s `if (RoomManager.Instance != null) return;` (a defensive guard, safe when null — falls through to the normal dead-count win check) and `RoomTrigger.OnTriggerEnter`'s `RoomManager.Instance?.OnRoomEntered(...)` (null-conditional, and this scene has no `RoomTrigger` anyway since no zones exist yet). Nothing else references either type. Confirmed live in Play Mode: zero null-ref spam, zero missing-component warnings, with both absent. Adding either in an empty/unconfigured state would have been pure inert scaffolding one commit ahead of the zone-design conversation — left out per the task's own "don't half-configure ahead of design" instruction.

**No scene-local rewiring needed, also verified rather than assumed.** Read `pfb_GameManager`'s live serialized fields (`_gameOverUI`, `hudController`, `_runStartUI`, `_metaScreen`, `_upgradeScreen`, `_shopScreen`, `_runEndScreen`) via `SerializedObject` on Room 1's own instance — **all seven are null there too**. `GameManager.Start()` resolves every one of them via `FindAnyObjectByType<T>(FindObjectsInactive.Include)` fallback at runtime; Room 1 never wires them in the Inspector. `PauseMenu`'s own two fields (`_panel`, `_pauseAction`) are both prefab-internal/asset references, not scene-external. Mirrored this exactly — `pfb_GameManager` was added with zero Inspector overrides, exactly like Room 1.

**One real, separate defect found and left unfixed (prefab asset, out of scope — see B89): `pfb_hud_v4.prefab`'s `HUD_Canvas` carries 9 duplicate `StyleSelectPanel`/`MinimapPanel` child pairs baked into the prefab asset itself** (confirmed via raw grep on the `.prefab` file, not scene-instance drift — Room 1's live instance and a fresh `LoadPrefabContents` read of the prefab both show the same 9x count). Not touched, since fixing it would require editing a prefab asset (forbidden for this scene-instance-only task) and it's pre-existing across every scene using this prefab, not something this pass introduced.

**Correction of record (found during the B109 review pass, 2026-08-26): the claim below was wrong even at the time it was written.** `GameManager.ZoneIndexByScene`/`ZoneStartScene[0]` was set to `"CulDeSac_WildWestCity"` in commit `b80953ca` — already in `HEAD` before this B88 entry's own Play Mode verification ran (confirmed via `git log -S`). So `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` **is** a `ZoneStartScene` value, `atRunStartRoom` is `true`, and the "Choose Your Hero" picker showing (as B109 separately, correctly observed) is the intended behavior, not a mystery. The original claim just below was never true; it wasn't a later regression.

~~Also confirmed by reading `RunStartUI.Show()` directly: since `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` is not a `GameManager.ZoneStartScene` value, `Show()` takes the early "silent pass-through" branch (`!atRunStartRoom` is true) — it never activates the character-picker panel or sets `Time.timeScale = 0`. No manual `Hide()`/deactivation workaround was needed for this scene's Play Mode verification, unlike Room 1.~~

**Play Mode verification:** entered Play Mode once, console clean throughout (only the pre-existing benign `[EnemySpawnPoint]` template warning, `MCP-FOR-UNITY` port-reconnect noise, the harmless URP/Metal "depth surface memoryless" log, and the expected `[GameManager] No enemies found`/`No EnemySpawner found` messages — all confirmed as pre-existing/expected elsewhere in this doc, none new). HUD confirmed rendering via screenshot (health bar, IP counter, action buttons, joystick, weapon slots all visible). B87's arena clamp override re-verified intact and functional via the same reflection-invoked `ClampToArenaBounds()` technique (in-bounds point unclamped, out-of-bounds point pulled back to exactly `distFromCenter=23.00`). Player position correctly reverted to spawn on Stop. Scene saved.

### B89. `pfb_hud_v4.prefab`'s `HUD_Canvas` bakes 9 duplicate `StyleSelectPanel`/`MinimapPanel` pairs — found 2026-08-26, **FIXED 2026-08-26** (`ui-ux-designer` pass, bundled with the minimap circular-mask task)

Found while adding `[HUD]` to `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` (B88). `HUD_Canvas` had 30 children; indices 5–22 were 9 repeated `StyleSelectPanel`+`MinimapPanel` pairs, byte-identical in structure (same buttons, same layout groups). Confirmed as a real prefab-asset defect, not scene-instance drift: `grep -c 'm_Name: StyleSelectPanel$'`/`'m_Name: MinimapPanel$'` on `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/UI/pfb_hud_v4.prefab` both returned exactly 9, and a fresh `PrefabUtility.LoadPrefabContents` read of the untouched prefab asset showed the same count — this was baked into every scene using this prefab (Room 1, Ambush Alley, the city), not something any one scene introduced. Each duplicate carried a live `RunStartUI` component, so `GameManager`'s `FindAnyObjectByType<RunStartUI>()` fallback resolved to whichever instance Unity's internal object enumeration happened to return first — functionally harmless (all 9 were identical, correctly-wired copies) but wasteful (9x the UI elements, layout components, and button listeners for one on-screen panel) and a real risk the day someone edited "the" `StyleSelectPanel` and only changed one of nine.

**Fixed by direct raw-YAML surgery on the prefab asset** (not via Unity's interactive Prefab Stage — see the caution note below), guided by a full parse of the `.prefab` file into its GameObject/Transform/Component graph: confirmed via `RectTransform` field comparison that all 9 `MinimapPanel` copies (and all 9 `StyleSelectPanel` copy *roots*) shared byte-identical `m_AnchoredPosition`/`m_SizeDelta`/`m_AnchorMin`/`m_AnchorMax`, all parented to the same `HUD_Canvas` — i.e. genuinely stacked on top of each other, not offset, so removing 8 of each pair changes nothing visually.

**Correction of record (found during the follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26): "byte-identical" did not hold below the panel roots.** All 9 `MinimapPanel` copies really were fully identical. The 9 `StyleSelectPanel` copies were not: the kept copy's button colors (gold-selected/grey-unselected) differ from the 8 deleted copies' baked colors (blue/green/orange/red), and `Title`'s `m_SizeDelta` differs too. This is harmless, not a bug — `RunStartUI.SetHighlight()` unconditionally overwrites every button's color on `Show()`/on selection change, so the deleted copies' colors were dead authored values that would have been overwritten the first time any of them was ever actually shown; the RectTransform deltas are likewise overridden by the panel's own `VerticalLayoutGroup`/`HorizontalLayoutGroup`s. Worth knowing: the kept copy carries baked runtime selection state (Male+Ninja+Medium highlighted) — another instance of this project's known Editor-Play-Mode-state-leak pattern, not a new one.

Kept the first pair in `HUD_Canvas`'s sibling order (`StyleSelectPanel` fileID `93608530122769368` / `MinimapPanel` fileID `1168566419173634358`), deleted the other 8 pairs' full subtrees (184 GameObjects, 888 fileIDs total including components), and removed their 16 corresponding entries from `HUD_Canvas`'s `RectTransform.m_Children` list. Verified zero dangling `{fileID: ...}` references to any deleted object anywhere in the resulting file before writing it, then confirmed via `manage_prefabs get_info`/`get_hierarchy` post-reimport: exactly one `StyleSelectPanel` and one `MinimapPanel` remain, `childCount` dropped from 416 to 232 (a reduction of 184 GameObjects — exactly 8 × 23, matching the expected per-pair subtree size). `HUDController_V2`/`GameManager`/`RunStartUI` were confirmed beforehand to reference these panels only via `FindAnyObjectByType`/self-contained internal fields, never a serialized cross-reference to a specific duplicate, so no rewiring was needed.

**Caution for future prefab-asset surgery on this file (or similarly large/shared prefabs):** opening `pfb_hud_v4.prefab` in Unity's interactive Prefab Stage (`open_prefab_stage`) and making even one small live edit via `manage_gameobject` (a single RectTransform position change) triggered Unity to write the *entire* prefab asset back to disk — including collapsing three unrelated `RectTransform`s (in `ActionIcons`/`WeaponSlots`-area layout children) to degenerate `AnchorMax {0,0}`/`AnchoredPosition {0,0}`/`SizeDelta {0,0}`, rewriting two TextMeshPro internal cache fields, and baking a bogus `AnchoredPosition` onto the very `MinimapPanel` being edited (a world-space `[0,0,0]` was evidently reinterpreted through the Canvas transform into `{0, -514.89}`). This was caught immediately via `git diff` before anything was saved/committed and fully reverted (`git checkout` on just this one file, prefab stage closed without saving) before redoing the work as pure text edits instead. Root cause likely compounded by this Unity Editor session already carrying unrelated leaked/dirty state from before this task started (the scene was already `isDirty: true` and `SpinCycleAI.cs` had unrelated uncommitted changes neither of which this task touched) — consistent with the known Play-Mode-state-leak pattern this project has hit before. **Recommendation: prefer headless/text-level edits (or `manage_prefabs modify_contents`) over `open_prefab_stage` + live `manage_gameobject` edits for structural changes to shared, complex prefabs, and always `git diff` immediately after any prefab-stage interaction before trusting the result.**

Verified live in Play Mode in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`: HUD renders with exactly one minimap and one character-select panel (the latter incidentally exercised on this same Play session — "Choose Your Hero" panel rendered and functioned correctly off the single kept `RunStartUI` instance), console clean of any new errors, on-disk scene file unchanged (only the prefab asset was edited, per the task's constraint). See the minimap circular-mask work directly below for the paired change and its own Play Mode screenshot verification.

**Scope correction (follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26): this entry's "FIXED" status covers only the `StyleSelectPanel`/`MinimapPanel` instance — the same duplication defect class is still present at four other paths in this same prefab**, found by scanning the surviving hierarchy for same-parent same-name siblings: `HUD_Camera/HUD_3D/HUD_StyleIconFrame/Bust_Ninja` (×9), `Bust_Cowboy` (×9), `HUD_Canvas/ActionIcons/BG_Special_Back/CountdownOverlay` (×8), `HUD_Canvas/ForgePanel/Content/ButtonRow/Row` (×3) — 26 redundant siblings total, not touched by this pass (out of scope, pre-existing, same likely root cause). The `Bust_*` pair is the most worth a follow-up: 9 stacked 3D-rendered meshes under the HUD camera is a real mobile draw-call cost, not just tidiness.

**Follow-up done — see B111 (2026-08-26).** `Bust_Ninja`/`Bust_Cowboy`/`CountdownOverlay` were genuine duplicates and are now collapsed to one copy each (plus a related, previously-unlisted `StyleBustSwapper` ×9 component duplication on `HUD_StyleIconFrame` that had to be resolved alongside the Bust fix). `ForgePanel/.../Row` (×3) turned out NOT to be a duplication defect on inspection — three distinct rows sharing a generic name — and was correctly left alone. Full detail in B111.

**Also found, not fixed:** `CulDeSac_BossArena.unity` (a shipping scene, registered in `EditorBuildSettings.asset` index 7) carries 8 stale `m_IsActive: 0` prefab-instance overrides targeting the now-deleted `MinimapPanel` copies' fileIDs. Behavior is unaffected — the override on the kept `MinimapPanel` is still present, so the minimap stays correctly hidden in that scene — and Unity will drop the unresolvable entries silently on next save. Not fixed here because it requires opening/re-saving `CulDeSac_BossArena.unity`, one of the reference-only old scenes this project has a standing rule against touching without the owner's say-so; flagged for the owner to decide rather than done unilaterally.

### B90. `pfb_MinimapCamera`'s prefab-default framing (position, clip planes) is tuned for Room 1's small room and doesn't work on `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`'s much larger street — **FIXED 2026-08-26, scene-local instance override**

Found live, mid-task, by the owner: the HUD's minimap panel rendered as a flat solid-color square with no map content. Root-caused to two independent problems with the prefab's default transform/`Camera` values (`position (0,40,7)`, `orthographicSize 18`, `near 0.3`/`far 1000`, full culling mask) once instantiated fresh in this scene:

1. **Wrong framing area.** `Ground`'s true center in this scene is `(-25.2437, 0, -25.2437)` (see B87) with an AABB half-extent of ~31m (the street rotated 45°, per B86). The prefab-default camera position `(0,40,7)` and `orthographicSize 18` frame a ~36×36m area centered near world origin — nowhere near the actual street, so the camera saw empty ground and rendered nothing but its own solid clear color.
2. **Camera/blip altitude collision, a second, independent bug — present in the prefab default itself, not scene-specific.** `MinimapIndicator._altitudeY` (the world-Y where every entity's colored minimap blip floats) defaults to `40` — the *exact same* Y as the minimap camera's own default position. A top-down orthographic camera at the same height as its subject puts that subject at zero distance, inside the camera's own near-clip offset (`near 0.3`/`0.1` in the two scenes checked) — the blip mesh (a thin disc, ~0.1m tall, centered at Y=40) ends up entirely on the wrong side of the near plane and never renders. Verified empirically, not just by formula: setting the camera above the blip altitude (see values below) made a player blip visibly appear in a direct screenshot of the minimap camera's own output; leaving it at the shared `Y=40` default did not, in repeated tests. This second issue is not scene-specific — any scene using `pfb_MinimapCamera` at its raw prefab-default position likely has the same latent non-rendering blip, masked in Room 1/Ambush Alley only by nobody having looked closely at a raw minimap-camera-only screenshot before.

**Fixed as a scene-local `pfb_MinimapCamera` instance override in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` only** (prefab asset untouched, confirmed via `git status`): `position (-25.2437, 42, -25.2437)` (centered on `Ground`, 2m above the `Y=40` blip altitude — enough clearance for `near 0.5` to comfortably exclude the blip from being clipped without pushing the camera unnecessarily high per owner feedback mid-task that the first attempt, `Y=45`, felt too far from the ground), `orthographicSize 32` (covers `Ground`'s full ~31m AABB half-extent with a small margin), `near clip 0.5`, `far clip 55` (camera-to-ground distance ~42 plus margin), `cullingMask` narrowed to layers `Default`(0)/`Building`(8)/`Minimap`(20) — bits `1048833`, matching the same narrowing Room 1's own scene-instance already applies (its `.unity` file was read directly to confirm this exact mask value and the rationale, not guessed).

**Verified via direct screenshots of `pfb_MinimapCamera`'s own output** (not just the small in-HUD panel) at each iteration: first attempt (blank/flat) → after position+clip fix (full rotated street layout with a colored player blip, correctly framed) → after the owner's "closer to the ground" feedback (same correct content at the lower height). Also confirmed in a full gameplay screenshot that the HUD's `MinimapPanel`/`MinimapView` `RawImage` (which just displays the `MinimapRT` render target) shows the same content at its actual in-HUD size. Re-verified clean console and no regression to B87's arena clamp in the same Play Mode pass. Scene saved.

**Not fixed at the source** (`pfb_MinimapCamera.prefab`'s own default transform, or `MinimapIndicator._altitudeY`'s shared `40` default) — out of scope for a scene-instance-only task, and the blip-clipping issue in particular needs verifying against Room 1/Ambush Alley's actual live behavior before deciding whether the fix belongs on the prefab default or is something every future scene should keep overriding per-geometry. Worth a `unity-gameplay-engineer` follow-up pass to check whether Room 1's own minimap blip actually renders today.

### B91. Building colliders in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` are far wider than their meshes — the player can walk into porches and facades — found 2026-08-26, not fixed

Found while measuring the street for ADR-0004's boss-arena analysis. Every building in `[ENV - Static]/[Buildings]` carries a single `BoxCollider` that does not match its mesh footprint. Measured in the street-local frame (the ENV root is rotated 45°, see B86): the **collision** corridor between the two building rows is about X ±12.9, while the **visual** corridor is X −8.8…+10.0. The player and every NavMesh agent can therefore walk 2–4m into what looks like solid building on both sides of the street for most of its length. Several building colliders also extend well below ground (`bank` y −3.8…3.8, `stables` y −4.2…4.2, `barber_shop` y −3.0…3.0), which is the same symptom — a raw unit cube scaled by the prefab's compensating import scale (B85) rather than fitted to the mesh.

Not caused by the 45° rotation (B86) and not scene-local: the colliders come from the building prefabs. Two consequences already accepted in ADR-0004: all its clearance figures are computed against **mesh** footprints, not colliders, precisely because the colliders are optimistic; and the runtime NavMesh (which bakes from `PhysicsColliders`) is correspondingly too generous at the street edges. Needs an `asset-engineer` pass to fit each building's collider to its mesh — likely alongside B78–B85, which are the same family of prefab-level defects.

### B92. Boss and wagon-wheel-roller `NavMeshAgent` radii exceed the project's only baked NavMesh agent type — found 2026-08-26, not fixed

The project has exactly **one** NavMesh agent type (ID 0 Humanoid: radius 0.5, height 2, climb 0.75, slope 45) and `LevelBuilder.BuildNavMeshDeferred` bakes for it. But two prefabs use `m_AgentTypeID: 0` with much larger agents: `pfb_enemy_spincycle` (`m_Radius: 1`, `m_Height: 4`) and `pfb_enemy_wagonwheel_roller` (`m_Radius: 0.95`, `m_BaseOffset: 0.9`). The mesh is carved 0.5m from every obstacle, so any gap between 1.0m and 2.0m reads as walkable and physically is not — the agent will grind against geometry or stall.

Latent in the old rooms (open arenas, few pinch points); `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` exposes it, because the boardwalk props line both sides of the street at street-local |X| ≈ 7.5–10 and leave exactly that class of gap. ADR-0004's zone and spawn layouts deliberately keep both agent types away from those pinch points, so this is not a blocker for the city build, but it is a real correctness bug. Two candidate fixes: add a second NavMesh agent type for large agents and bake both (more correct, more setup), or reduce those two agents' `m_Radius` to match their actual visual footprint (cheaper, and probably right — neither model is genuinely 2m wide). Needs a `unity-gameplay-engineer` decision, ideally with the boss actually walked around the city street.

### B93. `SpinCycleAI`'s jump-back, spin charge, and jump charge have no NavMesh or bounds clamp on their landing point — found 2026-08-26, not fixed

`SpinCharge`, `JumpBack`, and `JumpCharge` all set `_agent.enabled = false` and move the boss by raw `transform.position` arithmetic, then `_agent.Warp(transform.position)` afterwards. None of them validates the destination against the NavMesh or any arena bounds first. In the worst geometry — boss on the outside, player between it and the arena centre — `jumpBackDistance = 4` can put the boss several metres outside the playable area, into a building. `Approach()`'s off-mesh recovery loop re-`Warp`s every frame until the agent is back on the mesh, so it self-corrects rather than soft-locking, but with a visible teleport pop.

Pre-existing — the same hole is present in `CulDeSac_BossArena` today, masked there by a 28×28m boundary box that makes the bad case rare. ADR-0004 §2 measured the city's boss arena at r 8.39m (with the covered wagons cleared), which is enough for the attacks to function but has a thinner margin than the old arena, so the case becomes more reachable. Recommended fix is cheap and local: clamp each landing point with `NavMesh.SamplePosition` before committing to the move. Explicitly **not** a reason to enlarge the arena — the owner's "no ground expansion" decision stands.

### B94. `RoomManager.HasZoneAfterCurrent` is a hand-set boundary check, not derived — fine today, latent trap for a future non-boss-ending world — found 2026-08-26 (code review), not fixed

`HasZoneAfterCurrent => _currentRoom >= 0 && _currentRoom < _rooms.Count - 1` assumes the *last* `RoomDataSO` in a scene's `LevelBuilder.RoomData` array is always the one that ends the run (a boss zone, or otherwise never "cleared" via `RoomManager.OnRoomCleared`). True for every scene that exists today (`CulDeSac_WildWestCity`'s zone 2 is `bossOwnedWin`), so no behavior bug currently. But nothing enforces that assumption: a future single-scene world with a non-boss final zone (e.g. a last zone that *does* fire `OnRoomCleared`) would have `HasZoneAfterCurrent` report `false` one zone too early, causing `GameManager.OnUpgradePicked()` to fall through to the legacy `LoadNextRoom()` scene-load path instead of staying in-scene. Low priority — no reproduction case exists yet — but worth a comment/assert (or deriving it from `bossOwnedWin` on the last `RoomDataSO` instead of pure array length) before a second single-scene world is built.

### B95. `GameManager`'s enemy-count HUD total can overcount when a `RoomDataSO` spawn point has a null `enemyPrefab` — found 2026-08-26 (code review), not fixed

`GameManager.Start()`'s `maxSpawns` calculation sums `sp.spawnCount` across every `RoomDataSO.spawnPoints` entry, including entries whose `enemyPrefab` is null. `LevelBuilder.BuildSpawnPoints()` correctly skips null-prefab entries when building the *real* runtime spawn point list (logs a warning and `continue`s), so the enemy actually never spawns — but `GameManager`'s separate total-count pass reads the raw `RoomDataSO` data directly and doesn't apply the same filter, so the HUD's starting "enemies remaining" count can read higher than the number of enemies that will ever exist. Cosmetic only (the counter would just never reach exactly 0 by killing the last real enemy — though `RoomManager`'s own clear logic is unaffected, since it tracks real spawned/alive counts independently). Not currently reproducible in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` (no known null-prefab entries in its `RoomDataSO` assets at time of writing), but the two code paths should share one filtered source of truth rather than silently drifting.

### B96. `WildWestCityZoneDirector`'s doc comment claiming "zero other subscribers" to `RoomManager.OnRoomCleared` was stale — found 2026-08-26 (code review), fixed as a side effect of H4

Flagged by code review as inaccurate: the comment said `RoomManager.OnRoomActivated`/`OnRoomCleared` had "zero other subscribers project-wide," but `GameManager.HandleRoomCleared` was already subscribed to `OnRoomCleared` at the same time — the claim was wrong the moment `WildWestCityZoneDirector` was written. Resolved as a side effect of the H4 fix (2026-08-26, `docs/BACKLOG.md`/this session): `WildWestCityZoneDirector` no longer subscribes to `RoomManager.OnRoomCleared` at all (it now reacts to `GameManager.OnRoomClearScreenShown` instead, specifically to close the gate-opens-before-the-reward-screen exploit window), so `OnRoomCleared` genuinely has exactly one subscriber (`GameManager.HandleRoomCleared`) again, and the class doc comment was rewritten to describe the actual current subscription instead of asserting a subscriber count. No further action needed.

### B97. `RoomTrigger_Zone1`/`RoomTrigger_Zone2`/`RoomGate_Zone0`/`RoomGate_Zone1` transforms carry a stale `m_LocalEulerAnglesHint` — found 2026-08-26 (code review), not fixed

Minor Editor-only nit flagged by code review: these four `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` transforms were authored/rotated in a way that left `m_LocalEulerAnglesHint` out of sync with the transform's actual rotation (a purely cosmetic Inspector-gizmo value Unity uses to avoid gimbal-lock jitter when *displaying* Euler angles — it has no runtime/gameplay effect). Harmless today; worth a quick re-select-and-nudge (or a script that recomputes the hint) the next time any of these four objects is touched in the Inspector, so the Inspector's rotation fields don't show a confusing stale value.

### B98. `EditorBuildSettings.asset` missing-scenes issue — already tracked, `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` itself confirmed correctly registered — found 2026-08-26 (code review), no new entry needed

Code review flagged "the `EditorBuildSettings.asset` missing-scenes issue" as a loose end to confirm-or-log. Checked live: `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity` **is** registered in `ProjectSettings/EditorBuildSettings.asset` (`Assets/_Project/Scenes/CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity`, build index 11 per `manage_scene get_active`) — this scene itself has no missing-registration bug. The general class of `EditorBuildSettings` defect (other scenes' GUIDs not matching any real `.meta` file, e.g. the pre-existing `CulDeSac_Room1` corruption, and `LoadingScreen`/`TownSquare_Room1`/`TownSquare_BossHall` referencing not-yet-built scenes) is already tracked in the "Tooling gotcha" write-up earlier in this document (2026-08-24, B69 verification pass) — not duplicated here.

### B99. `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`'s 26 m `RoomGate`s only sealed the building corridor, not the full street width — player could walk around the outside of the entire building row and reach the boss zone without triggering any `RoomTrigger` — found 2026-08-26 (second `code-reviewer` fix pass), **FIXED 2026-08-26**

The M9 fix (B90's sibling pass, see the ADR-0004 §1 changelog) widened both `RoomGate`s to 26 m to seal flush with the flanking buildings' `BoxCollider`s (outer face at street-local X ±13…18.63, varies per building). That closes the corridor between the two building rows, but the street's `Ground` plane is wider still — true walkable edge at street-local X ±20 — and the player's circular arena clamp (B87, radius 23 around the ground centre) doesn't tuck in to the building line either. The result: a corridor 2–7 m wide between the outermost building collider and the ground's true edge, running the full length of the street, that a gate — open or closed — never touched. A player hugging that outer lane could walk from spawn straight past both closed gates into the boss zone.

**Fix:** two new permanent `BoxCollider`s, `StreetBoundary_West` and `StreetBoundary_East`, added as scene-root children of `[Zone Boundaries]` (`Building` layer, no renderer, matching the gates' "invisible wall" treatment — see ADR-0004's now-resolved open question 2). Deliberately plain colliders, not `RoomGate`/`NavMeshObstacle` instances — these never open, so the extra machinery buys nothing. Placement (as originally shipped): street-local X ∈ [−20, −17] (west) and [17, 20] (east), full `Ground` Z-span (−59.45…−11.95), height 4 m, world position/rotation computed from the scene's standard `[ENV - Static]`-relative street-local→world conversion (ADR-0004 §0). ~~Chosen to overlap every building's outer face by ≥0.5 m on the tightest case (`sheriffs_office`, outer face X 16.20) so there is zero gap anywhere along the flank, confirmed analytically against all 10 buildings' live `Collider` bounds (not the visual mesh bounds — collision matters here, not what's visible).~~ **This claim was wrong for two buildings — see the correction below.**

**Verified with a real physics flood-fill, not static inspection.** Built a 0.5 m grid over the entire `Ground` footprint and marked each cell walkable via `Physics.OverlapCapsule` at the player's actual `CharacterController` radius (0.3 m), `QueryTriggerInteraction.Ignore`. BFS from the spawn cell, three configurations:
- **Boundaries disabled, gates closed (pre-fix reproduction):** 5,814 of ~7,700 cells reachable, boss-zone area (street-local Z ≥ −36) **reachable** — reproduces the exploit exactly as described.
- **Boundaries enabled, gates closed (shipped state):** 1,523 cells reachable, confined to zone 0, boss-zone area **not reachable** — the fix closes the gap.
- **Positive control — boundaries enabled, gates disabled (simulating a cleared run):** 4,981 cells reachable, boss-zone area **reachable** — proves the flood-fill method itself correctly detects connectivity rather than under-reporting by construction, and that a legitimately cleared run still gets through.

The same three-configuration check was re-run live inside Play Mode after the runtime NavMesh bake (not just Edit Mode), console clean. This also confirms the ~66 m NavMesh wraparound route around the south end of the building rows, found during the first fix pass and at the time reasoned to be "outside the arena clamp so no known impact," is now **physically unreachable by the player** rather than merely outside a clamp radius — that earlier reasoning was correct about the clamp but incomplete about the actual bypass risk, which this fix closes directly.

**Correction of record (third `code-reviewer` fix pass, 2026-08-26): the "zero gap anywhere, confirmed analytically against all 10 buildings" claim above was backwards for two buildings.** Re-deriving each building's true street-local outer-face X by transforming its `BoxCollider`'s 8 corners into the street-local frame (not the collider's raw world-space `Bounds`, which is a larger axis-aligned box once a 45°-rotated collider is involved, and doesn't equal the true street-local extent) found `sheriffs_office`'s outer face at street-local X 16.2030 and `stables`' at X −16.7479 — both *short* of the walls' original inner face at |X| 17.002, leaving a 0.80 m gap (`sheriffs_office`) and a 0.25 m gap (`stables`) wide enough for the player's 0.3 m-radius capsule to stand in, just outside the zone-1 fight. Confirmed with a targeted `Physics.CheckCapsule` sweep of both gaps at the player's real capsule dimensions: every sampled point in both pockets came back unblocked pre-fix.

**Re-fix:** widened both walls' `BoxCollider.size.x` from 3 to 4 (half-width 1.5 m → 2 m) and shifted each wall's street-local X centre by 0.5 m toward the street centreline (west: −18.5 → −18.0; east: 18.5 → 18.0), which by construction holds the outer face fixed at the `Ground` edge (|X| 20.0, unchanged) while moving the inner face from |X| 17.0 to |X| 16.0. This overlaps `sheriffs_office` by 0.203 m and `stables` by 0.748 m — both now genuinely sealed, re-verified with the same `Physics.CheckCapsule` sweep at 0.3 m/1.0 m grid resolution over both former gap regions: 91/91 sample points blocked (`sheriffs_office` band) and 130/130 blocked (`stables` band), both in Edit Mode and re-confirmed live in Play Mode after the runtime NavMesh bake. A control sweep at street-local X = ±10 (well inside the |X| ≤ 13 play corridor) confirmed the widened walls don't block anything legitimate. Final wall extents: `StreetBoundary_West` street-local X [−20.00, −16.00], `StreetBoundary_East` X [16.00, 20.00], both still Z [−59.45, −11.95] (unchanged, matches `Ground` exactly).

### B100. Two of `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` zone 1's seven spawn points sat inside `RoomGate_Zone1`'s NavMesh carve volume while the gate is closed for the entire zone-1 fight — found 2026-08-26 (second `code-reviewer` fix pass), **FIXED 2026-08-26**

`RoomGate_Zone1`'s carving `NavMeshObstacle` (added in the M9 fix, Box shape, local size `(26, 4, 1)` centered on the gate at street-local Z −36.5) carves street-local Z ∈ [−37.0, −36.0] while the gate is closed — inflated to roughly [−37.5, −35.5] once the baked NavMesh agent radius (0.5 m) is applied, confirmed empirically. `RoomData_CulDeSac_WildWestCity_Zone1.asset`'s spawn points 5 and 6 (0-indexed; a GnomeGrunt at street-local (0.50, −37.0) and a WagonWheelRoller at (−4.75, −37.0)) both sat inside that inflated carve region — `NavMesh.SamplePosition` at their exact positions failed within a 0.3 m tolerance and only resolved ~0.5 m away with a wider tolerance, meaning any `NavMeshAgent` instantiated there while the gate is closed (which is always, for the whole zone-1 fight — `RoomGate_Zone1` only opens once zone 1 is already cleared) would warp on spawn or, in the worst case, fail to place cleanly, risking a `RoomManager` clear-condition deadlock if the enemy count never resolves.

Zone 1's third spawn point in that same Z band, spawn 4 (`(1.00, −38.0)`), was already right at the edge (only ~0.5 m clear of the carve, per the ADR-0004 §5 authoring) — confirmed still valid (`SamplePosition(0.3)` succeeds, actual offset ~0.07 m) and left unchanged.

**Fix:** re-validated all seven zone-1 spawns live via `NavMesh.SamplePosition`/`NavMesh.CalculatePath` with `RoomGate_Zone1` closed (its actual default state for the whole zone-1 encounter). Nudged spawns 5 and 6 south — well clear of the carve rather than just past its edge — to street-local (0.50, −40.5) and (5.50, −41.0) respectively (both remain inside zone 1's Z span, −44…−36, so the encounter's shape is preserved: they're still the northernmost / last-arriving pair the ADR-0004 §5 narrative describes). Both new positions verified: `NavMesh.SamplePosition(0.3)` succeeds with sub-decimeter offset, `NavMesh.CalculatePath` to a representative zone-1 point returns `PathComplete`, zero overlap against any `Building`-layer collider (1.2 m check radius), and ≥1.5 m pairwise clearance from all five other zone-1 spawns (minimum observed 2.02 m) — matching the ADR's own ≥1.5 m clearance rule. `RoomData_CulDeSac_WildWestCity_Zone1.asset` updated; re-verified end-to-end after the edit by reading the live `SerializedObject` off the actual asset (not hardcoded test values) and re-sampling all seven positions against the NavMesh with the gate closed — all seven pass.

**Correction of record (third `code-reviewer` fix pass, 2026-08-26): spawn 5 shipped with the wrong X value — this write-up's own documented target, (0.50, −40.5), was never what actually landed in the asset.** `RoomData_CulDeSac_WildWestCity_Zone1.asset`'s spawn 5 was found at world `(−30.759, 0, −26.516)`, which converts to street-local `(−3.00, −40.50)` — the Z matches this fix exactly, but the X is a 3.5 m transcription error (spawn 6, alongside it, correctly matched its documented `(5.50, −41.0)`). Re-derived independently rather than trusting either number: measured clearance to `pfb_env_stacked_crates_02` (closest prop) at both candidates minus the GnomeGrunt's `NavMeshAgent` radius (0.3 m) — the shipped `(−3.00, −40.50)` cleared the crate by only ~0.08 m (tightest clearance of any zone-1 spawn), while the documented `(0.50, −40.5)` cleared it by ~1.63 m, consistent with every other spawn's clearance in this encounter. Corrected `RoomData_CulDeSac_WildWestCity_Zone1.asset` spawn 5 to world `(−28.284, 0, −28.991)` (street-local `(0.50, −40.50)`, matching the original intent). Re-verified live in Play Mode with `RoomGate_Zone1` closed, reading straight off the live `SerializedObject`: all seven spawns pass `NavMesh.SamplePosition(0.3)` (spawn 5 offset 0.083 m, identical to five of the other six), zero `Building`-layer overlap within 1.2 m, `CalculatePath` to another zone-1 spawn returns `PathComplete`, and spawn 5's nearest neighbour is now 2.549 m away (was already ≥1.5 m at the old value too, so this dimension didn't change the earlier pass's clearance conclusion — only the crate clearance did).

### B101. `MAT_Ground.mat` — a shared material also used unedited by `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` and `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` — had been mutated in-place with `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`'s western-dirt texture/tiling/color — found 2026-08-26 (second `code-reviewer` fix pass), **FIXED 2026-08-26**

A prior pass building `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` assigned `Tex_WesternDirt_Ground.png` (8×8 tiling) and a white `_BaseColor`/`_Color` directly onto `Assets/_Project/Materials/MAT_Ground.mat`'s `_BaseMap`/`_MainTex`, rather than onto a scene-specific copy. `docs/ROADMAP.md` documents `CulDeSac_Room1_v2` and `CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` as "kept on disk as reference, not deleted, not edited" — this mutation silently changed their `Ground` appearance too (both scenes reference the same shared asset), and would have shown up as an unexplained, unintended diff for those two untouched scenes had it been committed.

**Fix:** reverted `MAT_Ground.mat` to its git-committed baseline (`_BaseMap`/`_MainTex` texture `{fileID: 0}`, `_Scale (1,1)`, `_BaseColor`/`_Color` `(0.45, 0.45, 0.45, 1)` — confirmed zero `git diff` against `HEAD` after the revert). Created `Assets/_Project/Materials/MAT_Ground_WildWestCity.mat` as a duplicate carrying the mutated (western-dirt) values, and reassigned `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`'s `Ground` renderer to it instead. Verified both reference scenes read the reverted shared material correctly by loading each (read-only, no save) and checking the live `Renderer.sharedMaterial` — both report `MAT_Ground`, `_BaseColor (0.45,0.45,0.45,1)`, no `_BaseMap` texture, matching their pre-mutation appearance; neither scene file shows any diff from this pass.

### B102. Originally filed as "`pfb_player`'s `PlayerController._animator` unassigned, spamming `UnassignedReferenceException` every frame" — **diagnosis was impossible and does not reproduce; corrected 2026-08-26 (fourth pass)**

**The original diagnosis was never possible.** `PlayerController._animator` (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/Player/PlayerController.cs:60`) is a plain private field with no `[SerializeField]` — it is resolved at runtime via `_animator = GetComponentInChildren<Animator>();` in both `Awake()` (line 74) and a `BoxSystem.OnModelChanged`-driven `RefreshAnimator()` (line 99), and every one of its ~10 call sites is null-guarded (`_animator?.` or `if (_animator != null)`). `UnassignedReferenceException` is a Unity-specific exception reserved for a serialized `UnityEngine.Object` reference left empty in the Inspector; a plain non-serialized C# field cannot produce it, and even a genuine null here would throw an ordinary `NullReferenceException` on direct (unguarded) access — which doesn't occur either, since every access is guarded. Live re-check confirmed `SerializedObject` iteration over both loaded `PlayerController` instances (prefab + scene clone) finds **zero** `ObjectReference`-typed properties at all besides `m_Script` — every serialized field on this class is a `float`/`bool`/`Vector3`/`LayerMask`.

**Re-investigation, this pass:** entered Play Mode in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` fresh (console cleared first), used `EditorApplication.Step()` bursts (per the project's established technique for this Editor session's frozen-frame issue) to advance ~400 real simulated frames / ~44s of idle time, then a further ~400 frames / ~215s while forcing `RoomManager.ActivateRoom(0)` via reflection, teleporting the player next to a live-spawned `pfb_enemy_gnome_grunt`, and letting a real fight play out to the player's actual death (`PlayerStats.IsDead == true` confirmed live) — exercising `PlayerController.Update()`, movement, jump-adjacent animator calls, `CombatController`'s hit/parry/death paths, and `OnPlayerDeath()`'s animator access. **Zero occurrences of `UnassignedReferenceException` (or any `Unassigned*` message) at any point** (`read_console` with `filter_text: "Unassigned"` returned 0 entries across the whole session); the only console errors seen were two benign `Assertion failed on expression: 'dt > 0'` lines, a known artifact of the `EditorApplication.Step()` polling technique itself (Unity's own frame-time assertion under near-zero delta stepping), unrelated to any project script.

Also audited every `UnityEngine.Object`-typed `[SerializeField]` reachable from the live `pfb_player` scene instance via `SerializedObject` (not assumed): `PlayerInput.m_UIInputModule`/`m_Camera` (Unity-internal, harmless), `PlayerStats.hitSparkPrefab`, `CombatController._hitSparkVFX`/`_parryRingVFX`/`_aerialSlamVfx`/`_parryFlashMaterial`/`_tumbleshotPrefab`, `WeaponHolder.defaultWeaponData`/`weaponPrefab`, and `ForgePresenter._imaginationVolume`/`_forgeRevealSfx`/`_upgradeRevealSfx` are all genuinely unassigned on this scene's `pfb_player` instance — but every single usage site in `CombatController.cs`, `WeaponHolder.cs`, and `ForgePresenter.cs` is null-guarded (confirmed by reading each call site), so none of them can throw either; they just silently no-op (missing VFX/flash/forge-audio polish), which is a legitimate but separate cosmetic gap, not this bug. Separately noted: `Boxhead.Player.WeaponEquipController` (which does carry the only genuine `[SerializeField] private Animator _animator;` in the whole `Assets/_Project/Scripts/` tree) is not attached to any GameObject currently loaded in the project (`Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll<WeaponEquipController>()` returns 0) — it appears to be dead/unused code, not a live source of anything.

**Conclusion: this bug does not currently reproduce, in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` or (by inspection — `PlayerController`/`pfb_player` are shared, unedited by any scene) any other scene.** Given the original report's own quoted exception message can't be produced by the code it names, the most likely explanation is a misattributed or stale console read during the prior pass's verification (e.g. leftover buffer content from an unrelated diagnostic session), not a real regression introduced by the WildWestCity/ADR-0004 work. No code or scene change was made this pass — read-only investigation only, Play Mode state confirmed to revert cleanly on Stop (player position reverted to its authored spawn position, `git status` shows no diff on any scene/script). Closing as not-reproducible; reopen with a fresh stack trace (via `read_console` `include_stacktrace: true`) if it's ever seen again.

### B103. `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`: defeating SpinCycle did not end the run — `SpinCycleAI.DefeatSequence()` used SCALED time for a sequence that must always reach `TriggerWin()` — found and **FIXED 2026-08-26**

Owner report: played `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` for real end-to-end (all three zones, real combat) and genuinely defeated SpinCycle, but the game never ended — no win screen, no state change, nothing.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct observation, not guessed:** `SpinCycleAI.DefeatSequence()` (the coroutine `HandleDeath()` starts on `EnemyStats.OnDeath`) plays a multi-step animation — stumble pause, wobble, particle burst, shrink-to-zero, then an `ImaginationRestore` volume lerp that calls `GameManager.Instance?.TriggerWin()` — using entirely **scaled** time: `_waitDefeatStumble` was a `WaitForSeconds`, and `WobbleRoutine`/`ShrinkAndFade`/`LerpImagination` all accumulated elapsed time via `Time.deltaTime`. If `Time.timeScale` is anything other than `1` at or after the moment the boss's HP hits zero — for *any* reason, from *any* system in the scene — every one of those waits stalls forever: the boss visually freezes mid-animation (or right at the top, before it even starts shrinking), and `TriggerWin()` is never reached. There is no timeout, no error, no recovery — the run is silently unwinnable from that point on.

**Reproduced live, twice, under controlled conditions:** with `Time.timeScale` forced to `0` immediately before the killing blow (simulating "something elsewhere left the game paused"), `stats.TakeDamage()` still correctly fired `OnDeath`/`HandleDeath`/started `DefeatSequence`, but ~800 stepped frames later the boss was still sitting at its pre-shrink scale, `_imaginationVolume.weight` still `0`, and `GameManager.State` still `Playing` — permanently, with no sign of ever resolving. The moment `Time.timeScale` was set back to `1`, the sequence immediately resumed and completed normally.

**Fix:** converted the entire defeat-animation timing chain to unscaled/real time — `_waitDefeatStumble` is now a `WaitForSecondsRealtime`, and `WobbleRoutine`/`ShrinkAndFade`/`LerpImagination` accumulate on `Time.unscaledDeltaTime` instead of `Time.deltaTime`. This exactly matches a pattern this same codebase already uses for the identical class of problem elsewhere (`ForgePanel.WaitForCutsceneThenClose`'s "keeps advancing once per Update regardless of Time.timeScale" comment; `GameManager._waitRoomClearShow`/`_cutsceneStartDelay` are both `WaitForSecondsRealtime` for the same reason) — not a new pattern for this codebase. `Assets/_Project/Scripts/Enemy/SpinCycleAI.cs` is shared by both `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` and `CulDeSac_BossArena` (the scene SpinCycle was originally built for); this is a pure robustness improvement with no behavioral change under normal conditions (`Time.timeScale == 1`, the overwhelming common case), so it cannot regress `CulDeSac_BossArena` — not touched, per task constraint.

**Verified fixed, twice, under the exact adverse condition that caused the bug:** with `Time.timeScale` forced to `0` again (both immediately before, and continuously through, the killing blow — the identical repro above), `GameManager.State` now reliably reaches `Won` (boss object destroyed, defeat sequence ran to completion) in real time — once in ~14.6s and once in ~22.0s of `Time.unscaledTime`, both while `Time.timeScale` stayed at `0` the entire time. Also re-verified once under fully natural conditions (no forced timeScale, real `RunStartUI`/`UpgradeScreen`/`ShopScreen` click-throughs, `Time.timeScale` naturally `1` throughout) with the same successful `Won` outcome.

**What specifically leaves `Time.timeScale` stuck in a real playthrough was not conclusively pinned down** — the mandatory progression path (`RunStartUI` character picker, `UpgradeScreen`, `ShopScreen`) was live-tested end-to-end this pass and is symmetric/correct (every `Show()` is matched by a `Hide()` that restores `Time.timeScale = 1`). A credible, code-confirmed (not live-reproduced) candidate was found instead — see B104. This fix makes the win condition unconditionally robust regardless of which upstream cause is actually responsible, so it resolves the reported symptom either way.

### B104. `WorkbenchProp`'s proximity-exit detection is gated behind the very `Time.timeScale == 0` state that entering proximity causes — found 2026-08-26 (same investigation as B103), **deprioritized by owner 2026-08-26 after the Zone 1 lengthening (B107)**

**Owner assessment (2026-08-26):** not a concern after the board was lengthened/Zone 1 de-cramped — closing this as a live risk based on real playtesting. Note for whoever picks this up later: the underlying code mechanism described below (the `Update()` guard that can strand `Time.timeScale` at 0) is unchanged by B107 — the two workbench positions themselves (`~(-35.5, 0, -30.9)` and `~(-18.0, 0, -27.2)`) were not moved during that pass, only the surrounding zone geometry was. If this ever resurfaces (in this scene or if `WorkbenchProp`/`ForgePanel` get reused elsewhere), the suggested fix shape at the end of this entry still applies.

`WorkbenchProp.Update()` auto-opens the Forge panel on proximity (`OnPlayerEntered` → `ForgePanel.Open()` → `Time.timeScale = 0f`), and separately fires `OnPlayerExited` → `ForgePanel.Close()` → `Time.timeScale = 1f` once the player leaves the 3 m interact radius. But the same `Update()` method starts with `if (Time.timeScale == 0f) return;` — a blanket guard, intended (per its own comment) to stop proximity events from double-firing while some *other* pause screen is up. Because that guard doesn't distinguish "paused by something else" from "paused by this exact workbench a moment ago," once the panel auto-opens, `WorkbenchProp` can never run its own exit check again — walking away does not close the panel or restore `Time.timeScale`, only an explicit UI click (Close, or a successful Forge/Upgrade) does. Two workbenches exist in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` (street-local-derived world positions ~`(-35.5, 0, -30.9)` and ~`(-18.0, 0, -27.2)` per `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_WildWestCity.asset`), both well within the zones the player fights through before reaching the boss — a player who brushes within 3 m of either mid-combat without immediately clicking Close would have the game pause with no self-recovery path.

Not fixed this pass: B103's fix already makes the reported win-condition bug unconditionally resolve regardless of this mechanism, and this specific proximity/pause interaction was not live-reproduced end-to-end (only confirmed by reading `WorkbenchProp.cs`/`ForgePanel.cs` and reasoning through the guard logic), so per the task's "confirm by observation, don't guess" standard for the *fix* (as opposed to a flagged finding) it's recorded here rather than changed. `WorkbenchProp.cs`/`ForgePanel.cs` are shared scripts (also used by `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`/`CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2`); `CulDeSac_BossArena` was not touched or investigated for a workbench (the legacy pure-boss-arena design makes one very unlikely to exist there, but not confirmed). Suggested fix shape for a follow-up pass: only suppress the *enter* branch while `Time.timeScale == 0f`, always evaluate the *exit* branch once `_playerInRange` is true, so the panel this same script opened can always be the one to close itself again.

### B105. `GameManager.TriggerWin()` and `HandlePlayerDeath()` share one `GameState` guard with no arbitration — a player death during the (now-bounded, post-B103) defeat-animation window can permanently block a real win — found 2026-08-26 (same investigation as B103), **not fixed, flagged**

Both `TriggerWin()` and `HandlePlayerDeath()` start with `if (State != GameState.Playing) return;`. `HandlePlayerDeath()` sets `State = Lost` synchronously the instant the player's HP hits zero; `TriggerWin()` is only reached several real seconds later, at the end of `SpinCycleAI.DefeatSequence()`'s animation. If the player also dies — from any cause — at any point during that window, `HandlePlayerDeath()` wins the race unconditionally, and the later `TriggerWin()` call silently no-ops even though the boss genuinely died. `HandleDeath()`'s own comment ("Destroy any in-flight projectiles so they cannot kill the player during the defeat sequence") shows the original author already knew of and partially defended against exactly this race — but the defense only covers the boss's own already-instantiated projectiles at the instant of death, not any other damage source, and not the rest of the (pre-B103, unbounded; post-B103, real-time-bounded but still multi-second) window.

Reproduced as a side effect of this investigation's own test methodology (leftover live zone-1 enemies, spawned via a real `RoomManager.ActivateRoom(1)` call and never cleaned up before force-advancing to zone 2, killed the idle player over several hundred stepped frames) rather than a scenario a real player would hit by cleanly defeating the boss — so this is a confirmed, real code-level race condition, not a confirmed match for the originally reported symptom. Not fixed this pass: arbitrating `Won` vs. `Lost` priority is a small state-machine judgment call (e.g. should a `TriggerWin()` already in flight always take priority over a same-window death?) better suited to a deliberate follow-up than a same-pass addition to B103's narrowly-scoped fix.

### B106. `GameManager._runEndScreen` read null after `TriggerWin()` in every post-B103-edit Play Mode test this pass, despite resolving correctly in the pre-edit baseline test — found 2026-08-26, **not fixed, unresolved, needs a manual owner check**

During B103's fix verification, the very first clean Play Mode test (before any script edit/recompile) showed the full chain working correctly end-to-end: `GameManager.Start()`'s `FindAnyObjectByType<RunEndScreen>` fallback populated `_runEndScreen`, and `TriggerWin()` → `_runEndScreen.Show()` correctly activated the win-screen panel and paused audio. After editing `SpinCycleAI.cs` (forcing an Editor script recompile/domain reload mid-session) and re-entering Play Mode, every subsequent test in this same Editor session — including one run under fully natural conditions with no forced `Time.timeScale` manipulation — showed `GameManager.State` correctly reaching `Won`, but `GameManager`'s own `_runEndScreen` field reading `null` at that point, meaning the win screen itself never appears even though the win state is genuinely reached. `FindAnyObjectByType<RunEndScreen>` called independently at the same moment still successfully finds the one, correctly-live `RunEndScreen` object in the scene (confirmed only one `GameManager` and one `RunEndScreen` instance exist, no duplicates) — so the object exists and is findable, but `GameManager.Start()`'s own fallback assignment to its own field did not stick (or was reset) in these later sessions.

**Not confirmed as a real code regression** — this session showed extensive, independently-observed Editor/MCP-bridge instability throughout (Play Mode ending silently between tool calls with no apparent trigger, an unrelated mid-session domain reload, `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_WildWestCity.asset` changing on disk outside of any edit made this pass, repeated "port not released yet after reload" console noise), consistent with this project's already-documented Play-Mode/MCP flakiness (`.claude/agent-memory/unity-gameplay-engineer/feedback_unity_mcp_play_mode_capture.md` and related memory). The B103 fix does not touch `GameManager.cs` or `RunEndScreen.cs` at all, so it cannot be the direct cause. Flagged rather than chased further or fixed speculatively — **recommend the owner do one real, manual Play Mode pass in the Editor (Play button, not MCP-driven) through to a real SpinCycle kill and visually confirm the run-end screen appears.** If it does appear normally, this was test-harness noise from the mid-session recompile and can be closed with no action. If it reproduces for real, it is a second, distinct bug from B103 (a `_runEndScreen` wiring fragility, consistent with the already-known "`GameManager`'s UI screen SerializeFields are null even in Room 1's own live instance, `FindAnyObjectByType` fallback is the only wiring mechanism" pattern noted in `.claude/agent-memory/unity-gameplay-engineer/project_wildwestcity_build.md`) and would need its own investigation.

### B107. Zone 1 widened 8 m → 20 m (owner-reported: workbenches nearly overlapping, no room for the fight) — fixed and re-verified 2026-08-26; two reusable test-methodology lessons found along the way

Owner played the whole scene and found zone 1 ("Ambush Alley") only 8 m deep — its two forge workbenches sat ~8 m apart, essentially on top of each other, because 8 m is nearly the entire depth of the zone. Owner direction: *"Buildings can move, do what it takes."* Full technical detail (exact deltas, tables, re-verification results) is in `docs/adr/0004-world1-single-continuous-scene.md` §0/§1, which now records this as the scene's **second major geometry revision**; summary here for fast recall.

**Fix, a uniform rigid translation, not a re-layout.** Identified which of the scene's 10 buildings, 34 street props, 2 wagons, `RoomGate_Zone1`, and `RoomTrigger_Zone2` sit "north" of the zone-1/zone-2 chokepoint (6 buildings, 18 props, both wagons, the gate, the trigger — 27 objects total) and translated all of them by the identical world delta `(+8.485, 0, +8.485)` (= street-local `+12` on Z, since this scene's `[ENV - Static]` sits at world yaw 45°). Everything south of the chokepoint (`stables`, `general_store`, `porchcabin`, `barber_shop`, `RoomGate_Zone0`, `RoomTrigger_Zone1`, their props) was left untouched — zone 0 is unaffected. `Ground` was lengthened from 47.5 m to 59.5 m (scale.z 4.75→5.95) to absorb the shift, extending only the north edge (south edge, where the player spawns, is bit-for-bit unchanged). `StreetBoundary_West`/`_East` (the B99 anti-flank-bypass walls) were resized to match. Because the whole north cluster moved by one identical vector, its internal relative geometry — and therefore the boss arena's derived 8.39 m fight floor, the doorway math, and the intro-camera dolly path, all painstakingly measured in this ADR's §2 — is preserved by construction; re-verified empirically anyway (below), not just trusted from the math.

**Result:** zone 1 depth 8 m → **20 m** (exceeds zone 0's 15.5 m, per the task's "at least matching zone 0, or more" bar). Workbench separation 8.0 m → **17.9 m**. Zone 1's 7-enemy roster (unchanged roster/order/narrative beats) re-spread across the new depth, minimum pairwise spawn clearance improved from ~2.2–2.9 m to **4.51 m**. Player's B87 arena clamp re-derived (not just translated, since the elongated rectangle changes the optimal single-circle balance) from world `(−25.244, −25.244)` r=23 to `(−24.218, −24.218)` r=**24.5**.

**Lesson 1 — a physics flood-fill grid that extends past the level's true collision mesh produces false-positive "reachable" cells through off-mesh void.** Re-running B99's gate-bypass flood-fill (0.5 m grid, `Physics.OverlapCapsule` at the player's real capsule radius, BFS from spawn) on this longer street initially showed the boss zone "reachable" even with the anti-bypass walls enabled and both gates closed — alarming, since that's exactly the shipped/safe configuration. Root cause: the grid's south-end margin (added to help detect wall placement, same technique as B99) extended slightly past `Ground`'s true south edge; `Physics.OverlapCapsule` returns zero hits at a position with genuinely nothing there (true void) exactly the same way it does at a position with clear floor, so the BFS found a "path" that actually required walking off the map — something a real gravity-bound `CharacterController` cannot do. Fix: added a downward raycast ground-support check as a second condition for "walkable," alongside the existing capsule-overlap check. Re-verified with the standard three-configuration control (bug-repro / shipped / positive-control) once fixed: shipped-state boss-zone reachable = false, as it should be. **Worth carrying forward to any future flood-fill test on this or any other outdoor scene: bound the grid to the level's actual collision footprint, or add ground-support verification — an unbounded or margin-padded grid can silently test claims about terrain that doesn't exist.**

**Lesson 2 — a `RoomGate`'s `NavMeshModifier(ignoreFromBuild)` footprint is permanently excluded from the baked NavMesh, independent of the gate's open/closed state, and this reads as a false "gap" on a NavMesh ring-sample near any gate.** While re-verifying the boss arena's 8.39 m open-floor requirement at its new (translated) location, a NavMesh-based ring-sample around the arena center showed only 107/128 points on-mesh even with the gate open and both wagons cleared — but a physics-based raycast sweep of the same region (matching the ADR's own original measurement method) found a clean 8.44 m of real clear floor, limited by the same prop (`pfb_env_water_trough_02`) the original derivation named. The gate's thin excluded strip plus NavMesh agent-radius erosion around it is a permanent characteristic of the `ignoreFromBuild` + carving-obstacle pattern (B2's fix), not something that heals when the gate opens — only the *carving obstacle's* hole heals; the modifier's own exclusion doesn't depend on it. Not a defect (`SpinCycleAI`'s attacks read raw `transform.position`/physics per the ADR's own existing caveat, not the NavMesh), but **worth remembering when judging "is there enough room" near any `RoomGate` on this street: trust a physics raycast sweep over a NavMesh ring-sample when the two disagree near a gate.**

No new geometry-dependent defects found this pass — the three previously-fixed bugs (gate-bypass B99, spawn/carve-overlap B100, camera clearance ADR-0001 §2.7) were all re-verified clean on the new layout via the same rigor used to find them originally (real physics/NavMesh queries, three-configuration controls where applicable), not just re-derived math. Full verification numbers in the ADR.

### B108. Fifth `code-reviewer` fix pass on `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`/ADR-0004: one project-wide blocker (leaked `Time.timeScale = 0`) plus one silent-failure hardening item, one narrower-than-its-gate `RoomTrigger` pair, and a stale weapon pickup — **all fixed and verified 2026-08-26**

**Blocker: `ProjectSettings/TimeManager.asset` had `m_TimeScale: 0`** — a project-wide setting, not scene-specific, almost certainly leaked from an earlier Play Mode session's live `Time.timeScale` write persisting into the asset. As shipped this would start the Editor/game frozen in every scene, not just this one. Reverted to `1` (confirmed via `git diff` against the last commit: this is the only line that had drifted in the file). Verified live post-fix: a fresh Play Mode session correctly showed `Time.timeScale` at `0` only during the legitimate `RunStartUI` picker freeze, returning to `1` on dismiss — not stuck.

**Silent-failure win/UI path hardened.** `GameManager.TriggerWin()`/`HandlePlayerDeath()`/`ShowRoomClearScreenDelayed()` called `_runEndScreen?.Show()`, `_gameOverUI?.Show(won:false)`, `_upgradeScreen?.Show()`, `_shopScreen?.Show()` — all null-conditional, so if any Inspector/fallback wiring for these four fields is ever null, the game's win/lose/reward moment would silently do nothing with zero console output (save/IP already committed by that point). Added a shared `GameManager.ShowScreenOrLogMissing<T>(ref T screen, Action<T> show, string screenName, string consequence)` helper: re-resolves a null field once via `FindAnyObjectByType<T>(Include)`, and if still null, `Debug.LogError`s a message naming the screen and why it matters (same tone as `RoomManager`'s spawn-failure logging and `WildWestCityZoneDirector`'s H5 gate-failure logging), instead of doing nothing. Applied at all four call sites. Verified live in Play Mode via reflection (`MakeGenericMethod` + `MethodInfo.Invoke`, since the helper's `ref` parameter can't be driven through a real field without a compiled call site): (1) nulled `GameManager`'s real `_shopScreen` field, invoked the helper — it re-resolved the real `ShopScreen` instance and called `Show()` (confirmed via `Time.timeScale` dropping to 0), zero error logged, then `Hide()` cleanly reverted; (2) invoked the helper with `PermitPulperBossAI` (a type with zero instances in this scene) as a safe stand-in for "genuinely absent" — confirmed the show callback never fired and the exact expected `[GameManager] ... missing — ...` error appeared in the console. Both branches proven correct without touching or risking any real scene object.

**`RoomTrigger_Zone1`/`RoomTrigger_Zone2` were `size.x = 22`, narrower than their paired `RoomGate_Zone0`/`RoomGate_Zone1` at `size.x = 26`.** Not reproducible as a bypass against the current building placement (the walkable corridor at both boundaries is narrower than even the 22 m trigger), but a `RoomTrigger_Zone2` bypass specifically would be severe and silent: zone 2 never activates, the boss never gets `SetActive(true)`, run permanently unwinnable, zero diagnostic output — and this scene's building layout has already moved twice in its history (B86 rotation, B107 north-cluster shift). Widened both triggers' `size.x` from 22 to 26 to match their paired gates exactly. Re-verified via `Physics.OverlapBox` at the widened box dimensions for both triggers: every new overlap is with static geometry the paired gate already tolerates at the same width (buildings, `Ground`, adjacent props, the gate itself) — no overlap with any other `RoomTrigger` or player-reactive trigger, and since `RoomTrigger.OnTriggerEnter` only reacts to the `Player` tag, the wider `isTrigger` box changes nothing about physical navigation or the NavMesh. Also re-verified a full Play Mode round-trip afterward: console clean, on-disk scene file confirms both triggers saved at `(26, 3, 3)`.

**Scattered weapon pickup nudged 1 m further from `RoomGate_Zone0`.** One `scatteredObjects` entry in `WeaponDropTableSO_CulDeSac_WildWestCity.asset` (street-local `(−6.50, −43.00)`, 1 m north of the gate's north face) sat inside a 1 m physics-overlap check against the gate's own `BoxCollider` — visually tight against the barricade. Moved to street-local `(−6.50, −42.00)` (world `(−34.295, 0, −25.102)`); re-verified via `Physics.OverlapSphere`(r=1m) that the new position no longer overlaps `RoomGate_Zone0`'s collider (only `Ground` and the harmless `RoomTrigger_Zone1` trigger volume remain in range). Hand-edited the asset's YAML directly (consistent with how these `WeaponDropTableSO`/`RoomDataSO` assets have been authored all along) and forced `AssetDatabase.ImportAsset(ForceUpdate)` + `Refresh()` to confirm Unity picked up the out-of-band edit before trusting it.

**Documentation sync.** `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s World 1 section still had the *original* (pre-B107) geometry numbers — ground 40×47.5 m, zone 1 `−44 → −36` (8 m), zone 2 `−36 → −11.95`, walls X ±17…20, arena clamp radius 23, boss-arena centre `(0.80, −30.90)` — none of which reflected the second geometry revision. Rewrote the status block, zone table, and boss-arena paragraph to the current shipped values (ground 40×59.5 m; zone 1 `−44 → −24.5`, 20 m; zone 2 `−24.5 → +0.05`, 24.55 m unchanged; walls X ±16…20 — one widening past the ±17…20 this task described, from the ADR's own third fix pass; clamp radius 24.5 on `(−24.22, −24.22)`; boss arena centre street-local `(0.80, −18.90)` / world `(−12.80, −13.93)`, re-measured minimum radius 8.44 m). Also corrected `docs/adr/0004-world1-single-continuous-scene.md`: (1) softened the "uniform translation leaves the arena's relative geometry mathematically unchanged" claim — `stacked_crates_02`, one of §2's limiting props, did **not** move with the rest of the shift-group (it happened to enlarge rather than shrink the arena, but the original wording incorrectly implied the empirical re-measurement was just a formality); (2) updated a leftover `StreetBoundary` Z-span sentence still reading the pre-B107 `(−59.45…−11.95)` to the current `(−59.45…+0.05)`; (3) reworded the gate-1/`bank` chokepoint sentence to name the actual margin (~0.10 m — `bank`'s span starts at −24.60, the chokepoint sits at −24.5) and say it's preserved by that 0.10 m of overlap, verified via physics flood-fill, rather than "preserved by construction," so a future building move doesn't assume the margin is structurally guaranteed; (4) updated the §1 "Exact placements" table's `RoomTrigger` sizes from `(22,3,3)` to `(26,3,3)` and added a fourth-fix-pass paragraph documenting this pass's widening; (5) filled in the previously-unstated boss-arena "new center" coordinate in the §1 re-verification bullet, derived from the documented shift delta and cross-checked live against `pfb_env_covered_wagon_01`'s actual post-shift position.

**Also fixed while in `GameManager.cs`:** the `"No enemies found at Start"` warning now only fires when no `RoomManager` is present (previously fired on every `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` run despite the boss-owned win path working correctly, reading as an alarming warning on the flagship scene's happy path) — verified live that it does not fire when `RoomManager.Instance` is non-null. The `OnUpgradePicked()` comment claiming "Close the screen and hand control back in place" was corrected — the code doesn't close anything; `UpgradeScreen.OnCardPicked` already calls `Hide()` before firing the event.

Full Play Mode round-trip (enter → verify → stop → re-save) completed cleanly: console clean of new errors/warnings, on-disk scene confirms all geometry changes, `TimeManager.asset` shows zero diff against the last commit. Not committed, per project rule — this is the fifth `code-reviewer`-driven fix pass on this scene, plus B107's owner-reported geometry pass, without a commit.

### B109. In-game minimap changed from square to circle in `pfb_hud_v4.prefab` — **DONE 2026-08-26** (`ui-ux-designer`)

Owner request: change the minimap from a square to a circle. Bundled with the B89 9x-duplication cleanup above, since fixing only one of nine stacked copies would have left the HUD inconsistent depending on which copy Unity's object enumeration happened to render.

**Masking approach:** standard `UnityEngine.UI.Mask` technique, matching the project's existing hand-drawn-cardboard HUD convention rather than inventing a new visual style. `MinimapPanel` (the 180×180 background `Image`, dark tint `rgba(0.1, 0.08, 0.06, 0.85)`, previously `m_Sprite: {fileID: 0}` i.e. a plain square) had its `Image.m_Sprite` set to `Assets/_Project/UI/Icons/circle_bg.png` (guid `aafea98acd52a4734b2c10a9921a8dc1`) — a 128×128 solid white circle on transparent background. **Correction of record (follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26): this was not "never wired up anywhere, prepared in advance" as originally claimed — it's already the project's standard round-button background, in active use by 17 other `Image` components in this same prefab (`BG_Attack`, `BG_Dodge`, `BG_Parry`, `BG_Jump`, `BG_Special_Back`, `BG_Joystick`, `CountdownOverlay`s).** Reusing an existing, already-battle-tested sprite is still the right call — just not for the reason originally stated. Added a `Mask` component (`m_ShowMaskGraphic: 1`, so the dark tint still shows as a thin rim) to the same `MinimapPanel` GameObject. `MinimapPanel`'s single child, `MinimapView` (the `RawImage` displaying `MinimapRT`, inset 5% on all sides), needed no changes — `Mask` clips all child `Graphic`s to the parent `Image`'s alpha shape automatically, regardless of the child's own rect shape.

**Geometry note (follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26): the result is a circular silhouette with a rounded-square map underneath, not a true uniform-rim circle.** `MinimapView`'s 5%-inset half-extent (81px) is smaller than the mask's own radius (90px), so the mask's circular edge only actually clips the four diagonal corners — the map content's own straight edges are what bound it everywhere else, tapering the dark rim from 9px (N/S/E/W) to 0px (diagonals). Reads as a circle at a glance in the verification screenshot, so likely fine as shipped — noted for the record, not fixed. A true uniform rim would need `MinimapView` stretched to fill 0–1 with the rim coming from a separate ring graphic instead of the inset.

**Blip verification.** `MinimapIndicator.cs` doesn't draw a UI overlay — it spawns a world-space disc (layer 20, `_altitudeY` above the scene) that the minimap camera itself photographs into `MinimapRT`, which the HUD's `RawImage` just displays. So the blips are baked into the same render-texture content the new circular mask clips, not a separate layer needing independent alignment — confirmed live in Play Mode in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` (screenshot): the rotated street map and all colored blips (green = player, orange/red = other tracked entities) render correctly clipped to the circle, with no square corners poking out and no misaligned/unclipped blip.

**Verified specifically in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`** (the flagship scene, B90's minimap-camera-tuning target): entered Play Mode, minimap rendered as a clean circle with correct rotated-street content and blips, console clean (only pre-existing benign warnings — see below), player position/scene state reverted correctly on Stop, no scene file changes (`git status` confirms only the prefab asset changed). Did not independently re-verify `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`/`CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` in Play Mode — this is a prefab-asset-level change with no scene-specific overrides on the affected objects (confirmed during the B89 investigation), so it should propagate automatically, but a quick look next time either scene is opened would be cheap confirmation.

**Unrelated finding, not fixed (out of scope):** entering Play Mode in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` for the verification above surfaced a pre-existing `NullReferenceException` in `WeaponHUDSlots.GetTierIcon()` (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/UI/WeaponHUDSlots.cs:307`, called from `RefreshSlot`→`RefreshAllSlots`→`OnEnable`) — unrelated to `WeaponSlots`' sibling `MinimapPanel`/`StyleSelectPanel`, not touched by this task. **Root cause correction (follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26): not an empty/unassigned slot** — `RefreshSlot` already guards `weapon != null` before this call, so the NRE is `weapon.Data == null` on a genuinely *non-empty* slot. `GetDisplayName` (`WeaponHUDSlots.cs:311`) has the identical latent hazard, not yet hit.

**Resolved (follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26):** the "Choose Your Hero" picker showing is correct, expected behavior, not a mystery — see B88's own correction-of-record above. `GameManager.ZoneStartScene[0]` was `"CulDeSac_WildWestCity"` before this task ever ran; B88's claim that it wasn't was simply wrong when written.

**Also noted, not touched:** `Assets/_Project/Scripts/Enemy/SpinCycleAI.cs` carries unrelated uncommitted changes (removing the pre-boss standoff cutscene call in favor of running `BossIntro()` directly) that predate this task and were not made by it — flagging so they aren't lost track of, since they're sitting alongside this task's changes in the same dirty working tree.

### B110. `pfb_MinimapCamera` in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` was a static wide shot showing void past the level's real edges — **FIXED 2026-08-26** (`unity-gameplay-engineer`)

Owner request: the minimap should only ever show real ground, never void/background beyond the level's edge, and should follow the player instead of being a fixed wide shot. New script `Assets/_Project/Scripts/Core/MinimapCameraFollow.cs` added to the scene instance of `pfb_MinimapCamera` (scene-only override, prefab asset untouched — confirmed zero `git diff` on `pfb_MinimapCamera.prefab`): `LateUpdate` tracks the player's X/Z every frame, clamped so the camera's square capture never extends past `Ground`'s real edge; Y/rotation/near/far/culling mask stay exactly as authored. Orthographic size shrunk `32 → 8` (was covering most of the street; now reads as a real "nearby surroundings" minimap).

**Two real bugs found and fixed during verification, both worth generalizing:**

1. **A world-axis-aligned camera clamped against a yawed ground rectangle needs a support-function margin projection, not a naive per-axis margin subtraction.** This street is rotated 45° in world space, but `pfb_MinimapCamera` never yaws (fixed top-down). The first implementation clamped the camera's centre to `(groundHalfExtent - orthoSize)` along the ground's own local axes — correct only if the captured square shared those axes. It doesn't, so the square's corners could still poke past the rotated rectangle's true edge even with the centre nominally `margin` inside it (caught immediately by the task's own "screenshot the minimap early" instruction — first render showed a black void wedge in one corner). Fix: for each ground-local axis unit vector `u`, the true required margin along that axis is `orthoSize * (|u.x| + |u.z|)` (a square's support-function projection) — exactly `√2×` the naive value at 45°, `1×` when the ground happens to be world-axis-aligned. Verified both analytically (2,000 random points, 0 corner-outside-rectangle failures) and empirically (8 real Play Mode screenshots: spawn, mid-street, boss arena, both flank walls, north edge, both diagonal corners — zero void in any).
2. **`MeshFilter.sharedMesh.bounds` is not a reliable way to read a static-batched object's true local mesh bounds at runtime.** `Ground` is marked Batching Static; Unity's static batching replaces a batched renderer's `MeshFilter.sharedMesh` at runtime with one "Combined Mesh" spanning the *entire* static batch (all batched objects' geometry, non-zero height, wrong bounds by a ~7× factor here) — silently, with no error, and identical in Edit Mode (where the original unbatched Plane mesh is still there) versus Play Mode (where it isn't). This produced an unclamped camera (a much bigger void wedge) on the second verification pass, caught the same way — direct camera screenshot, not trusted math. Fix: read `MeshCollider.sharedMesh.bounds` instead — colliders are never touched by static batching, so it reliably returns the real, un-combined Plane asset bounds (`Center (0,0,0)`, `Extents (5,0,5)`) in both Edit and Play mode. General lesson: never read `MeshFilter.sharedMesh` for a *static* object's true local geometry at runtime; use the collider's mesh, or an Edit-time-only bounds snapshot, instead.

**Unrelated, addressed mid-task per owner feedback:** enemy minimap blips (`MinimapIndicator._discRadius`, authored per-enemy on 6 prefabs under `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/Enemies/`, range 1.8–2.8) were tuned for the old `orthoSize=32` view; at the new `orthoSize=8` (4× more zoomed in) they read roughly 4× too large on screen. Scaled all 6 down by the same 4× factor (`1.8→0.45`, `2.2→0.55`, `2.8→0.70`), preserving the existing size hierarchy between regular grunts and the boss.
