Player's own blip (`pfb_player`, `_discRadius=0.8`) was left untouched — not part of the owner's request. Verified live: activated the scene's (normally-inactive-until-triggered) `pfb_enemy_spincycle` boss next to the player in Play Mode, confirmed its blip now reads clearly smaller than before, then restored its original inactive state/position (confirmed via live re-read after `Stop`, matches the authored scene exactly).

**Cross-scene sizing risk, flagged to the owner, explicitly accepted as-is (2026-08-26):** this blip resize is a shared-prefab edit (`_discRadius` on all 6 enemy prefabs), but the camera-zoom fix above is a `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`-only scene-instance override. `CulDeSac_Room1_v2`/`CulDeSac_AmbushAlley_v2` still use their original, wider `pfb_MinimapCamera` framing (no `MinimapCameraFollow`, `orthoSize` unchanged from the prefab default) — with the blip radius now tuned for an 8-unit ortho view, their blips will render noticeably smaller than intended relative to their own, wider camera framing. Owner's call: "It's fine, let's not worry about it." Not fixed — recorded here in case it's ever reconsidered.

**Also noted, not touched (pre-existing, from a concurrent B109 task, not this one):** `pfb_hud_v4.prefab` and `Assets/_Project/Scripts/Enemy/SpinCycleAI.cs` both carry large uncommitted diffs unrelated to this task (the B89/B109 HUD dedup+circular-mask work and an unrelated SpinCycle intro-cutscene change) — present in the working tree before this task started, left exactly as found.

### B111. `pfb_hud_v4.prefab`'s four remaining duplication spots flagged by B89's own scope-correction note — **FIXED 2026-08-26** (`unity-gameplay-engineer`)

Follow-up to B89's "Scope correction" paragraph, which found (but explicitly left unfixed) four more same-parent same-name sibling groups in this prefab: `HUD_Camera/HUD_3D/HUD_StyleIconFrame/Bust_Ninja` (×9), `Bust_Cowboy` (×9), `HUD_Canvas/ActionIcons/BG_Special_Back/CountdownOverlay` (×8), `HUD_Canvas/ForgePanel/Content/ButtonRow/Row` (×3). Investigated each independently rather than assuming they were all the same defect — they weren't.

**`Bust_Ninja`/`Bust_Cowboy` (×9 each) — genuine stacked duplicates, fixed.** All 9 copies of each are byte-identical below root (same `Transform` position/rotation/scale, same `SpriteRenderer` sprite/color/sorting) except `m_IsActive` — 8 of the 9 `Bust_Ninja` copies were `0`, one was `1` (baked runtime selection state, the same Editor-Play-Mode-state-leak pattern B89 already found on `StyleSelectPanel`'s button colors). **Also found, not named in the original scope-correction note: the parent `HUD_StyleIconFrame` GameObject itself carried 9 duplicate `StyleBustSwapper` components** (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/UI/StyleBustSwapper.cs`, a `[SerializeField] GameObject _bustNinja/_bustCowboy` pair-swapper driven by `CombatController.OnStyleChanged`), one per Bust pair, each with its own serialized `_bustNinja`/`_bustCowboy` reference — this had to be resolved as part of the fix, not left alone, since collapsing the Bust pairs down to one without also collapsing the components would have left 8 dangling references. Kept the pair + component that were already first in both file order and `HUD_StyleIconFrame`'s actual sibling order (`Bust_Ninja` fileID `8301172485438163675` / `Bust_Cowboy` fileID `8318842599714239654` / `StyleBustSwapper` fileID `4099510050605050182`) — this also happened to be the one carrying the baked "Ninja selected" active state and the one the kept component's serialized fields pointed to, so no rewiring was needed. Deleted the other 8 pairs (48 objects: GameObject+Transform+SpriteRenderer ×16) and the other 8 `StyleBustSwapper` components. **Also found, explicitly left unfixed (separate, unrelated defect, out of scope for this task):** ~~`HUD_StyleIconFrame` also carries 3 duplicate `HUD3DPositioner` components~~ **correction (follow-up `code-reviewer` pass, 2026-08-26): this understated the defect by two-thirds.** Three GameObjects each carry 3 identical `HUD3DPositioner` components — `HUD_ChargeMeterFrame`, `HUD_StyleIconFrame`, and `HUD_HealthbarFrame` — 9 duplicate components total, 6 redundant, not 3/2. `HUD3DPositioner` has no reference to the busts or anything this pass touched (confirmed by reading the script: it only serializes `_hudCamera`/`_viewportAnchor`/`_depth` and writes its own `transform.position`), and its steady-state cost is negligible (`Reposition()` only runs when `Screen.width`/`height` actually changes), so this remains a real but low-priority cleanup, not a performance blocker. Flagged here, corrected scope, for a future pass across all three frames.

**`CountdownOverlay` (×8) — genuine stacked duplicates, fixed, kept a non-first copy for a real reason.** All 8 copies are byte-identical below root (`RectTransform` anchoring, `Image` sprite/color/type/fillAmount, all `m_IsActive: 1`). `HUDController_V2._cooldownOverlay` (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/UI/HUDController_V2.cs`, drives the special-attack cooldown sweep) is a single serialized `Image` reference — and it pointed to the *last* copy in `BG_Special_Back`'s actual sibling order (fileID `7048608685226182779`), not the first. Kept that one (matching the live serialized reference, not hierarchy position), deleted the other 7.

**`ForgePanel/Content/ButtonRow/Row` (×3) — investigated and found NOT a defect; left untouched.** Unlike the other three groups, these three `Row` GameObjects are not duplicates of each other at all — they're three different rows in the forge panel's vertical layout that happen to share the generic name "Row": one holds `Forge 0/1/2`, one holds `CLOSE`, one holds `Upg 0/1/2`. Confirmed via `ForgePanel.cs`'s serialized fields (`_forgeSlot0-2Button`, `_upgradeSlot0-2Button`, `_closeButton`): each button lives inside a different one of the three "Row" GameObjects, so all three are load-bearing and deleting any of them would have deleted a third of the forge UI. This is exactly the false-positive risk the task's own same-parent-same-name scan method warns about — a generic layout-row name, not a copy-paste defect. (Also noted in passing: the `ForgePanel.cs`-attached `MonoBehaviour`'s `m_EditorClassIdentifier` still reads `Boxhead.UI.TestForgePanel`, a stale label from before the script was apparently renamed — cosmetic only, Unity resolves the component by the script's guid, not this string; not touched.)

**Cross-reference check (same discipline as B89):** grepped every `.unity`/`.prefab` in the project for this prefab's guid (`f22ab94ff407a49dfbe1ad64b8535cd6`) — only `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity`, `WeaponGripTest.unity`, and `ForgeLoop_Test.unity` reference it, and none of the three carries a `PrefabInstance` modification targeting any fileID this pass deleted, nor any scene-local override of `_bustNinja`/`_bustCowboy`/`_cooldownOverlay`. Nothing needed rewiring beyond the `StyleBustSwapper` collapse described above.

**Verification.** Full raw-YAML edit (not the interactive Prefab Stage, per B89's own caution) via a small Python parser built for this pass: parsed the prefab into its object graph, gathered each deleted subtree's complete fileID set (84 objects total: 16+16+7 GameObject/Transform/SpriteRenderer/CanvasRenderer/Image subtrees plus 8 `StyleBustSwapper` components), removed the corresponding `m_Children`/`m_Component` list entries from the three affected parents, and confirmed programmatically that the edited file is an exact line-for-line subsequence of the original (pure deletion, zero insertions/modifications — git's default diff display showed some misleading "insertions" from its own move-detection heuristic on the large deletions, not real changes). **Zero dangling internal `{fileID: ...}` references** anywhere in the resulting file (full scan, not sampling — checked every bare `{fileID: X}` with no accompanying `guid:` against the surviving object set). Post-edit `manage_prefabs get_info`/`get_hierarchy` confirmed exactly one `Bust_Ninja`, one `Bust_Cowboy`, one `CountdownOverlay`, one `StyleBustSwapper`, and `Row` still at 3 (childCount 232→209, exactly the 23 GameObjects removed); `StyleSelectPanel`/`MinimapPanel` (B89) untouched at 1 each.

Verified live in Play Mode in `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`: HUD rendered correctly with no new console errors/warnings (only the same pre-existing benign lines already documented elsewhere in this doc). Screenshot of the "Choose Your Hero" run-start screen showed the style-icon-frame bust rendering as a single clean Ninja bust (no z-fighting or doubling). Reflection-invoked `HUDController_V2.SetCooldownOverlay(0.4f)` and screenshotted the SPECIAL action icon: a single clean radial dark overlay, no ghosting. Reflection-invoked `ForgePanel.ToggleFromHUD()` and screenshotted the forge panel: all three rows (`Forge 0/1/2`, `Upg 0/1/2`, `CLOSE`) render simultaneously and correctly, confirming the "not a defect" call on that group was right. No scene file changes (`git status` shows only `pfb_hud_v4.prefab` modified).

**Likely root cause, found by the follow-up `code-reviewer` pass (2026-08-26): `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/UI/pfb_hud_v2.prefab`.** It shares fileIDs with `pfb_hud_v4` (confirming v4 was cloned from it) and carries the *same* duplication groups B89 and this entry just fixed — `StyleSelectPanel` ×9, `MinimapPanel` ×9, `CountdownOverlay` ×8, `Bust_Ninja`/`Bust_Cowboy` ×9 each — plus it's referenced by nothing else in the project (dead weight). Strongly suggests the duplication was baked in once, at whatever point `v2` was cloned into `v4`, rather than introduced by repeated copy-paste in `v4` itself. Not fixed/deleted here (this entry's task was v4 only) — worth a follow-up to either delete `pfb_hud_v2.prefab` outright (confirm nothing references it first) or, if kept as a reference, fix it too so it doesn't reseed this defect if ever cloned again.

### B112. First real on-device Pass A profiling capture, 2026-08-27 — draw calls and triangles over budget; SRP Batcher measured at zero; undocumented 30 FPS cap found

Full session in `docs/PERFORMANCE_PROFILING.md` ("Session 2026-08-27"). Captured via screenshots of the Unity Profiler window read directly by Claude — the MCP Unity server's `manage_profiler` tool calls (`get_frame_timing`, `get_counters`, `memory_take_snapshot`) returned idle local-Editor data even with the device selected as the Profiler's connection target; only the native Profiler window UI showed live device data. Worth recording as a tooling limitation for any future profiling pass attempted through that MCP path.

**Draw calls and triangles measured over budget.** One frame (11825/13122) within a full `CulDeSac_WildWestCity` playthrough (Zone 0 → Zone 1 → Zone 2, through defeating SpinCycle; exact zone/enemy-count at this specific frame not confirmed) showed **205 draw calls** against the **< 100** budget (TDD §3.2) and **356.7k triangles** against the **< 300k** budget (~19% over). SetPass Calls 38, Vertices 464.6k (both record-only, no budget). This is exactly the trigger condition the note above (line ~671, B69's Ambush Alley entry) named for reaching for `StaticBatchingUtility.Combine` on an env-prop-only subroot.

**But before applying that fix — the SRP Batcher itself measured at zero for this frame.** The Draw Calls Breakdown read `Standard: 204, SRP Batcher: 0, BRG: 0, Standard Instanced: 0, Null Geometry: 1`. `Mobile_RPAsset` has the SRP Batcher enabled, and both TDD §3.6 and the note at line ~671 assumed it "still applies" to these renderers — reducing per-draw CPU cost even if not the draw-call count itself. This frame shows it contributing **nothing**, not just failing to reduce draw-call count. That's a different and more direct question than "add static batching": something is preventing the SRP Batcher from engaging at all on these renderers right now (shader/material variant incompatibility is the most likely candidate). Recommend investigating that before scoping a `StaticBatchingUtility.Combine` pass — it may be the cheaper and more correct fix, or it may explain why draw calls are elevated independent of batching entirely.

**Texture memory measured well under budget** — see the B1 addendum above (41.2 MB / 52 textures vs. the 150 MB budget). Good news, not yet a full close-out (detailed per-zone sample per TDD §4.5 not yet taken).

**Undocumented 30 FPS cap found, contradicts TDD §3.1's stated 60 FPS target.** `GameManager.cs:101-102` sets `QualitySettings.vSyncCount = 0` and `Application.targetFrameRate = 30` explicitly. An earlier, quieter frame in the same session showed only ~4.34 ms of CPU work against the resulting 33 ms (30 FPS) frame budget — substantial headroom below even the 16.6 ms (60 FPS) budget, so the cap reads as a deliberate/leftover setting rather than a real performance ceiling. **Owner decision needed:** raise the cap to 60 and re-test, or correct TDD §3.1's documented target to 30. Not changed here.

**Not yet done this session:** Pass B (Xcode Instruments / non-development build), the S4 thermal run (minute 1 vs. minute 12), the §4.5 detailed per-zone texture sample, device model/class recorded (TDD Rule 2 — results may be a lower bound if the test device is newer than the 3–4-year-old target class). See `docs/PERFORMANCE_PROFILING.md` §7 for the full results table, including blanks still to fill in.

**Five pre-existing stale prefab-instance overrides, unrelated to this pass, found while cross-checking scenes:** `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity` (2) and `WeaponGripTest.unity` (3) each carry override entries targeting fileIDs that don't exist in `pfb_hud_v4.prefab` — confirmed these predate both this pass and B89 (already stale at commit `1c533178`). Same harmless class as the `CulDeSac_BossArena` case noted above (Unity drops them silently on next save); noted here for the record rather than fixed, consistent with not editing scenes outside a task's actual scope.

**Recommendation, not yet actioned:** this defect class has now required hand-archaeology twice on the same file, and the `ForgePanel/Row` case shows the same-parent-same-name detection heuristic throws false positives that need manual judgment every time. An EditMode validator asserting no unexpected same-parent same-name siblings under `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/UI/**` (with an allowlist for legitimate cases like `Row`), plus a check for duplicate single-instance components like `StyleBustSwapper`/`HUD3DPositioner`, would catch this class automatically instead of needing another full review pass next time it recurs.

### B113. World 2 scene-scaffold pass (ADR-0005) — `Backyard_Dojo.unity` created, `WildWestCityZoneDirector` generalized to `ZoneDirector`, `ZoneStartScene[1]` corrected

Scene-scaffold-only pass per ADR-0005: no zone geometry, props, encounters, or enemy placement authored — that is explicitly the next task (game-designer/puzzle-level-designer against `docs/v4/levels/World2/backyard-dojo/unity-blueprint.md`, once that doc is reconciled with ADR-0005 §Open Questions 1's three-zone structure).

**`WildWestCityZoneDirector` → `ZoneDirector` rename (ADR-0005 §5).** File and class renamed together (`git mv`, preserving the `.cs.meta` GUID) so `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity`'s existing component reference survived with **zero scene-file change** beyond the GameObject's own display name (`WildWestCityZoneDirector` → `ZoneDirector`, cosmetic). All four fields, every guard, and all five loud-failure logs kept behaviour-identical; only the `[ClassName]` prefix inside log strings and the doc comments were generalized off "WildWestCity"/"the street" language. Verified via reflection-driven Play Mode simulation (per `.claude/agent-memory/unity-gameplay-engineer/feedback_reflection_invoke_event_handlers.md` — more reliable in this environment than agent-driven physical movement, see that memory's citation of repeated prior failures): confirmed zone 0 auto-activates, `RoomManager.RoomCleared(0)`/`(1)` → `GameManager.OnRoomClearScreenShown` → `ZoneDirector.HandleZoneCleared` opens `RoomGate_Zone0`/`RoomGate_Zone1` in order, and entering zone 2 clears both covered wagons and activates `pfb_enemy_spincycle` — the exact ADR-0005 §Validation-1 sequence, console clean throughout. Confirmed Play Mode state (gate/boss/wagon states) reverted cleanly on Stop this session.

**`Backyard_Dojo.unity` built from scratch** (not cloned from `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity` and pruned — a fresh scene composition avoids carrying over any of World 1's street geometry or its 45° `[ENV - Static]` rotation by accident). Mirrors World 1's manager/player/HUD wiring pattern exactly: `pfb_AudioManager`/`pfb_AttackTelegraphService`/`pfb_ProgressionSystem`/`pfb_SaveSystem`/`pfb_MinimapCamera` at scene root (never nested — see `.claude/agent-memory/unity-gameplay-engineer/feedback_dontdestroyonload_root.md`), `pfb_GameManager`/`pfb_RoomManager`/a plain `LevelBuilder` GameObject under `[Managers]`, `pfb_player`/`pfb_Main_Camera`/`pfb_CM_FollowCam` under `[Player]`, and the five HUD/screen prefabs under `[HUD]`. `[ENV - Static]` authored at yaw 0 per ADR-0005 §6 item 1 (World 1's 45° rotation was named as the single largest avoidable authoring tax); `LevelBuilder._cameraYawDegrees = 0` and the camera rig's local rotation set to `(36, 0, 0)` (pitch only) to match. Ground is a single placeholder `Plane` primitive (40m × 70m) — not final geometry.

Three new `RoomDataSO` assets (`RoomData_Backyard_Dojo_Zone{0,1,2}`, `Assets/_Project/ScriptableObjects/Rooms/`) wired into the new `LevelBuilder`, matching the ADR-0005 zone table exactly (zone 2 `bossOwnedWin = true`, all `spawnPoints` empty pending the encounter-authoring pass). One new empty `WeaponDropTableSO_Backyard_Dojo` (`Assets/_Project/ScriptableObjects/Levels/`) — all arrays empty, the yard is meant to be hand-dressed per ADR-0005 §6 item 6. Two placeholder `RoomTrigger_Zone1`/`RoomTrigger_Zone2` volumes added at generic positions along a straight +Z walk axis (Z=20/Z=40) so the `RoomManager`/`RoomDataSO`/`RoomTrigger` zone-advance mechanism is exercisable end-to-end; verified via the same reflection-driven method as above (`RoomManager.OnRoomEntered(1)` → `(2)` correctly advances `_currentRoom` through all three RoomDataSO-driven zones, zone 2 correctly reports `bossOwnedWin = true`).

**Deliberately NOT added this pass, and why:** no `ZoneDirector` instance, no `RoomGate` objects, and no boss placement in `Backyard_Dojo.unity`. `ZoneDirector.Awake()` fails loudly (by design, H5) when `_boss` is unassigned — wiring an instance now would mean either a permanent false-error every scene load or a placeholder boss reference standing in for `GrasscutterAI`, which does not exist yet (ADR-0005 §Out of scope). A `RoomGate` that nothing will ever open (no `ZoneDirector` subscribed) is worse than no gate — it would not test the mechanism, only create a future trap. The level-design/boss-implementation pass must add `RoomGate_Zone0`/`RoomGate_Zone1` and a `ZoneDirector` instance together, once real geometry and the Grasscutter prefab exist. Likewise, no perimeter/boundary colliders yet (ADR-0005 §6 item 3's sealed-stockade-from-day-one rule applies to real geometry, not a placeholder plane) — the gate-bypass flood-fill validation (ADR-0005 §Validation 3) is gated on that pass, not this one.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way, not caused by this pass:** `pfb_Main_Camera.prefab`'s `UniversalAdditionalCameraData` carries one stale null entry in its camera stack (`m_Cameras` serialized field — not `m_CameraStack`, despite the public C# property name; found by dumping `SerializedObject` property names directly, since the property-path guess failed twice). Every fresh instantiation of this prefab logs `"1 camera overlay no longer exists and will be removed from the camera stack"` at Play Mode start. Harmless in itself (Unity self-heals the null entry, and `pfb_hud_v4`'s Canvas is Screen Space – Overlay, not camera-stacked, so nothing actually renders through this stack) — confirmed `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity`'s own live `pfb_Main_Camera` instance already carries a scene-local override clearing this same field to empty, i.e. World 1 silently already works around the same prefab-level defect. Applied the identical override (`m_Cameras.Array.size = 0`) to `Backyard_Dojo.unity`'s instance rather than fixing the shared prefab asset itself (out of scope for a scaffold pass; the prefab-level fix — and auditing whether any other scene instancing this prefab needs the same override — is a good small follow-up).

**`GameManager.ZoneIndexByScene` now has three scene-name keys resolving to zone index `1`** (`TownSquare_Room1`, `TownSquare_BossHall`, `Backyard_Dojo`) — not a compile conflict (different string keys, dictionary literal is fine) and not introduced by this pass' addition alone, since `TownSquare_Room1`/`TownSquare_BossHall` already shared value `1` before this change. `TownSquare_Room1`/`TownSquare_BossHall` remain **dead entries** — those scene names still don't exist on disk (separate, apparently-unbuilt content referenced by `PermitPulperBossAI`/`BossHallDoor`, unrelated to Backyard/Dojo) — left untouched per ADR-0005 §7's literal instruction (add `ZoneIndexByScene["Backyard_Dojo"] = 1`, replace `ZoneStartScene[1]`'s value only). Worth a follow-up decision on whether "TownSquare" is still live scope or should be retired from these dictionaries entirely.

**Not done, explicit next step:** hand off to game-designer/puzzle-level-designer for zone geometry, prop placement, spawn points, and the `ZoneDirector`/`RoomGate`/boss wiring against `docs/v4/levels/World2/backyard-dojo/unity-blueprint.md` (that doc still describes the superseded five-room random-draw structure per ADR-0005 §Open Questions 1 and should be corrected or marked historical before or during that pass).

### B114. Boss/large-agent NavMesh radius exceeds the single baked agent type (0.5)

**Decided 2026-08-31, revised same day (ADR-0005 §Open Questions 3):** boss `NavMeshAgent.radius` intentionally exceeds the single baked NavMesh agent type (0.5); the physical `Collider` carries the visual/physical bulk instead of a second baked agent type. First call was to cap new bosses at 0.5; revised to **match Grasscutter's radius to SpinCycle's (1)** for boss-to-boss consistency, since SpinCycle already ships at radius `1` with no reported pathing symptom.

**Current/target values:** `pfb_enemy_spincycle.prefab` (World 1 boss) `NavMeshAgent.Radius = 1`, unchanged. Grasscutter (World 2 boss, not yet implemented) should be built at radius `1` to match. WagonWheelRoller (`radius = 0.95`, per ADR-0004's original risk note) is a separate, non-boss case, not part of this decision — left as-is, no action taken or planned. Revisit any of these only if a real pathing symptom shows up (won't approach close enough, clips through geometry, `NavMesh.SamplePosition` failures near tight spaces).

### B115. World 2 Stage A build (`docs/v4/levels/World2/backyard-dojo/zone-layout-spec.md` §9) — geometry, dressing, RoomData_Zone0, weapon-drop-table partial

Built to the zone-layout-spec's own Stage A scope: ground resize, full stockade perimeter (BD-01 placeholder cubes, 45 modules), both `RoomGate`s and both `RoomTrigger`s re-derived from one authored value each, all three zones' geometry/dressing from the 8 existing dojo ENV prefabs, `RoomData_Backyard_Dojo_Zone0` fully populated (5 spawn points, `maxConcurrentEnemies = 4`), lighting retune (jade-white key, flat cool green-grey ambient), and NavMesh/camera-clearance/flood-fill validation run early per the spec's own instruction (all done in Edit Mode via `NavMeshSurface.BuildNavMesh()` + `Physics` queries — no Play Mode was used this pass, so the documented Play-Mode-scene-leak risk does not apply here).

**Real bug found and fixed: a 90° axis-swap in the stockade wall-tiling helper.** The first wall-building pass computed `yaw = Atan2(dir.x, dir.z)` (the standard "align local +Z with `dir`" convention) but assigned wall length to local **X** and thickness to local **Z** — the opposite axis. Every non-axis-aligned/rotated wall segment (the Z=17 and Z≈39 chokepoint returns, all 14 sides of zone 2's 16-gon ring) ended up thin-side-on to its intended direction, leaving multiple physical bypasses. **Caught by the gate-bypass flood-fill validation itself** (ADR-0005 §6.3), run before trusting the build: Config B (gates closed, stockade on) reached the full 59 m yard instead of stopping at zone 0, which should have been impossible. All 45 wall modules were deleted and rebuilt with the corrected convention (length on local Z, matching the yaw formula); the three-configuration flood-fill (Physics.OverlapCapsule/CheckCapsule at the player's real 0.3 m `CharacterController` radius, 0.5 m grid, BFS from spawn, plus a ground-support raycast per cell **restricted to a plausible ground-height band** — the first version of this check falsely treated the tops of gates/walls as valid footing) now passes all three configurations: (a) stockade disabled → reaches the full yard (positive control for a bypass), (b) stockade enabled, gates closed (as-shipped) → confined to zone 0 only (X −7..7, Z −2.5..16.5), (c) gates open → reaches all three zones (positive control for connectivity).

**Real bug found and fixed: two ENV-prefab-derived colliders placed in the wrong local space.** Every one of the 8 existing dojo ENV prefabs except the koi pond basin bakes a **270° local X rotation** at its root (a project-wide Meshy/Blender import convention — see the "compose, don't overwrite" memory), and two of them (`pfb_env_stepping_stone_tile`, `pfb_env_cherry_blossom_tree`) additionally bake a **100× uniform scale**. (1) A `CapsuleCollider` added as a **child** of the cherry-tree instance (for the trunk, since the source prefab ships with zero colliders at all) inherited that 100×/270° distortion and ended up at world `(0, 0, −152.5)` with `35×35×200` extents — fixed by re-parenting it as a **sibling** under the zone-2 dressing group instead, placed directly in world space. (2) The six zone-2 court-dressing props' `NavMeshObstacle.center`/`.size` (added for the future `ZoneDirector._clearOnBossZone` wiring, see below) were computed via a naive world-space bounds subtraction that ignored the 270° local rotation, swapping the obstacle's Y/Z axes — fixed via `transform.InverseTransformPoint` for center and an explicit Y/Z-swap for size. **Any future script that adds a collider/obstacle as a child of, or computes local-space geometry against, one of these 8 prefabs must account for this baked rotation/scale — never assume identity.**

**Real bug found and fixed: shed orientation and koi pond scale, both wrong at default placement.** `pfb_env_bld_shedwithcrate`'s default (unrotated) orientation runs its long axis east–west; the spec requires north–south along zone 1's west wall (load-bearing for the post-boss engawa-callback sightline in §4.5/§3.3) — fixed with an additional +90° world-Y rotation, confirmed via a top-down Scene View screenshot. `pfb_koi_pond_basin` at its default scale renders at roughly 1.9 m × 1.9 m against the spec's 6.0 m × 5.0 m basin footprint — rescaled to `(3.175, 1, 2.66)` to hit the target footprint exactly (basin has no baked rotation, so this scale is a plain per-axis fit, not distorted by the rotation issue above). **The shed's overall footprint is still roughly 2× the spec's assumed 3.0×8.0×4.2 m envelope** (measured ~7.4×9.5×6.4 m after the rotation fix) — left at native scale rather than non-uniformly rescaling a detailed building mesh; flagging for `art-director`/`asset-engineer` to confirm whether the spec's dimension table or the prefab is authoritative.

**Finding, not fixed: the engawa (BD-02 placeholder platform) does not auto-connect to the surrounding gravel on the baked NavMesh.** `NavMesh.CalculatePath` from a zone-1 interior point to a point on the engawa (Y=0.35) returns `PathPartial`, stopping at the platform's edge — the 0.35 m step-up never bridges. Two contributing facts: (1) the spec's own §3.2 assumes the project's baked `NavMeshAgent.climb = 0.75`, but the scene's actual `NavMeshSettings` (`Backyard_Dojo.unity`, and this is a project-wide default, not scene-local) is `agentClimb: 0.4` — still technically above the 0.35 m step, but close enough to the voxel-quantization margin (`cellSize: 0.16667`) that it did not connect in this build. (2) This may also be an artifact of the engawa being a single placeholder box with zero vertical air gap from the ground plane rather than the real tiled BD-02 art. **Needs `technical-director` input**: either correct the project's `agentClimb` to match the spec's assumed 0.75 (a project-wide NavMesh setting, not a per-scene fix), or add an explicit small ramp/`NavMeshLink` if 0.4 is intentional and load-bearing elsewhere. Not resolved unilaterally here since it's a cross-cutting NavMesh-bake setting, not a level-geometry fix.

**CORRECTED 2026-09-01 — the "no `WeaponObjectSO` wrapper" claim below was wrong.** `WeaponObjectSO` does not wrap the *equipped* weapon (Bo Staff/Katana/Shurikens) at all — it's a `WeaponData` subclass representing the **raw household item** that forges into one (`rawObjectName`, `rawObjectPrefab`, `forgeCost`, `baseEquippedData` pointing at the equipped `WeaponData`). All three already exist, just under their raw-item names: `WeaponObject_Broomstick.asset` → Bo Staff, `WeaponObject_CardboardTube.asset` → Katana, `WeaponObject_Ruler.asset` → Shurikens (12 total exist project-wide, one per weapon, `ScriptableObjects/Weapons/WeaponObjects/`). No grip-tuning is needed or possible on these — grip data lives on the equipped `WeaponData`/`WeaponData_*_nf` assets, already fixed project-wide earlier this session. Populated `WeaponDropTableSO_Backyard_Dojo.scatteredObjects` with 5 entries (Broomstick/Bo Staff + GardenTrowel/Quickdraw Blade in zone 0; GardenHose/Water Whip + CardboardTube/Katana + Ruler/Shurikens in zone 1), positioned near already-rotation-corrected cardboard-pile/workbench spots. **Note: including GardenHose/Water Whip here resolves ADR-0005 §Open Questions 5 by inclusion — owner can veto/remove if that's premature.** Positions are placed near known-good spots but not yet NavMesh-verified live.

~~Blocking gap found, not resolved: no `WeaponObjectSO` wrapper exists for Bo Staff, Katana, or Shurikens.~~ *(Original, incorrect entry, kept for the record — see correction above.)* The zone-layout-spec's §4.9 assumed these three were "already design-complete and in-project"; verified false for the *scattered-pickup* layer specifically. The equipped-weapon `WeaponData` assets, ability SOs, materials, and pickup-visual prefabs (`pfb_pickup_bostaff`/`katana`/`shuriken`) all exist, but no `WeaponObjectSO` asset wraps them (the type `LevelBuilder.SpawnWeaponEntry`/`WeaponDropTableSO.scatteredObjects` actually needs), and each pickup prefab's `WeaponPickup._weaponObject` is null. Creating one requires authoring `gripPositionOffset`/`gripRotationOffset`/`gripScale` per weapon — exactly the per-character grip-tuning work `.claude/agent-memory/unity-gameplay-engineer/feedback_weapon_grip_tuning_approach.md` flags as previously failed via agent automation and awaiting a different approach from the owner. **`WeaponDropTableSO_Backyard_Dojo.workbenchPositions` and `.cardboardPiles` are fully populated per spec §2.4/§3.6/§4.8; `.scatteredObjects` is left empty** rather than guessing at rarity/forgeCost/grip values. This blocks the visible weapon-pool variety §4.9 wanted but does not block the level's core loop (forge + cardboard piles alone still let a run start).

**Zone 2's `ValidateCameraClearance` diagnostic is far higher than the spec anticipated.** Measured (Edit-mode bake, gates closed, 1046 NavMesh verts total): zone 0 21.2% "behind" violations, zone 1 28.8%, **zone 2 57.7%** — against World 1's shipped 8% and the spec §4.6's own expectation of merely "a small cluster north of the tree trunk." The magnitude is explained, not mysterious: zone 2 is only 17 m deep, and it has *two* Building-layer occluders working against it from opposite ends — the shed's north face (affecting the zone1/2 seam) and the cherry-tree trunk (affecting most of the arena floor north of centre) — each casting an 8 m "diagnostic shadow" that, combined, covers most of a 17 m-deep zone. This is a diagnostic only (does not block Play Mode, per `LevelBuilder.ValidateCameraClearance`'s own design) and is the direct, acknowledged consequence of the arena-size-vs-central-obstruction tension the spec's own §4.6 already flagged as needing `technical-director` + owner sign-off (still open, not resolved by this pass). Recording the real measured percentage here since "small cluster" undersold it.

**Deliberately deferred to Stage B, not built this pass (per the spec's own §9 table):** the `ZoneDirector` instance and `_clearOnBossZone` wiring for the six zone-2 court-dressing props (they exist, grouped under `[ENV - Static]/Zone2_CourtDressing`, each already carrying the `NavMeshObstacle`+`NavMeshModifier` pair per §4.7 — only the actual director component/wiring is missing); the Skeptic beat (chair, Skeptic instance, `BackyardDojoBeats` trigger script — §3.5, not cited in Stage A's own scope list even though its craftsmanship *dressing* is); the 3-second Assembly Beat's activation-delay mechanism (§1.5/§9 open question 9); the zone-0 intro camera (§1.4, owner call); the Water Whip slot and the §4.6 arena-metric amendment (both owner/technical-director calls per the spec).

**Simplifications made for placeholder-primitive geometry, to be refined when BD-01/02/03/04/06/07 real art lands (Stage C):** the west-side "jog" connecting the Z=17 chokepoint's gap edge to zone 1's proper west wall was built as a single straight two-point connector rather than a multi-segment jog (the spec only gives an explicit dimensioned jog for the east side); the engawa is one contiguous placeholder box rather than 6 tiled BD-02 modules (identical walkable footprint, just not the art-triangle-budget-matching module count); per-zone floor materials (gravel vs. grass, §5.4) were not split out — `Ground` remains one plane on its single existing material, since splitting it into per-zone quads is explicitly spec'd as "a valid alternative... do not do it pre-emptively," and a real material split needs the texture-import-policy prerequisite (§5.6) enabled first anyway.

### B116. World 2 zone re-layout to ADR-0006's dimensions — zone 1 to 20.0 × 28.0 m, arena to r = 10.0 m — **DONE 2026-09-01**, `unity-gameplay-engineer`

**Completion note (2026-09-01):** Built and validated in Edit Mode via `execute_code` (no Play Mode). Full re-derivation, not translation, per this entry's instruction. All ADR-0006 §Validation blocking items passed: three-configuration flood-fill (0.75 m grid), `NavMesh.CalculatePath` `PathComplete` onto the engawa boards and every workbench/pickup/pile/boss-marker point, M1 re-computed by brute-force min-enclosing-circle over ADR-0006 §3.4's own indicative 7-spawn roster (binding window `{2,3,4,5}` = 8.707 m, budget ≤ 9.0), M2 by 1°-step `Building`-layer raycast sweep from the new arena centre (outer radius 10.000 m to the wall's inner face, radial band 9.65 m, obstruction 0.055%, traversal ratio 0.325 — all pass). `RoomData_Backyard_Dojo_Zone1`/`_Zone2` left with empty `spawnPoints` per owner instruction (AI doesn't exist); M1 is a paper/computed proof the space accommodates the roster, not a populated asset. Zone 0 and `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity` confirmed untouched via `git diff`.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during this pass, beyond the geometry itself:**
1. The Stage A cherry tree's **visual mesh was ~7 m from its own trunk `CapsuleCollider`** (trunk correctly at the old arena centre, visual mesh's `localPosition.z` was 54.51 not 47.5 — a plain placement bug, confirmed not a baked-rotation artifact via `Renderer.bounds.center == transform.position`). Fixed by re-centring both at the new centre `(0,0,55.0)`.
2. **Wall centreline vs. inner face:** placing a wall module's centreline exactly on the authored boundary (e.g. zone 2's apothem 10.0) puts the wall's *inner* face at `10.0 − half-thickness = 9.85`, not 10.0 — M2 clause 1 measures to the inner face. Fixed by offsetting every wall centreline outward by half the wall thickness (0.15 m) for both the zone 1 rectilinear boundary and the zone 2 16-gon.

**One ADR-0006 factual claim corrected:** the project's actual `NavMeshSettings.agentClimb` is **0.75**, not the 0.40 ADR-0006 §3.3 cites (verified via `ProjectSettings/NavMeshAreas.asset`, unchanged in git history since the first commit, and `NavMesh.GetSettingsByIndex(0)` at runtime). A 0.35 m engawa step is comfortably within 0.75 m climb; `PathComplete` onto the boards was achieved without the ramp doing load-bearing work. Built the south-step stepping-stone anyway (cheap, thematically justified per the ADR's own `kutsunugi-ishi` framing). ADR-0006 should be corrected at the source.

**One unexpected regression flagged, not chased (diagnostic-only per spec):** `ValidateCameraClearance`'s zone-0 "behind" violation rate rose from B115's measured 21.2% to **44.2%**, despite zone 0 itself being completely untouched. Most likely cause: the world −Z ("behind") ray from some zone-0 NavMesh vertices now grazes the enlarged/relocated zone-1 geometry at the 45°-tilted world angle (zone 1's west wall moved from X −7.0 to −8.0 design, and the shed moved from X −5.5 to −7.5 design, both further from origin in world space). Zone1 36.0%, zone2 45.2% (both also diagnostic-only, not chased per the ADR's own instruction).

**Not built (unchanged from B116's own scope, still correctly deferred):** `GrasscutterAI`, the dash-lane ground telegraph, BD-01-Long as finished art (walls tiled with standard ~4 m X-scaled BD-01 placeholders instead), the Skeptic beat, `ZoneDirector` instance wiring (though all six zone-2 court props now carry pre-fitted `NavMeshObstacle`+`NavMeshModifier` pairs, so Stage B wiring needs no further prep), B112's SRP-Batcher investigation.

Original task spec preserved below for reference.

**Why:** the owner playtested B115's built Stage A geometry on 2026-09-01 and reported **zone 1 and zone 2 both too small** (zone 0 not flagged). [ADR-0006](adr/0006-world2-zone-scale-and-arena-metric.md) is the decision record with the full derivation; this entry is the task. It also folds in three of B115's own open items, so read ADR-0006 §3.2, §3.3 and §6 before opening the scene.

**This is a re-layout, not a translation.** World 1's equivalent (B107) worked because a uniform rigid `(+8.485, 0, +8.485)` translation preserved the north cluster's internal geometry by construction. Here the zone envelopes themselves change, so interior positions must be **re-derived**. Do not scale coordinates.

**Read first, in this order:** ADR-0006 in full → `docs/v4/levels/World2/backyard-dojo/zone-layout-spec.md`'s superseded-sections banner → ADR-0005 §6 (the eight authoring lessons, all still binding).

**Target dimensions (ADR-0006 §1, §3, §4):**

| | Built now | Target |
|---|---|---|
| Zone 0 | X ±7.5 × Z −3.0…17.0 | **unchanged — do not touch** |
| Zone 1 | 16.5 × 22.0 m, Z 17.0…39.0 | **X −8.0…+12.0 (20.0 m) × Z 17.0…45.0 (28.0 m)**; 1A Z 17.0–32.0, 1B Z 32.0–45.0 |
| Zone 2 | 16-gon r = 8.5, centre `(0,0,47.5)` | **16-gon r = 10.0 (20.0 m across), centre `(0,0,55.0)`, Z 45.0…65.0**; side 3.978 m → BD-01 at native 4.0 m, **still 14 modules** |
| Zone 2 south opening | 6.76 m | **7.80 m** (vertex at due south, 2 segments omitted) |
| `ZONE2_BOUNDARY_WIDTH` | 8.8 | **9.8** — `RoomGate_Zone1` Z 39.0 → **45.0**, `RoomTrigger_Zone2` Z 41.0 → **47.0** |

**Zone 2 derived positions (ADR-0006 §1.4):** court dressing (4 makiwara + 2 lanterns) r 6.8 → **8.0**; boss dormancy `(0,0,54.5)` → **`(0,0,63.0)`**; intro walk target `(0,0,51.5)` → **`(0,0,59.5)`**; tall grass Z 53–56 → **60.5–65.0**; loot `(±5.0, 0, 41.5)` → **`(±5.0, 0, 47.5)`**; victory Shot A `(0,3.0,40.5)`→`(0,5.0,47.5)` becomes **`(0,3.0,46.5)`→`(0,5.5,55.0)`**; Shot B re-derive to the 9.2 m / ~17° framing against the chair's final position.

**Two hard constraints on zone 1's spawn re-derivation (ADR-0006 §3.4).** These bind and they are 0.5 m-sensitive:
1. **Spawn 5 (east-corridor gnome) no further north than Z ≈ 33.5 and no further east than X ≈ +9.0.** At `(9.5, 34.0)` the binding concurrency window measures 9.06 m and **fails** the ≤ 9 m budget. Compute it; do not eyeball it.
2. **If the Crane moves, the southern spawns move north with it** (spec §3.4's existing rule).

**Two of B115's own open items are resolved here, not deferred again:**
- **Shed:** stays at **native scale** (no rescale of a detailed building mesh) and **straddles the west stockade line** — X −11.2…−3.8, 4.2 m inside the yard, east face and doorway at X = −3.8, long axis north–south (B115's +90° world-Y correction stands), and the shed body replaces that 9.5 m run of wall. Zone 1B's X budget then closes exactly at 20.0 m: `4.2 + 3.0 engawa + 0.5 gap + 6.0 pond + 6.3 corridor`. **The built zone was over-subscribed by 4.4 m** and this is the fix.
- **Engawa NavMesh gap:** add a **low ramp / step wedge at the south step, with a collider, inside the bake**. Keep the boards at 0.35 m and 3.0 m wide. **Do not raise the project's `agentClimb` from 0.40** — that is a project-wide bake setting and would silently change enemy traversal in shipped `CulDeSac_WildWestCity`. Fallback only if the ramp fails: `agentClimb` → **0.50** (not 0.75), plus a full World 1 zone walkthrough re-verified.

**Prop counts do not grow with the area — this is half the fix (ADR-0006 §3.1).** Hold **movement-blocking** props at their current counts (6 makiwara, 2 lanterns, shed, pond, engawa, 1 rack). Collider-less floor dressing (stepping stones, leaf mounds, craftsmanship) may scale with lane length. Zone 1 total **≤ 66**; zone 2 total **28**. A count that grew is a regression even if it looks good.

**Pay for the growth (ADR-0006 §5.2):** author a **second BD-01 variant at 8.0 m nominal length (≤ 1,800 tris, same material)** for straight runs — zone 0's two 20 m walls and zone 1's two 28 m walls become 12 × 8.0 m modules instead of 24 × 4.0 m. Keep the 4.0 m module for the arena ring and short returns. Worst-case wall draw calls then land at ~35, **nine below the 44–45 in the built scene**. X-scale is permitted; **Y-scale is forbidden** (2.4 m wall height is load-bearing in the spec's Derived rule A).

**Coordinate-frame warning — verified in the scene file today.** `[ENV - Static]` is at **yaw 45°** with a **stale `m_LocalEulerAnglesHint (0,0,0)`**, so the Inspector may read 0 while the quaternion is 45°. Every dimension in ADR-0006 and the spec is an **`[ENV - Static]`-local** coordinate. Anything parented outside that root needs the 45° transform applied by hand: `RoomGate_*`, `RoomTrigger_*` (currently holding baked rotated world values — `RoomTrigger_Zone1 (13.612, 1, 13.258)`, `RoomTrigger_Zone2 (28.99, 1, 28.99)`, `RoomGate_Zone1 (27.577, 0, 27.577)`), all three `RoomDataSO` spawn arrays, `WeaponDropTableSO_Backyard_Dojo` positions, `PlayerController._arenaCenter`, and the victory-beat camera keys. ADR-0005 §6 item 1 records that this tax materialized exactly as warned the first time.

**Three prefab gotchas from B115, all still live (ADR-0006 §6):** (1) seven of the eight dojo ENV prefabs bake a **270° local X rotation**, and stepping-stone/cherry-tree additionally bake **100× scale** — add colliders and `NavMeshObstacle`s as **siblings in world space**, never as children; (2) `pfb_env_koi_pond_basin` is ~1.9 × 1.9 m native and needs re-fitting at its new position, not a copied scale; (3) the wall-tiling helper's `yaw = Atan2(dir.x, dir.z)` convention puts **length on local Z** — the arena ring is being rebuilt at a new radius, so the axis swap that left every rotated segment thin-side-on is live again.

**Acceptance — ADR-0006 §Validation 1–12 in full**, additive to ADR-0005 §Validation. The blocking ones: the **three-configuration flood-fill** (with B115's plausible-ground-height band; the shed now forms 9.5 m of the west boundary and must seal), **`PathComplete` onto the engawa boards**, **M1 re-run** with the worst window per zone reported, and **M2 re-measured by physics sweep** against visual mesh footprints. Measure and report the free-floor and prop-count audits — Fact 7a is what happens when a design number stands in for a prefab's real size.

**Not in scope:** `GrasscutterAI` (its Spin-Dash cap moves 8 → **6.5 m** per ADR-0006 §1.2, but the AI is not written), the dash-lane ground telegraph (`ui-ux-designer` / ADR-0003, but it is a **condition** of the 20 m arena — flag if it slips), BD-01-Long as finished art (`asset-engineer`, gated on the texture-import policy), the Skeptic beat and `BackyardDojoBeats`, the `ZoneDirector` instance wiring, and B112's SRP-Batcher investigation.

### B117. `GrasscutterAI.SpinDash`'s endpoint clamp validates the landing point, not the path to it — found 2026-09-01 (code review), not fixed, flagged

**Impact:** `ClampToNavMesh` (used by both `SpinDash` and `JumpBack`) calls `NavMesh.SamplePosition` on the desired endpoint only, then lerps the boss straight there over `Time.deltaTime`. `SamplePosition` proves the *landing point* is on the NavMesh; it says nothing about whether the straight line connecting the start and (possibly snapped-sideways) end points stays on the NavMesh the whole way. On an irregular boundary (Backyard Dojo's zone 2 16-gon, or any concave corner) this can let the dash's midpoint clip through a wall/obstacle segment that both endpoints avoid, even though every individual sampled point looks valid.

**Correct primitive:** `NavMesh.Raycast(startPos, desiredEnd, out NavMeshHit hit, NavMesh.AllAreas)` walks the actual segment and reports the first point the path leaves the mesh (`hit.hit == true`, `hit.position` = the boundary crossing) — this is what should gate the dash's committed distance, not just `SamplePosition` on the endpoint.

**Why not fixed now:** raised during the B1–B3 code-review fix pass (2026-09-01, `unity-gameplay-engineer`) alongside three confirmed-real blockers (missing `Enemy` tag, intro vcam framing world origin during the dormancy window, Cut-Grass Trail hazards riding along with the boss). Changing the dash's endpoint-resolution logic is a behavior change to a combat-critical movement path in the boss's signature Phase 2 attack — it deserves its own verification pass (does the shortened/redirected dash still read well against the telegraph, does it change `spinDashMaxDistance`'s effective range in the two arenas that use it) rather than being bundled into an unrelated quick-fix pass. `JumpBack` (used by `PetalToss`, shared with `SpinCycleAI`'s equivalent) has the same gap but is lower risk — its distance is much shorter (`jumpBackDistance = 3.5`) and it retreats away from the player rather than charging through the arena.

**Suggested fix shape:** in `ClampToNavMesh` (or a new sibling used specifically for the dash), after `NavMesh.SamplePosition` resolves a candidate endpoint, run `NavMesh.Raycast(fallback /* the pre-move position */, candidate, ...)` and if it hits, clamp the travel distance to `hit.position` (backed off slightly) instead of trusting the full sampled endpoint. Needs a flood-fill-style Play Mode or Edit Mode verification (a dash aimed directly at a known concave corner should stop at the wall, not clip through) before it ships, per this project's `feedback_physics_floodfill_reachability` testing convention.

### B118. World 2 boss — build the ground-plane dash-lane telegraph (ADR-0007). **Implemented and Play-Mode-verified 2026-09-01 — device/human playtest still required.** `unity-gameplay-engineer`

**Status update (2026-09-01):** Implemented per the checklist below. Both halves are done:

- **Part A (channel):** `TelegraphOverlayUnlit.shader`'s `_ZTest` promoted to a material property (default 8/Always — confirmed `mat_TelegraphOverlay.mat` on disk is byte-for-byte unmodified, still resolves to Always). New `mat_TelegraphLane.mat` at `_ZTest = 4` (LEqual). New `Core/AttackTelegraphLane.cs` (pooled, world-anchored, no billboard/tracking). `AttackTelegraphService` gained the `_laneSourceMaterial` field, a separate 2-slot lane pool (`_lanePool`/`_laneGeneration`), `AttackTelegraphHandle.PoolId`, and `ShowGroundLane(...)`. `pfb_AttackTelegraphService.prefab`'s `_laneSourceMaterial` wired to `mat_TelegraphLane.mat` (its `Backyard_Dojo.unity` instance has no override on that component, so it inherits correctly — confirmed via scene YAML).
- **Part B (`GrasscutterAI.SpinDash`):** aim block moved above `WindUp` — heading now commits to `_committedDashDir` at rev start, before the tell is shown, and is never re-aimed. `_dashContactRadius` (1.5, was a magic `1.5f`) now drives both the hit test and the lane's 3.0 m width from one source. `ComputeDashLaneChord`/`FarthestHitDistance` measure the full chord via two `RaycastNonAlloc` calls on the `Building` layer with a preallocated buffer (zero allocation), `_dashLaneFallbackLength = 20.0` backstops a missing wall. Lane raised same frame as the heading commit, held 1.4 s, explicitly `Hide()`'d on the `travelDist < 0.05` abort and — since `HandleDeath`'s `StopAllCoroutines()` discards `SpinDash` mid-flight without running its own cleanup — explicitly `Hide()`'d in `HandleDeath` too (an in-coroutine-only Dead check would never fire in that path; noted as a real gotcha caught during implementation, not from the ADR text). `spinDashRevDuration` floor-clamped to 0.75 s in both `OnValidate` (Editor) and `Awake` (build-safe backstop); default 0.9 s is unaffected.
- **Confirmed a factual gap in the ADR's own reasoning, not a defect:** `CherryTree_TrunkCollider` in `Backyard_Dojo.unity` **is** on the `Building` layer (layer 8), same as every `BD01_WallModule` — contrary to the ADR's "presumably isn't" aside. Doesn't matter: the farthest-hit-per-direction approach is correct regardless, since the wall is always farther from the boss than the central trunk. Recorded so nobody re-derives this from scratch later.
- **Play-Mode-verified (reflection-driven, this session, `Backyard_Dojo.unity`):** heading is bit-identical between rev-start capture and dash-execution readout even after moving the (test-proxy) player 140° off the original bearing during the wind-up — the commit-timing fix holds. `ShowGroundLane`/`Hide` pool separation confirmed directly: 3 rapid-fire lane calls correctly round-robin-evict across the 2-slot pool (with generation bump), and 9 rapid-fire billboard calls (exceeding the 8-slot billboard pool) left both lane slots untouched. Raised lane geometry sanity-checked (width scale = 3.0 exactly, length ≈ 17.7 m — a plausible full chord for the r = 10 m arena). Zero compile errors/warnings introduced; `git diff --stat` on both `Backyard_Dojo.unity` and `CulDeSac_WildWestCity.unity` confirmed clean (no Play Mode scene-state leakage) before and after.
- **Not verified — needs a human/device pass, not something this session could do:** §Validation 1 (on-device, south-rim-vs-north-rim readability — the actual grant condition ADR-0006 §Validation 10 requires), §5 (the escape window "holds in play" from a real player, not a scripted one), §8 (greyscale/colour-blind pass, and visual distinction from Cut-Grass Trail quads), §6's allocation number in a clean isolated scene (the live scene's Profiler reading was too noisy from unrelated systems to attribute cleanly — zero-allocation is true by construction here: `RaycastNonAlloc` into a preallocated buffer, no per-dash `new`/`Instantiate`, but that's a code-review-level claim, not a measured one). B117 (endpoint-only NavMesh clamp) remains separately flagged, unchanged, exactly as scoped.

Original checklist preserved below for reference.

**Priority: P1.** B116 has already built the arena at r = 10.0 m. ADR-0006 §1.3 granted that size **on** a ground-plane lane telegraph existing, and it does not. `GrasscutterAI.SpinDash` telegraphs with the ADR-0003 overhead billboard instead (`WindUp(Color.red, AttackTelegraphKind.MeleeUnparryable, _waitSpinDashRev)` → `AttackTelegraphService.Show(transform, …)`, body-anchored 2.6 m above the boss). Found by `code-reviewer` 2026-09-01; routed to `technical-director`, decided as **ADR-0007** — build the telegraph, do not take §1.3's shrink-the-arena fallback (that fallback has no legal landing site: M2.2 needs `R ≥ 8.85 m`, a body tell readable rim-to-rim needs `R ≤ 7.65 m`).

**Read `docs/adr/0007-ground-plane-lane-telegraph.md` in full before starting.** Everything below is a working checklist, not a substitute for it — in particular §2's rationale for depth-testing and §4's escape arithmetic are the two things most likely to be "simplified" into a defect.

**Part A — the channel (`Assets/_Project/Scripts/Core/`). This is the reusable half; do not put any of it in `GrasscutterAI`.**

1. `Shaders/TelegraphOverlayUnlit.shader`: promote the hard-coded `ZTest Always` to a material property — `[Enum(UnityEngine.Rendering.CompareFunction)] _ZTest ("ZTest", Float) = 8` in `Properties`, `ZTest [_ZTest]` in the pass. **Default 8 = `Always`, so `mat_TelegraphOverlay.mat` and the shipped billboard path are behaviourally unchanged** — verify that (ADR-0007 §Validation 10). If you would rather not touch a shipped shader at all, a separate 40-line shader file is an accepted alternative; say which you chose.
2. New `Assets/_Project/Materials/mat_TelegraphLane.mat` — same shader, `_ZTest = 4` (`LEqual`).
3. New `Core/AttackTelegraphLane.cs`, pooled component. One **static shared** quad mesh built once: XZ plane, `x ∈ [−0.5, 0.5]`, `z ∈ [0, 1]`, normal +Y. Copy `AttackTelegraphIndicator`'s `EnsureSharedMeshes` pattern **including** its `#if UNITY_EDITOR` `playModeStateChanged` → `DestroyImmediate` cleanup (without it the native mesh leaks for the rest of the Editor session). No billboard, no target tracking — `Update` is a countdown and nothing else.
4. `Core/AttackTelegraphService.cs`:
   - `[SerializeField] private Material _laneSourceMaterial;` — derive one tinted shared instance in `CreateSharedMaterials` using the existing `UnparryableColor`, destroy it in `OnDestroy`. **Reference the material asset through the Inspector; never `Shader.Find` (B32 — the shader gets stripped from real builds while working fine in the Editor).** Null must log an error naming the asset path, as `_overlaySourceMaterial` already does.
   - A **second pool of 2** (`_lanePool`, `_laneGeneration`), warmed in `Awake` beside the existing one. **Not the existing 8-slot pool** — `FindSlot` evicts at its round-robin cursor when full, so a 1.4 s boss lane sharing it is recyclable by grunt wind-ups.
   - `AttackTelegraphHandle` gains `internal readonly byte PoolId` (0 = billboard, 1 = lane) so `Hide` routes correctly. Internal-only construction, so this is source-compatible.
   - New entry point: `public static AttackTelegraphHandle ShowGroundLane(Vector3 start, Vector3 direction, float length, float width, AttackTelegraphKind kind, float duration, float groundY = 0f)`. Normalize `direction` on XZ inside the service; a degenerate direction returns `None` and logs once. Transform: position `start + Vector3.up * (groundY + 0.02f)`, rotation `Quaternion.LookRotation(direction, Vector3.up)`, scale `(width, 1, length)`.
   - **`AttackTelegraphKind` does not gain a member.** Geometry is chosen by which method you call; the enum keeps meaning parryability only. Call with `AttackTelegraphKind.AreaUnparryable`, which already maps to `SoundEvent.TelegraphAreaUnparryable` in `MapAudioCue`. Leave all twelve existing `Show` call sites untouched.
5. Assign `mat_TelegraphLane.mat` on `Assets/_Project/Prefabs/Core/pfb_AttackTelegraphService.prefab` (already instanced in `Backyard_Dojo.unity` — verified, no scene wiring needed).

**Part B — `GrasscutterAI.SpinDash` (`Enemy/GrasscutterAI.cs:746-816`).**

6. **Move the aim block (`:754-777`) above the `WindUp` call.** The heading must be committed at rev start and must not re-aim. This is the load-bearing change: today the dash re-aims to `_player.position` after the 0.9 s rev, so at 13 m/s against the player's 5 m/s it is not dodgeable by position at all, and the `:743` comment *"dodge perpendicular to the lane"* is currently false.
7. Promote the magic `1.5f` in `IsPlayerWithinRange(1.5f)` (`:800`) to `[SerializeField] private float _dashContactRadius = 1.5f`, and **derive both the hit test and the lane width from it** (`width = 2 × _dashContactRadius` = 3.0 m). A drawn band narrower than the hitbox is a fairness lie that costs the player health.
8. Measure the **full chord** — wall inner face to wall inner face, not the 6.5 m of travel. Two `Physics.RaycastNonAlloc` calls into a preallocated buffer from `startPos + Vector3.up * 0.5f` along `±dir`, `Building` layer, capped 24 m, taking the **farthest** hit each way (farthest, not first — that is what makes the central tree trunk irrelevant without a name or tag hack, since the wall is always beyond it). No hit on a side → fall back to half of a serialized `_dashLaneFallbackLength = 20.0f` there and log once; a missing wall must not collapse the lane to zero length.
   - **Why full-chord and not the travel segment:** a 6.5 m segment starting at a north-rim boss is entirely off-frame for a south-rim player — the exact failure the indicator exists to fix. A full chord always has its near end inside the frustum. The lane therefore over-states travel distance and never under-states width or heading; over-warning is fair, under-warning is not.
9. Raise the lane in the same frame as the heading commit, for `spinDashRevDuration + spinDashMaxDistance / spinDashSpeed` = **1.4 s** (held through the travel, so the band stays visible while the player dodges out of it). `Hide(handle)` on death, on stagger, and on the existing `travelDist < 0.05` abort.
10. **Keep the overhead billboard.** This is additive, per ADR-0003 decision 4: the billboard says *an unparryable attack is coming, from that enemy*; the lane says *and it goes here*.
11. **`spinDashRevDuration` has a hard floor of 0.75 s** while the lane is the fairness mechanism (0.38 s to walk clear + ~0.35 s recognition). 0.9 s stands. Do not reduce it, and do not widen the band without lengthening the rev.

**Acceptance — ADR-0007 §Validation 1–10 in full.** The blocking ones: **ADR-0006 §Validation 10 on device** (player at the south rim, boss at the north rim, 4.7 m off-frame — this is the condition the arena is granted on and nothing else substitutes for it); **the heading is honest** (committed direction at rev start vs actual travel vector — the NavMesh clamp may only shorten along that heading, never rotate it); **the band matches the hitbox** (hit at 1.4 m off-centreline, not hit at 1.6 m); **both escape routes work in play** from a standing start on the centreline; **zero per-dash allocation** over six dashes; and **`mat_TelegraphOverlay.mat` unchanged in behaviour** — the billboard still draws through walls.

**Not in scope:** the lane's visual treatment, including whether the committed travel segment is distinguished from the rest of the chord (`ui-ux-designer`); screen-edge indicators for the still-off-frame boss body (ADR-0003 Alternative 4 — the lane covers the attack, not the opponent); retrofitting `ShowGroundLane` to `SpinCycleAI.SpinCharge` or `WagonWheelRollerAI` (World 1 is shipped and playtested — separate decision, separate play pass); the orthogonal geometry/class refactor of `AttackTelegraphKind` (ADR-0007 §1 records the condition under which it should be paid); authoring `SoundData` clips for any `SoundEvent.Telegraph*` (none exist for any kind yet — pre-existing gap).

**Coupling to note before you start: B117 is on the same method.** Its `NavMesh.Raycast`-instead-of-`SamplePosition` path clamp and item 6's heading commit both change `SpinDash`'s movement resolution, and B117's own entry flags "does the shortened/redirected dash still read well against the telegraph" as its open question. They are separable — a shortened dash cannot make a full-chord lane a lie — but if you take both, take the telegraph first so B117 has something to read against.
